# Prototype verification log

This log records each dated verification pass over the throwaway guest workflow and operator concept prototype in [prototypes/guest-flow](../prototypes/guest-flow/README.md): the automated checks that ran, the browser paths exercised, the widths inspected, and what the independent reviewer found. Entries are newest first and are written by the verification pass after each slice lands, so a reader can see what was proven, by which method, and what was not (browser emulation is not a physical-device test, and none of these passes establish demand, price acceptance, or migration feasibility). Per-document change history is in git; ownership and review rules are in [the build execution and agent plan](v2/10-build-execution-and-agent-plan.md).

## 2026-11-09

### External payment and reconciliation (W3-G2.10), built November 9, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-g7e2w1`, from `main` at `2491337`, migrations `0031_payment_reconciliation.sql` and `0032_payments_check_index.sql` (renumbered at merge to the next free numbers if needed), under the owner's code-only pull-forward ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-09-w3-g210-external-payment-and-reconciliation-owner-decision-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Synthetic data, the fake provider, and the Stripe adapter's recorded-shape fixture only: no provider call, no binding, no deploy.

- **Built.** External settlements (cash, check, other; payment or refund; reason and key) with refund limits and fee reversal; fee assessment once per closed booking; draft fee invoices with the carry forward and the annual true-up; dispute events through `verifyWebhook` and the inbox, with a staff list; hourly provider readings of pending payments past their hold that never change a payment (D1, proposed); the month's reconciliation with its control total; a reconciliation sweep in fair turns; five staff routes.
- **Acceptance, against the goal's list** (missing, duplicate, changed, and mismatched settlement tests): `packages/domain-booking/src/reconciliation.integration.test.ts` covers a replayed key, a reused key, a refund too large and one that would pass the booking's total, records that never change or go, staff only, other tenants hidden; assessment once, at the close point, never for an open booking; thirteen invoices with a negative month carried forward, the true-up, and a figure that disagrees with the ledger refused; dispute events opened, moved, mismatched, unmatched, closed, and final; a pending payment read as pending, another amount, missing, and paid without its event, raised once each and never changed until its verified event; the month's report matching the ledger; and the sweep. `apps/api-worker/test-integration/reconciliation-staff.integration.test.ts` covers the routes' roles, idempotency, refusals, and a dispute delivered through the real webhook route. The Stripe and fake adapters' unit tests cover the dispute mapping and its refusals.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): the new suites pass, and `pnpm check` passed. Existing tests changed: the privilege catalog adds the new tables and update columns; the provider-neutral event now carries `dispute` (null outside dispute events); the sweep log tests mock the new sweep; the role, route, and export lists add the new permissions, routes, and tables.
- **Still open.** The coordinator defaults D1 to D6 await the owner. Dispute events from live Stripe are unobserved. A lost dispute does not reverse the fee. Credit redemption at checkout and trip cards are not part of the fee base yet. Invoices are drafts with no billing tool. The console has no screens yet for external money, disputes, readings, the report, or invoices; the API is in place.

## 2026-11-08

### Demo guest app journeys (W1N-JOURNEYS), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-bqlorn`, from `main` at `d2d68be`, under the owner's go of 22:16 UTC ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-demo-guest-app-pulled-forward-into-the-demo-owner-decision)). No migration, no API, contract, or guest site change.

- **Built.** In `apps/guest-native`: booking a trip (party or charter guests, extras, a price, the booker, the policy, a hold, the demo's test payment, and the outcome), "Your bookings" for sessions opened on the phone in the last hour, opening a booking by pasting the email link, and the booking's page with each named person's waiver status. The calls, headers, bodies, and words follow the guest web app. Booking sessions keep the web app's three fields in the platform's secure storage through expo-secure-store; checkout and payment secrets stay in memory. New packages: expo-crypto and expo-secure-store (Expo SDK 58 set) and the workspace design system's format module.
- **Checks.** Unit tests in Node, 47 in all: quote and checkout bodies parsed by the API's own schemas, the web app's routes, no secret, token, name, or email in any URL, refusals mapped to sold out, a smaller party, and a new price, secrets and keys, stored sessions (only the three fields, per operator, expired and malformed records dropped, storage failures never throw), and no logging in the source. Typecheck and Biome clean. `bundle:check` built Hermes bundles for iOS and Android on both variants.
- **Against a local API.** On the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container with the API in `wrangler dev`, the app's own modules, run in Node in the screen's order, booked a Sunset Harbor Cruise at Demo Harbor and a Two-Tank Morning Dive at Demo Reef with the test payment; each read back its booking and waivers through the stored session, and the same session sent with the other operator's origin got 401. A declined payment ended `failed`; a canceled hold ended `canceled`, and reusing its quote asked for a new price; a party of 499 said "Only 12 seats are left now"; a hold forced past its expiry read `expired`, and a payment sent after it still booked, as on the web. The emailed link opened its booking once (410 the second time), naming a person showed "Waiting for signature", and ending the session made the next read 401. In the console's API, Demo Harbor's booking staff found only Harbor's booking by reference and Demo Reef's owner only Reef's; each got 404 for the other.
- **Independent review:** no security finding. Fixed: a polling loop could outlive its checkout and replace a later one, so every step now ends the loops and answers of the one before; the hold's end reads again until the checkout is final, and an "expired" after a payment is read again for 30 seconds; a refused test payment or cancel reads the checkout's state; a checkout whose answer was lost allows only the same request until it resolves; one command at a time against double taps; canceling returns to the party step; the link field turns off autofill and suggestions. Not changed: Android backup settings for secure storage (an unreadable restored value is dropped).
- **UI review at phone size** (from the code; no simulator here): fixed screen reader access to buttons inside notices, labels on busy buttons, problems said aloud on iOS, the stepper's value, the keyboard covering the review step, the warning color's contrast (now 5.8:1), the policy row's size and its acceptance reset on a new price, the home screen as one scrolling list, a next action on every state, and wording matched to the web app.
- **Automated pull request review** on PR 133: twenty-one findings over five rounds, all fixed. The payment counts as sent before its request, so a hold that ends meanwhile still gets the late-success grace; after a payment attempt, expired and canceled outcomes say a payment that went through is booked or refunded, never that nothing was charged; a refused or undelivered test payment says it is unconfirmed and to press the same button again; a failed refund says to contact the operator; the booker is checked with the contract's `BookerDetails`; holds and prices count down on the server's clock, learned from a fresh quote or checkout as on the web; late answers never move a guest who has moved on; the link is exchanged once however it is submitted; stored sessions are written in order, and memory stays the truth while secure storage fails; a replayed answer never sets the clock; a test payment whose event did not reach the booking can be sent again after the hold ends, and the screen waits for it; ending a session forgets only that session, not the same booking opened again; stored sessions expire on the server clock the app has learned; the read that starts when a hold runs out never drops an in-flight payment's answer, and an expired read after a refused payment or cancel waits out the same grace.
- **Not shown.** No simulator or phone run. Hermes, React Native's fetch sending the `origin` header, secure storage on a device, and the screens at phone size with large text are for W1N-TESTFLIGHT on the owner's Mac.

### W1-WAIVER-RENEWAL: guest link renewal, a staff reset of the five sends, and a shared-address flag, built November 8, 2026

A guest whose emailed waiver link was used, expired, or replaced can ask for a new one from the signing page; staff can allow five more sends with a reason; the console flags a guardian address that is also another guest's own. Migration `0028` (provisional, claimed at merge) ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-waiver-renewal-guest-link-renewal-a-staff-reset-of-the-five-sends-and-a-shared-address-flag-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Built on `claude/project-thread-p7r7vu` on top of W1-G215-REMINDERS. Not released, and `0022` is not on Neon main.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed. `openapi.json` came from `pnpm contracts:generate`.
- **Integration.** On the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker): database 71 of 71 with `waiver_send_resets` pinned at SELECT and INSERT, domain-waivers 67 of 67, api-worker 331 of 331. The new `renewal.integration.test.ts` (5 tests) covers a spent link renewed to the same address and the new link working, the old token renewing nothing while the new link waits, an unknown token, a crew code, another operator, and an old address's opened link after a correction; three renewals a day held by the service and the database; a staff reset with a reason, audited, only once the five are used, up to 20 in all; and the shared-address flag. The API suite adds the public route's single answer, `no-store` and rate limit, and the staff route per role and tenant. One existing case now expects the new `waiver_deliveries_generations` refusal for a sixth send.
- **Independent review** (accept with fixes). Fixed: the two renewal holes below (it found them too), the existing test's expected refusal, and the shared-address flag no longer counts a minor's own email against their own row. Recorded: the renewal answer takes longer for a live token than an unknown one; only a holder of a valid token can see that.
- **Security review** (R9, the owner's standing go of 2026-11-08): nothing above low. Fixed, each with a test that fails on the old code: a link the old address had opened could still renew to the corrected address, and an old token could replace the guest's newest unopened link and spend their sends. Recorded: a direct writer acting as the guest can skip the three-a-day rule by leaving `guest_renewal` off (the five sends, 20 in all, and the other limits still hold); the reset trigger checks a staff actor but not their role, like every trigger; a guest who lost their newest email cannot renew until it lapses and is told to call or ask the crew.
- **UI review** at 375 and 1280 px in Chromium with Playwright: nothing blocking. Fixed: focus moves to the "5 more sends allowed" notice after a reset instead of dropping to the page, the reset dialog's errors talk about saving rather than sending, a row with all five sends used says so, clearer shared-address copy (not announced on load), "There's a newer link" leads with the newest email and makes the new-link button secondary, plainer "Check your email" copy with a fallback, and "Sending…" while busy.

### Per-operator sender domains and suppression (W3-G2.16-rest, first part), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-wqkheo`, from `main` at `ddc7d6d`, migration `0027_sender_domains_and_suppression.sql` (provisional), pulled forward as code only ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w3-g216-rest-per-operator-sender-domains-and-suppression-code-only-synthetic-pull-forward-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). No domain onboarded, no DNS change, no Cloudflare call, no send, no deploy.

- **Built.** `tenant_sender_domains` (read only for the runtime; added, verified, and retired only by the admin connection through `tools/email`, with audit rows); delivery sends from an operator's verified address, or holds the operator (`EMAIL_SENDER_POLICY=operator_only`, the production default) or uses the shared sender; `email_suppressions`, filled by the events consumer for complaints and hard bounces and read at each claim (`address_suppressed`), per operator; the consumer's domain check for operator domains; the provider revalidation table in the messaging README.
- **Acceptance, for this part.** Each operator sends from its own verified domain and never from a pending or retired one (S1, S2, S5, S6); suppression is per operator (S7 to S10); the consumer accepts an operator's own verified domain and refuses others (S11); a refused operator sender or a per-domain limit holds only that operator, a suspended account still stops the sweep (S3, S4, S4b). Scheduled reminders are not in this part.
- **Checks.** `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, and `pnpm check:docs` pass. `pnpm test:local-db integration` on the local PostgreSQL 17 container: 1,092 passed, 2 skipped, after merging `main` at `d2d68be` (other goals took `0023` to `0026`, so this migration became `0027`).
- **Reviews.** Security review (R9): no critical or high finding. Fixed: every account-level fault on an operator's own sender except a suspended account holds that operator only; parent and subdomain overlaps are refused; the known-domain check answers for domains verified now only; the CLI refuses addresses in audited text. Recorded: Cloudflare's suppression scope (per account or per sending domain) is an owner question; a writer with the runtime's credentials can still record a made-up complaint within its own tenant; the message does not store its sending domain. Independent review: no blocker or major. Fixed: dropped event types from an unknown domain are refused again rather than acknowledged; a held operator's lapsed attempt ends as `outcome_unknown`; a held operator gets no second turn; the after-commit log carries the hold; tests tightened (S2b, S3, S4, S11).
- **Owner steps.** Apply `0027` with the next release. Before an operator sends from its own domain: onboard it in Cloudflare Email Sending, publish its records, allow its address on the `send_email` binding, release, then `email domain-verify`. Ask Cloudflare where Email Sending data is stored, how long the Activity log keeps addresses, and whether suppression is per account or per domain.

### Staff sign-in through Cloudflare Access, built November 8, 2026

Deployed environments accept Cloudflare Access only, a Google or Microsoft account followed by Cloudflare's own second step ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-staff-sign-in-through-cloudflare-access-with-cloudflares-own-second-step-owner-decision)). The email sign-in link and its session cookie work in local development alone. No migration. The dashboard steps are the owner's ([staff sign-in runbook](runbooks/staff-sign-in-access.md)); nothing in Cloudflare was changed.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed: typecheck, lint, doc links, and every unit suite, including the new `staff-sign-in-methods.test.ts` (preview, staging, and production refuse a link request, a token exchange, and a session cookie, and ask for Access; local still checks the cookie) and five console tests (the Access screen replaces the email form for `access_required` and `invalid_access_token`, `access_not_configured` shows the unavailable screen, and a sign-in link on an Access-only console opens the Access session).
- **Integration.** The API suite passed on the local PostgreSQL 17 container (361 tests); every integration suite runs with `ENVIRONMENT` set to `local`, where the email link stays on.
- **Security review:** nothing above medium. Fixed: the console's "Continue to sign in" reloads the page instead of following a link built from the address (a `//host` path pointed off the console); the runbook pins Microsoft Entra ID to the operator's own tenant, ties each policy's exact staff addresses to one login method, turns off "Use identity provider MFA" (never a whole domain or `gmail.com`), adds no bypass policy, asks new staff to add their authenticator the same day, and checks that a second device needs the first. Recorded: the Access token carries no documented claim that the second step ran, so the Access settings enforce it and the runbook's check is the evidence.

### Refunds, booking credit, and fee reversal (W3-G2.9), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-g7e2w1`, from `main` at `7ced93c`, migrations `0029_refunds_credits.sql` and `0030_refunds_pending_index.sql` (renumbered at merge to the next free numbers), under the owner's code-only pull-forward ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w3-g29-refunds-booking-credit-and-fee-reversal-owner-decision-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Synthetic data, the fake provider, and the Stripe adapter's recorded-shape fixture only: no provider call, no binding, no deploy.

- **Built.** A `pending` refund state settled by the provider's refund events or an hourly sweep check; refund exceptions after 24 hours, for refused lookups, and for a failure after success, with a staff list; booking credit as an append-only ledger whose balance the database keeps; staff cancellation with the policy, its value as credit, or an owner's override with a reason, behind a preview that shows the fee reversed and the resulting balance; TideGrid's fee reversal on a succeeded cancellation refund; `cancelPayment` on the provider port, used when a checkout expires or is canceled. Screens learned the pending state and the credit remedy.
- **Acceptance, against the goal's list** (partial and full refund, retry, credit, and ledger tests): `packages/domain-booking/src/refunds-credits.integration.test.ts` covers a pending refund settled by its event, mismatched and unknown events, a pending refund that fails, the fee reversal amount on a taxed order, an exception only after 24 hours and once, credit issued, used, refused below zero, replayed by key, returned once its booking is canceled, and corrected only by staff, overrides that fit and that do not, a credit policy left to staff, and payment cancellation by the guest and by the sweep. `apps/api-worker/test-integration/refunds-credits-staff.integration.test.ts` covers who may preview, cancel, override, read, and adjust, idempotent replays and key reuse, and other tenants hidden. The Stripe adapter's unit tests cover the refund event mapping and `cancelPayment`.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): `pnpm test:integration` across every package after merging main (tax rates `0024`, waiver reminders `0025`, tenant access `0026`, staff sign-in, sender domains `0027`, waiver renewal `0028`), 1,117 passed and 2 skipped. `pnpm check` passed. An independent review found nothing blocking; its three smaller findings were fixed: a credit adjustment key the ledger refused, a provider rejection about an already accepted refund no longer settles it failed (and a success after a recorded failure raises `succeeded_after_failure` for staff), and a settled refund can no longer gain a provider id. Existing tests changed: the privilege catalog adds the new tables and update columns; one sweep test used `refund.updated` as an ignored type and now uses `payout.paid`; the Stripe pending-refund test now expects `pending`; the API suites that pay a canceled checkout on purpose turn the after-commit cancel off; the role and route lists add the new permissions and routes.
- **Still open.** The coordinator defaults D1 to D4 await the owner. Refund events from live Stripe and `cancelPayment` against live Stripe are unobserved. The fee's assessment entries arrive with G2.10. The console has no screen yet for the staff cancellation preview, credit, or refund exceptions; the API is in place.

### W3-G2.2b: tenant access remainder, built November 8, 2026

Support access, invitations, sign-out on disable, tenant export, account deletion requests, and abandoned-checkout retention, without managed OIDC and MFA ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w3-g22b-tenant-access-remainder-without-managed-oidc-and-mfa-synthetic-pull-forward-owner-decision-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Built on `claude/project-thread-34yl7y`, rebased onto `main` at `7b5f77b`. Migration `0026_tenant_access_remainder.sql`, numbered at merge after `0019` to `0025`. Not released, and not on Neon main.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed: typecheck, lint, doc links, and every unit suite.
- **Integration.** `pnpm test:integration` passed on the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container with `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`: every package, 1,074 tests and 2 skipped (after the rebase onto `0025`). New suites: `tenant-access.integration.test.ts` (two tenants on invitations, disable, support grants, export, and deletion requests) and `retention.integration.test.ts` (what qualifies, refusals for every role, a late payment success refunded).
- **Security review:** no High or Critical findings. Fixed: a late payment success on a redacted checkout is now refunded under the new reason `session_redacted` and the database refuses to confirm a redacted checkout; the retention floor is 30 days; the export leaves out stored waiver document bytes; README wording on direct writes.
- **Independent review:** accept with fixes. Fixed: invited people accept or decline on the console's no-access screen; support never sees resolution notes; refused support requests are audited too; OpenAPI regenerated; contract wording. Noted, not fixed: the export is built in memory (up to 10,000 rows per table), and disabling one membership signs a person out only when it was their last active one (D4).
- **Automated pull request review** on PR 123 (the reviewer named in [the build execution and agent plan](v2/10-build-execution-and-agent-plan.md)): eleven findings over five rounds, ten fixed. A state-neutral console title for `session_redacted`; a tool-neutral name for this reviewer. A person's membership disables are serialized, so two at once still sign them out; re-enabling an identity revokes any session that raced its disable; a grant's start and end come from one instant, so 240 minutes is exactly four hours; revocation finds a grant by id, however old; the export drops document bytes in the database; the support audit is bound to the grant and decision the request runs under; a booking insert is refused for a redacted checkout, whatever the writer; a late success on any redacted checkout (expired, failed, or canceled) is refunded under `session_redacted`. Not changed: a malformed request refused by validation before any tenant data is read is not audited (wording narrowed in D1).

### Demo guest app shell (W1N-APP), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-bqlorn`, from `main` at `ddc7d6d`, under the owner's go of 20:03 UTC and his decision of 17:45 UTC ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-demo-guest-app-pulled-forward-into-the-demo-owner-decision)). No migration, no API change.

- **Built.** `apps/guest-native`: one Expo SDK 58 and React Native 0.88 app, built per variant from `variants/` through `TIDEGRID_VARIANT`. It loads the brand and the next four weeks of trips through the existing public routes and `@tidegrid/contracts`, and fails closed on an answer for another operator. Strict `atob` and `TextDecoder` stand-ins load first, only where Hermes lacks them, for the brand contract's logo check.
- **Checks.** Unit tests in Node (variants, the endpoint guard, the loaders, the stand-ins against Node's own). `pnpm --filter @tidegrid/guest-native bundle:check` built the iOS and Android bundles for both variants with Metro. `expo prebuild` for Demo Harbor produced the bundle id and application id from the variant file; the generated projects were deleted.
- **Against a local API.** On the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`, `local-db.sh prepare guest-e2e`, the API in `wrangler dev`), the app's loaders, run in Node, returned each operator's brand and trips (42 for Demo Harbor, 45 for Demo Reef), `wrong-tenant` for a Harbor build given Reef's origin, and `not-published` for an unknown host.
- **Independent review:** accept with fixes, no tenant isolation finding. Fixed: the `atob` and `TextDecoder` stand-ins now follow the WHATWG algorithms in full (default and replacement decoding, the utf-8 labels, the exact `atob` refusals), and the app tests the engine's own versions at start instead of only checking that they exist, since Expo's fetch also decodes with `TextDecoder`; the API override must be https unless the build presents its own local origin, and `TIDEGRID_RELEASE=1` refuses any override; text on the brand color uses the contract's own ink color; the loaders' promises end on the failed screen if they ever reject; the trip list clears the home indicator; the slug-mismatch branch has a test. Recorded: the lockfile adds only the app's packages; vite and vitest snapshots in other packages now name `terser` as a peer, with no resolved version changed.
- **Not shown.** No simulator or phone run: this container has no Xcode or Android emulator. Whether Hermes keeps its own `atob` and `TextDecoder`, and whether React Native's fetch sends the `origin` header as set, are for W1N-TESTFLIGHT on the owner's Mac. The logo and brand fonts are not drawn yet.

### W1-G215-REMINDERS: waiver reminders before departure, built November 8, 2026

Two automatic reminder emails, 72 and 24 hours before departure, for a waiver still waiting for a signature, with migration `0025` ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-g215-reminders-waiver-reminders-up-to-a-completion-cutoff-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Built on `claude/project-thread-p7r7vu` from `main` at `ba9b6de` (W1-G215-SIGNING's `0020`). Not released, and `0021` is not on Neon main.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed, including the operator-site waiver tests, the cron sweep log suite, and the observability allowlist. `openapi.json` came from `pnpm contracts:generate` and did not change.
- **Integration.** On the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker): database 71 of 71 with `waiver_reminders` pinned at SELECT and INSERT, domain-waivers 61 of 61, api-worker 330 of 330. The new `reminders.integration.test.ts` (7 tests) covers both slots once each and the 2-hour cutoff, concurrent sweeps and another operator's trip, a slot an email already covered and a changed address, stopping at a signature, a revocation, or a participant change, the staff resend limits left untouched, signing end to end through a reminder link for an adult and a guardian, and tenant isolation with the database rules for every writer.
- **Independent review** (accept with fixes, nothing blocking). Fixed: a reminder no longer follows a first email by minutes (the 12-hour grace), the confirmed-booking check, the console's `next` slot after a sweep outage, "Reminder queued" until it is sent, a trip moved mid-sweep no longer fails the tenant's turn, and D2's wording. Recorded: an address whose last email failed still gets its reminders.
- **Security review** (R9, the owner's standing go of 2026-11-08). One high, fixed before the pull request: signing through a reminder link failed because evidence looked up the address only through `waiver_deliveries`; the evidence trigger and `signing.ts` now also read `waiver_reminders`, with a test. One low, fixed: the reminder trigger now ties the address to the latest invitation's. Recorded: a reminder supersedes an unused staff-assisted code made before it was queued, as a staff resend already does.
- **UI review.** Not run: no guest page changed; the console only gains reminder lines on the waiver card, covered by its unit tests.
- **Copilot review on PR 126**, four findings, all fixed: the reminder trigger now locks the request like the delivery trigger; the 12-hour grace counts from when an email was sent, not queued (the sweep and the staff read share one query); a reminder link closed by an address change now stops later reminders until a new invitation (service, staff read, and trigger); and the sweep skips requests it already saw in SQL with a `limit`. A second round found that requests already reminded or covered were read first on every run; the sweep now picks only requests whose due slot still needs a reminder. A third round: the console shows when a reminder was sent or failed, not queued; a failed reminder after a sent invitation shows as a failed email; and no reminder is promised as due now while an email is on its way, and the console says "no more reminders" only once nothing is pending. Two new cases fail on the old code. After the fixes: database 71 of 71, domain-waivers 62 of 62, api-worker 330 of 330, `pnpm check` passed.

### Per-location tax rates (W3-G2.5-TAX), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-v8gj5v`, from `main` at `ddc7d6d`, migration `0024_tax_rate_locations.sql` (`0023` went to the delivery foundation, PR 121), a synthetic pull-forward with a coordinator default in place of the CPA's answer ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w3-g25-tax-per-location-tax-rates-entered-by-each-operator-synthetic-pull-forward-coordinator-default-in-place-of-the-cpas-answer)).

- **Built.** A tax rate version names one location or none; offers and quotes use only the rates that apply at the trip's product location; the database refuses a quote line tax from a rate scoped to another location, and a rate naming another tenant's location.
- **Acceptance.** A rate applies per departure location, versioned (moving it appends a version). Quotes name the rate version they used, and a quote taken before a move reads back unchanged. Rounding in integer cents: 2.5% of 4500 is 112.5, rounded half up to 113.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): `pnpm test:integration`, every package, 1,047 passed and 2 skipped after merging the delivery foundation, including the new `tax-locations.integration.test.ts` (5 tests). Typecheck, lint, every unit suite, the release dry run, doc links, and the prototype checks passed.
- **Independent and security review** (R9, under the owner's standing go to merge without asking him): nothing above low. Recorded, not fixed: the trigger checks the named version, not that it is current (as 0005 already allows for stale versions); an archived location is accepted; the foreign key is added validated, on a small table with every value null.
- **Still open.** Staff screens for tax rates; apply `0024` on Neon main before the API release that carries it.

### Email bounces and complaints recorded from the provider's events (W1-EMAIL-EVENTS), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-wqkheo`, from `main` at `64e8ff7`, migrations `0021_email_events.sql` and `0022_email_events_index.sql` (provisional), pulled forward by the owner ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-email-events-a-local-consumer-for-email-bounces-and-complaints-owner-pull-forward-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Local only: no live event, no queue, no binding, no deploy.

- **Built.** A queue consumer in the API Worker for Cloudflare's Email Sending events (Cloudflare delivers them only by queue); the account and sending domain checks; the join on the provider id after the adapter's normalization, through `app.resolve_email_message`; the tenant's `email_events` inbox, first bounce or complaint applied once; unknown ids in `email_events_unmatched`; the unique provider id index built `CONCURRENTLY` (the first no-transaction file); `MessageStatus.deliveryReport`; "Bounced" and "Marked as spam" on the booking's Email card.
- **Acceptance, against the goal's list.** A verified event applies once (E1); duplicates and replays change nothing (E2); other accounts and domains are refused (E3); an unknown id is recorded and not joined (E4); the index is built in the CONCURRENTLY mode and is valid and unique (E10); the console card shows the report (card and model tests); two tenants (E6, E9). The message's state stays `sent` and the report sits beside it (D3, a coordinator default), rather than a new state.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): `pnpm test:local-db integration`, every package, 1,026 passed and 2 skipped (after merging the waiver signing PR, which took `0020`). `pnpm check` passed. Existing tests changed: two status key lists gain `deliveryReport`; three booking suites whose fake bindings answered the same provider ids in every test now answer ids unique per run, since provider ids are now unique across tenants; the migrate unit test names `0022` as the tree's one no-transaction file.
- **Security review** (R9, under the owner's go to merge without asking him): no critical or high finding. Fixed: refused events (wrong account or domain) are retried, not acknowledged, so a wrong setting dead-letters real events instead of dropping them; the adapter's guessable stand-in id (`cf_no_id.`) is refused; event times outside 2020 to 2099 are refused; the unmatched table is not append-only, so the owner can prune it, and its sequence is revoked from the runtime. Recorded: anything that can publish to the queue can forge an event (medium; the queue's producers are the only authentication, so none but Cloudflare's subscription may be bound); an event that arrives before its message is recorded as sent stays unmatched; a direct writer can still label its own tenant's sent message.
- **Independent review:** no blocker. Fixed: an event in a shape this code does not know (schema version, type, source) is retried, not dropped; retries back off from 30 seconds to an hour; a bounce that is not final is ignored; a stale database README line. Recorded: whether `failed` or `rejected` can follow an accepted send (if so, they should become reports); the race test does not force overlap.
- **Still open.** The live event subscription, the queue with a retry limit and a dead-letter queue, its consumer binding, and the `EMAIL_EVENTS_ACCOUNT_ID` secret: owner steps. O-P7 A9, the comparison of `send()`'s id with an event's `payload.messageId`, is still owed, so the join is unproven against the live service. The exceptions list does not show bounces yet.

### W1-G215-SIGNING: staff-assisted and QR waiver signing, built November 8, 2026

Staff start a waiver signing at the dock, on their device or as a QR code, with migration `0020` (provisional), pulled into the demo by the owner on 2026-11-08 ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-g215-signing-staff-assisted-and-qr-waiver-signing-owner-pull-forward-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Built on `claude/project-thread-p7r7vu` from `main` at `a55dc7e`. Not released, and `0020` is not on Neon main.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed in parts: typecheck, lint, every unit suite (operator-site 1,158, guest-site 971 and 16, api-worker 242, domain-waivers 24, contracts 257), the release dry run, doc links, and the prototype checks. `openapi.json` came from `pnpm contracts:generate`.
- **Integration.** `pnpm test:local-db integration` on the pinned PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`), with the open `0019` of pull request 116 applied locally so the numbering ran in sequence: every suite of this goal passed, database 71 of 71 with the new table pinned at SELECT and INSERT. Three failures outside it: two resend-limit cases that pull request 116 itself changes, and one checkout-expiry case that passed 9 of 9 when run again alone. The new `assisted-signing.integration.test.ts` (6 tests) covers online and staff-assisted bookings through the webhook and the sweep, an adult with no email and a minor's guardian; replay, a superseded code and its open session, the emailed link still working, the 15 minutes; cancellation, participant change, and version supersede; the identity tick, a non-staff actor, and two tenants; sign-out keeping the evidence and the PDF's "Assisted by" line; and the database rules for every writer. Forged-evidence cases now include another address and a staff method on an emailed link. The API suite adds the staff route per role and tenant, no token in any audit row, `no-store`, and the public end of a session.
- **Independent review** (G2.15 override model, per doc 10): accept with fixes, nothing blocking on evidence integrity, tokens, lock order, tenancy, or the QR encoder, which it decoded independently (32 of 32 at level M). Fixed: the API's route list test, the session end now rate limited like the other commands, neutral wording for links that came from a code, and this entry.
- **Security review** (rule R9, the owner's standing go of 2026-11-08): nothing above low. Fixed: a new start also ends a session an earlier code opened, the QR dialog tells staff to close it once scanned, the staff-device copy says the console is still open in the other tab, and a QR that does not fit no longer breaks the dialog. Recorded, not fixed: a direct writer with the runtime's credentials can name any staff member as the actor, the same trust as every actor-stamped table; the staff device's browser history may hold the link for its 15 minutes.
- **UI review** at 375 and 1280 px in Chromium with Playwright: nothing blocking. Fixed: a 20-second "Still there?" warning before the 2-minute clear (WCAG 2.2.1), a signer who already signed is never told to start again, the end screen on the guest's own phone no longer says to hand the device back, the one-time link takes focus and is retired once opened, Close is secondary until then, the link says it opens a new tab, and the QR button no longer uses the new-tab icon. Recorded: focus can drop to the page if the waiver was signed while the dialog was open.


### The 0014 resend residuals closed for direct writers (W1-0014-RESIDUALS), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-wqkheo`, from `main` at `c365545` and rebased onto `a55dc7e` (W1-FIN-EXC's `0018`), migration `0019_resend_residuals.sql` (provisional; `0018` belongs to W1-FIN-EXC) ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-0014-residuals-the-0014-resend-residuals-closed-for-direct-writers-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)).

- **Closes**, from the 0014 entry's "Not proven, or open" list, for a writer with `tidegrid_app`'s credentials:
  - "The two templates' keys are not pinned" (D4 C): closed for those two templates. A `booking_confirmation` or `booking_canceled` message under any key but its booking's original or a staff resend is refused (`email_messages_booking_key`), and an original must fit the booking's state (`email_messages_booking_state`). Case R1.
  - "The eligibility refusals stay in the service" (D5 A): closed. A direct resend for the wrong state, after the trip, with a failed refund, or while the original is being sent is refused by the ledger and leaves nothing (R2). A direct confirmation resend waits for a cancellation in progress and is then refused (R3).
  - "The waiver ledger's `send_after` hole" (D6): closed. A direct writer cannot bring a waiting waiver email due before its delivery's `send_after` (the new case in `sending-limits.integration.test.ts`).
- **The holes, shown.** Without the migration, R1, R2, R3, R5 and the `send_after` case fail on a fresh container: each direct write is accepted.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): `pnpm test:local-db integration`, every package, 999 passed and 2 skipped, in 323 s. `pnpm check` passed. Three existing tests changed: a claim case marks its first resend sent before the next, a binding case now fails earlier at the message (`email_messages_booking_key`), and the waiver re-add case moves its delivery's `send_after` before its email.
- **Independent review** on the first draft: no blocker. Fixed: an original key taken early (a false cancellation notice that would leave the real one a duplicate), a narrower lock-wait probe in R3, and the email trigger added to R5's catalog check. Recorded, not fixed: below.
- **Copilot review on PR 116**, three findings, all fixed: the in-progress check filtered outside its locking subquery, so PostgreSQL pushed the filter down and a claim of the original held open was skipped, not waited for (shown by the new R3b, which fails on the old query); a waiver email could move earlier while its delivery's insert was still open, so a delivery that is not yet visible now refuses the move; and R3 opens its gate in a `finally`. A second Copilot round found that the original's state check read the booking without a lock, so a confirmation could land after a cancellation's scan of pending emails; it now locks the booking `FOR SHARE` (new R3c, which fails without the lock). The full local run after the fixes: 1,008 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Security review** (R9, under the owner's go to merge without asking him, 2026-11-08) on `27185cc`: nothing above low. No privilege, tenant isolation, or `search_path` issue; error messages carry ids and codes only. One low finding, recorded below. Info: a message of another template can still take an original's key (the accepted other-template residual), and two comments now say the trigger's locks are weaker than the service's.
- **Still open.**
  - A message of another template is not held: a writer can send a confirmation's text under a template name of its own, or a `booking_manage` link message, under any key, and each claim mints a management link.
  - Eligibility is not decided again at the claim: a writer that cancels a booking by hand, skipping the cancel path's step that cancels pending confirmations, can let a queued confirmation resend go out.
  - The `0015` give-back forgery (W1-RESEND-CLAIM's D5) is unchanged.
  - A direct writer may leave the booking's earlier email of the template queued, so the original and a resend can go out close together (security review, low); the limits still count every resend.

### Staff resend limits held at the delivery claim (W1-RESEND-CLAIM), built November 8, 2026

Goal branch `claude/project-thread-wqkheo`, from `main` at `f8044a9` ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-resend-claim-staff-resend-limits-held-at-the-delivery-claim-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). Migration `0017_resend_bound_at_claim.sql` (provisional; `0018` reserved) and the delivery job's claim in `packages/domain-messaging/src/delivery.ts`.

- **The hole, reproduced.** Case C1 commits a resend after `SET CONSTRAINTS email_messages_staff_resend IMMEDIATE` and a wait past the 1 s bound: it commits. With its stamp moved an hour back, `0014` lets a service resend of the same booking through at once. At the claim it now waits 10 minutes from the first resend's claim, and a direct claim raises `email_messages_resend_interval`.
- **Proof.** On the local PostgreSQL 17 container (Docker, `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`): `pnpm test:integration`, every package, all passing, including the new `staff-email-resend-claim.integration.test.ts` (C1 to C10) and the two earlier staff resend suites (60 tests together). `pnpm check` passes. No Neon call, no deploy, no email sent.
- **Independent review** on `fabd793`: no blocking defect. Five findings were fixed: the delivery job only tries the tenant's resend lock, so a held lock never stalls the sweep; the claim log's insert takes that lock; the backfill skips a resend message with no booking; a refusal under two rules reports the later lift; the README's apply note now says an older delivery job stalls a tenant's mail while a resend waits. One is documented, not fixed: the claim log's stamp can lag a send by the run's deadline when a writer holds the booking row.
- **Still open.** A writer that forges the `0015` give-back after a send can make the same message go out again (D5, every message). Security review under R9: asked of the owner, answer pending. The `0014` residuals are W1-0014-RESIDUALS.

### Wrangler 4.152.0 and the Workers test pool (W1-TOOLING), built November 8, 2026

The three apps now pin wrangler 4.152.0 exactly, and the Workers test pool 0.23.0 resolves the same wrangler and miniflare through two overrides, so the lockfile holds one wrangler, one miniflare, and one workerd ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-tooling-wrangler-41520-and-the-workers-test-pool-owner-go-start-gate-waived-by-the-owner-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation)). The owner waived the start gate on 2026-11-08 by choosing "Waive gate, run TOOLING (Recommended)", so the prototype keeps its `npx wrangler@4.125.0` pin until W1-PROTO-RETIRE. The concrete versions follow that option's rule, the newest wrangler at least two weeks old plus the matching pool. The owner confirmed them on 2026-11-08 ("Versions confirmed, merge when green"). No runtime source, Wrangler config, or compatibility date changed. Nothing was released. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed: 4,059 unit tests and 1 skipped, the same as the baseline, with the Workers pool suites in workerd 1.20261019.1 (api-worker 241 of 241). The release tool tests passed 342 of 342. `release:dry` passed for all three apps on 4.152.0. Every integration suite passed on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 17: 983 tests passed and 2 skipped. The guest and console browser parts run in this pull request's CI.
- **nov1 readings.** Every reading the runbook and the release tool rely on was re-read in the 4.152.0 source and holds; the runbook Update notes map each old line to its new one. Declining the changed-secrets prompt (10220) still exits 0 but no longer prints the success line, which is safer. The anchor-alone postcondition is unchanged and still needed.
- **Body cap recount.** With a no-op stub that was never committed, 24 of 29 Workers cases fail first on both the old and the new pool, with the same cases and messages.
- **Same bundles.** At the baseline, `wrangler deploy --dry-run` gave byte-identical Worker bundles under 4.135.0 and 4.152.0.
- **Independent review.** Accept with listed fixes. Deploy and rollback now send a Durable Objects code-update strategy, which the first pass missed; the release README, the runbook Update notes, and the register now record it. No Worker here binds a Durable Object. The other findings were this entry and the owner's confirmation of the versions.
- **Security review.** Accept, no findings. Every new lockfile entry's integrity hash matches the registry, wrangler 4.152.0 carries npm provenance, no new package or install script was added, and the pool overrides cannot change the wrangler that releases use. It noted that wrangler maps 10215 to its refusal for `secret put` only, in both versions, so the release tool's plan text "secret put and delete fail with 10215" is not shown for delete by the source; a follow-up, not changed here. `nov1` still refuses both after a recorded API rollback.
- **Recheck.** Accept, with this entry.
- **Open risks.** `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` is deprecated and renamed `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`, whose 1.4.7 matches 4.152.0 exactly; the move is a proposed separate goal. The pool now runs with a wrangler and miniflare it was not published against. Whether the undici compiled into wrangler (7.29.1) clears the earlier Dependabot alert was not checked. The next `nov1` release is the first built with 4.152.0.
- **Process.** Two runs shared one local database container name and port, and one run recreated it during another's integration pass, which failed falsely. Each run now uses its own container; the counts above come from private containers.
- **Telemetry.** About one hour and ten minutes of agent time across build, two reviews, one fix round, and the recheck. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

### Runtime provenance brand checked by the inbox (W1-PROVENANCE), built November 8, 2026

The payments inbox now refuses any provider event that an adapter's `verifyWebhook` did not mint ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-provenance-runtime-provenance-brand-checked-by-the-inbox-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation), D1 confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-08 after the merge). The minting function records each event in a module-private set. `recordProviderEvent` checks that set before any SQL runs. A spread, `Object.assign`, a structured clone, a JSON round trip, a proxy, or a hand-built object is refused. A minted event is frozen, and its time cannot be changed. No migration and no contract change. Nothing was released. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed. Every integration suite passed on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 17: 892 tests passed and 2 skipped, including the new provenance suite, which shows that spread copies write no row and the real event records once. With the inbox check removed, 4 of the 7 brand unit tests fail.
- **Independent review**, under the payments reviewer override. Accept, with only this entry outstanding.
- **Security review.** Two low findings, fixed with tests that failed before. `Object.freeze` and `Date` are captured at load, like the set's methods. The scan that limits who may call the minting function now also refuses a namespace import or a dynamic import of the adapter module.
- **Recheck.** Accept with listed fixes. Deliberately obfuscated source (a comment inside an import, or a unicode escape in the name) still passes the scan. The package README now says the scan guards against mistakes, not against a hostile commit. The runtime brand still refuses every copy.
- **Open risks.** The brand is checked at the inbox only, so a forged event can read one account row before it is refused. Checking at the top of the booking and route functions is a proposed follow-up.
- **Telemetry.** About one and a half hours of agent time across build, two reviews, and one fix round, with one retry for a busy container port. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

### The Stripe card form in guest checkout (W1-STRIPE-ELEMENT), built November 8, 2026

Under the Stripe provider, the guest pay step now mounts a card-only Payment Element, loaded from js.stripe.com through an injectable loader with no npm dependency ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-stripe-element-the-stripe-card-form-in-guest-checkout-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation), coordinator defaults D1 and D2 pending owner confirmation). The publishable key is an API secret the owner sets, `pk_test_` only and never committed. It and the client secret reach only the checkout's holder through the Bearer payment read. Confirmation still waits for the checkout's confirmed state from a verified webhook. Decline, expiry, lost answer, and load error have designed screens. The guest CSP adds Stripe's script, frame, and connect origins (D3, approved by the owner on 2026-11-08). The fake provider path is unchanged. Nothing was released, and no Stripe call was made. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed. Every integration suite passed on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 17: 931 tests passed and 2 skipped. The new guest browser spec passed 12 of 12 at 375 px and 1280 px, with Stripe.js and the API doubled under the real CSP and every other Stripe host blocked.
- **Independent review**, under the payments reviewer override. Accept with listed fixes. A decline no longer counts as a payment tried, so leaving after a decline cancels the checkout and says nothing was charged, with tests that failed first.
- **Security review.** Accept, no findings: the CSP is set in one place and adds only the four Stripe origins, and the key and client secret reach only the holder.
- **Recheck.** Accept, with this entry.
- **Pull request review.** One high and three medium findings, each fixed with tests that failed first: a late read for an earlier checkout can no longer end the one that replaced it; the connected account is read before the last checkout read, so a checkout that closes meanwhile gets no client secret; the page lock covers the card field check and the confirmation, so Cancel cannot open over a payment in flight; and after an unknown outcome, no later failure claims nothing was charged. A recheck reproduced each on the earlier head and confirmed the fixes.
- **CSP approved.** The owner approved D3 on 2026-11-08 ("CSP approved, merge stripe-element then provenance").

### A guard on tenant-controlled email sender identity (W1-SENDER-GUARD), built November 8, 2026

Guest email now shows "Brand Name via TideGrid" from the fixed TideGrid address, and one sender name rule applies in brand validation and in rendering ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-sender-guard-a-guard-on-tenant-controlled-email-sender-identity-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation), coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation). The rule refuses names that read as TideGrid after lookalike folding, names made only of staff or mailbox words, a second relay, and characters outside a small Latin set. Email uses a brand's name only when it reads as the tenant's provisioned name. For both synthetic brands, the rendered subject and bodies match the old renderer byte for byte. No migration. Nothing was released. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **Checks.** `pnpm check` passed. Contracts 215 and messaging 112 unit tests pass after the last fix.
- **Independent review.** Accept with listed fixes. Digit words and filler words no longer let a staff name through.
- **Security review.** One high finding, fixed: Latin letters the skeleton could not fold were dropped, so "TɨdeGrɨd" passed and could bind to a provisioned name. Any letter that does not fold to a to z is now refused.
- **Three rechecks.** Each found a narrower bypass, and each fix has a test per name that failed before it. Seven Latin Roman numerals are now refused. "v!a" and "B!lling" no longer split around "!". A "!" or a digit at a word's edge no longer hides a staff word or "via" ("Support!", "Support1", "Harbor via1 Stripe"). The lead made the last fix. 
- **Pull request review.** Mixed readings still let "B!lling1", "B!lling!Team", and "Harbor v!a1 Stripe" pass. That was the fifth narrower bypass, so the fixed readings were replaced by one model: each digit or "!" may read as a letter, stay a digit, or separate words, and a name is refused if any reading is only staff words or has "via" after its first word. An adversarial recheck compared it with an independent brute-force oracle on 2.8 million generated names and found no bypass. It did find that a digit-only first word ("2 via Stripe") no longer counted as a first word; the lead fixed that, with tests that failed first. "Harbor V!A!Stripe" is now refused. Residuals: words joined with no separator ("Harbor ViaStripe", "SupportTeam") and the hand-written staff list. A further round fixed three more: delivery now keeps a stored sender name only if it binds to the provisioned name and drops a refused reply-to, so a message queued before the guard cannot send under another operator's name; the TideGrid lookalike check uses the same reading model ("Tide!Grid", "T!de!Gr!d"); and a domain label starting with xn-- is refused. Each had a test that failed first, and a recheck confirmed them against an independent oracle and every send path.
- **Telemetry.** About three hours of agent time across build, two reviews, and four fix rounds. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

One more finding: a claim that ended during the provider calls could still be resumed. `release` now reads the row again under the lock before resuming, with a test that failed first.

GuestGateway now budgets lookups of unknown hostnames at 20 per 10 seconds per isolate, and caps reads of each recently found host at 20 under way per isolate ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-guestgateway-miss-budget-before-cloudflare-for-saas-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation), coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation). - **Independent review.** Accept with listed fixes, four findings, all fixed. A token now comes back at most 10 seconds after its lookup began, even when the lookup never settles. Known-host reads are capped. Refusals log at most twice per window.
- **Security review.** Two findings. The cap on known-host reads fixed the second. The first is recorded, not fixed: a fresh isolate knows no host, so a flood of unknown hosts can make a live host answer 503 in that isolate while the flood lasts. It is the open risk for P10 in the register entry.

### A no-transaction runner mode for CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY (W1-MIG-CONC), built November 8, 2026

The migration runner now runs a file whose first line is exactly `-- tidegrid:no-transaction` outside a transaction, so `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` can run ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-w1-mig-conc-a-no-transaction-runner-mode-for-create-index-concurrently-owner-go-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation); the owner confirmed the D6 A standing rule on 2026-11-08, and D1 to D4 are coordinator defaults pending confirmation). Every other file still runs in one transaction. No migration in the tree uses the mode yet, and no real index was added. Nothing ran against Neon. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **The mode.** A marked file holds exactly one `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX CONCURRENTLY` statement, checked by a conservative scanner. Every run first looks for invalid indexes, under the advisory lock, and refuses with a printed repair command if it finds any; it never drops anything. Runners now poll the advisory lock instead of blocking on it, since a blocked runner holds a snapshot that a concurrent build waits for.
- **Proof.** Unit tests for the marker and the scanner, and integration tests on a throwaway PostgreSQL 17 container, using test-only fixture migrations. After the rebase onto `main`: `pnpm check` passed, with the database unit tests at 98 of 98, and `pnpm test:integration` passed 898 tests with 0 failed. A negative control with the old blocking lock deadlocked, which shows the polling lock is needed. A manual probe showed a long transaction elsewhere can fail a build and leave an invalid index, which the next run then refuses.
- **Independent review.** Accept with listed fixes. Fixed: the register's wording of who merges, and the rebase onto `main`. Rebutted with evidence: a feared silent reconnect between the session settings and the build, which happens only on an idle connection the runner never leaves. A security review was not run: the change touches no money, tenancy, token, secret, link, or hostname code.
- **Recheck.** Accept with listed fixes; the open item was this entry. It also noted a pre-existing hang when a connection is reset rather than closed, unchanged by this goal.
- **Telemetry.** About two hours of agent time across build, review, one fix round, and a recheck. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

### O-ACCESS: the D4 (a) run, steps 5 and 7 not shown, November 8, 2026

The owner ran the [November 1 runbook](runbooks/2026-11-01-preview-deploy.md)'s D4 (a) path for demo acceptance steps 4, 5, and 7, then directed the coordinator to finish it on the owner's work Mac through a remote session ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-o-access-the-d4-a-run-closed-with-steps-5-and-7-not-shown-owner-decisions)). The coordinator ran `nov1` there from about 04:24 to 04:41 UTC on November 8, on `main` at `69843c3` with Node 24.22.2, with every target declared by `--confirm`. The owner closed the run at 04:47 UTC with steps 5 and 7 not shown. The times and results below are *coordinator-reported*, from the JSON lines and Playwright output of that session; no log file was saved.

- **A1 seed.** The coordinator gave the owner the P2.4 seed (`nov1 db seed --live --go A1` with `SEED_SINGLE_TENANT_OWNER` for demo-harbor and `SEED_OWNER_DISPLAY_NAME` `Harbor Desk Owner`), and the owner reported at 04:15 UTC that it ran. The coordinator did not see its output. A first attempt with the placeholder text left in refused before any database connection (`tools/seed/src/seed.ts:59`). The owner later said they did not know which address the seed used, so the seeded address is not recorded and not confirmed.
- **Access policy.** Adding the address to the console's allow policy was the owner's step. Whether it was saved was not read back.
- **Sign-in, not done.** The headed `playwright open` window did not complete the sign-in (the owner: "verification in that chrome window isn't working"). The Access login page offered only "Sign in with: Cloudflare", a Cloudflare account login. The owner: "There's no pin. It's asking for a password." The runbook's P6 assumes a one-time-PIN sign-in. *Inferred:* one-time PIN is not a login method of the console's Access application, so a D4 address without a Cloudflare account cannot sign in until the owner adds it. The application's settings were not read.
- **Steps 2 and 4, guest part, pass.** `nov1 accept guest --grep 'Demo Reef' --project desktop`, about 04:24 to 04:25 UTC: a new Demo Reef booking, `TST93PQ4`, on the Two-Tank Morning Dive for 2026-11-12, canceled within policy with `full_refund`. The run did not record its booking and trip ids here.
- **Step 4, console part, not checked.** The owner declined the manual check of `TST93PQ4` in the console ("don't care, just keep going").
- **Step 5, fail, twice.** `nov1 accept console --grep walk-up`. At about 04:25 UTC both projects stopped at the Access login page, so the saved storage state carried no accepted session. *Inferred:* it was written when the failed headed window closed, since it was about 5 minutes old with mode 644; the coordinator set it to 600 and did not read it. At about 04:40 to 04:41 UTC, with a new state the owner saved, Access let the run in as the owner's own identity, not the D4 address: the console showed two operators and an Operator switcher, and `getByText('Demo Harbor Charters').first()` matched the switcher's hidden option, so both projects failed before the calendar. *Inferred:* no booking was written by either attempt.
- **Step 7, signed-in part, not run.** The `one-tenant identity` suite needs the D4 identity's state, which the run never had.
- **Step 7, public isolation probe, pass.** `nov1 isolation --profile preview`, about 04:27 UTC: `{"check":"isolation-public","probes":[{"probe":"B trip offer","status":404,"code":"trip_not_found","leak":false},{"probe":"B quote","status":404,"code":"quote_not_found","leak":false},{"probe":"B checkout","status":404,"code":"checkout_not_found","leak":false}]}`. It ran out of the runbook's order, between the two step 5 attempts and before step 7's signed-in part, on the owner's "just keep going". It writes one canceled synthetic checkout.
- **Cleanup.** After the owner's "Close it now", the coordinator removed `$NOV1_PRIVATE_DIR` with its storage state and traces.
- **Journal, unresolved.** `accept` and `isolation` each journal their result (`tools/release/src/commands/accept.ts:105`, `isolation.ts:152`). The session reported that the `journal.json` it read was last modified at 02:14 UTC and held no entry from this run. *Inferred:* the entries went to another state directory, or another file was read. Not checked further.

Not shown, as before: step 4's console observation and steps 5 and 7. To show them, the owner adds one-time PIN to the console application's login methods, signs in as a Harbor-only address whose inbox the owner can read, and runs a fresh step 2, since `TST93PQ4`'s trip date passes on 2026-11-12.

The owner runs the README's check step against Stripe's file before any Stripe-live window.
- **Independent review.** It ran with the reviewer override that the [build execution and agent plan](v2/10-build-execution-and-agent-plan.md) names for G2.7. Verdict: accept with listed fixes. Fixed: Stripe's own retry was wrongly named as a second backstop after a 200, since Stripe retries only on a non-2xx answer; the cron's checkout sweep is the only automatic one, about 16 minutes at worst. Deferred to the coordinator and applied in the pull request: this log entry, and dated Update notes on the payments README's two older risk bullets.
- **Security review.** Accept with listed fixes. The client address comes from `CF-Connecting-IP` only, matched exactly, and `X-Forwarded-For` is never read; a bad signature from a listed address still gets 400 and records nothing. Fixed: the same backstop wording. Open, recorded in the README: the list's protection depends on Cloudflare setting `CF-Connecting-IP` for every request, and the signature stays the credential.
- **Recheck on the final code.** Accept with listed fixes; the remaining items were this entry, the README notes, and an unsourced "retry within minutes" phrase, all fixed before the pull request.
- **Telemetry.** About two hours of agent time across build, two reviews, one fix round, and a recheck. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

### The release tool's CI gate (W1-CI-GATE), built November 8, 2026

`nov1 approve-sha` and `nov1 release` now refuse a SHA unless the newest run of `ci.yml` on `main` that tested it completed with all four jobs green ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-08-nov1-refuses-a-sha-without-a-green-four-job-ci-run-owner-request-coordinator-defaults-pending-owner-confirmation), coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation). Built on the owner's go of 2026-11-08. No release ran, no workflow was triggered, and no gate is claimed. The local runs and the reviews below are coordinator-reported.

- **Unit tests.** `pnpm --filter @tidegrid/release test`: 17 files, 342 tests passed, 0 failed. `test/ci-gate.test.ts` has 95 of them. The 247 tests from before the change still pass. `pnpm check` passed on the first version; the pull request's CI runs it again with the integration and browser suites, which this change does not touch.
- **Against GitHub, read-only.** The gate ran from a scratch script on the final code, with this container's own `gh` login passed in by the script, since `nov1` passes none. Passed: `7ef50cf` and `f72b1bd` (their push runs), and `91a4faa`, `9086a18`, and `1f39feb` (the owner's dispatch runs of 2026-11-08, each with a cancelled duplicate that never started). Refused: `dd30fc8`, whose push run [42142133952](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/42142133952) concluded success with integration, browser, and console-browser skipped; and `f79ec2a`, which only a pull request run tested. With `nov1`'s own child environment, `gh` found no login here and the gate refused, as D1 and D2 say.
- **Independent review.** Accept with listed fixes. Applied: a run cancelled before any job started is skipped (it had blocked the three dispatched SHAs); runs are ordered by their latest attempt's start, so a re-run counts; every page of runs is read, up to 1,000; the kill-switch's CI check is documented. A test exists for each.
- **Security review.** Accept, with hardening. No token reaches `gh`'s argv, printed output, or the journal. Applied: a dispatch counts only when its head is on `origin/main` and the run belongs to `jkowall/TideGrid` (a tag named `main` could otherwise run its own workflow file); `gh` is pinned to github.com; only the run URL `nov1` forms, and known states, are printed; a blank-input dispatch counts only under the name `ci`. Not applied, outside the goal's paths: a redaction rule for GitHub token shapes, and a time limit on `gh`. Both are recorded in the release tool's README.
- **Recheck on the final code.** An independent recheck, a security recheck, and an adversarial tester ran on the fixed code. The adversarial tester found no bypass and no wrong refusal, against crafted answers and against real runs on `main`. Both rechecks found one more gap, now fixed with tests: a newer run whose head a stale clone lacked was skipped, so an older green run decided. It now refuses and asks for `git fetch origin`. Also applied: a test for the kill-switch refusal, and the blank-input case in the runbook note.
- **Pull request review.** The automated pull request review raised four findings, each a way an older green run could decide over newer evidence. All four reproduced, and each now has a test that failed before the fix: a dispatch whose head looks off `main` still blocks when it is not green; an unreadable run row or job record refuses; a run seen in both queries keeps its highest attempt. A second pass raised two more, both real and fixed with tests: a date bound on the dispatch query (taken from the committer date, which a commit can set in the future) could leave out a newer failure, so the whole dispatch history is read; and a retry cancelled before it started now refuses, since it hides the earlier attempt.
- **Telemetry.** About 90 minutes from start to merge readiness, three rework rounds after reviews, no failed approach. Token use and cost per checkpoint: missing.

### Neon consumption read, November 8, 2026

This records a read of the TideGrid Neon project's usage and plan limits. The coordinator read the Neon console live at 00:50 UTC on November 8, in the owner's Chrome after the owner signed in. It was read only. The coordinator opened the projects list, Billing, the project overview, the branches list, and the project's Postgres settings, and changed nothing.

**Source,** *coordinator-reported*. The Neon console, read live by the coordinator. No export was kept as evidence. The console figures below are the coordinator's report. The November 5 figures are from the November 4 entry, and the marked lines are inferred.

- **Compute this month.** 5.19 CU-hrs, which is 18,684 CU-seconds (within 18 s, if the console rounds). The projects list, the project overview, and the branches list agree. The November 5 read, at 02:32 UTC, was 1,404 compute seconds, so 17,280 CU-seconds (4.80 CU-hrs) were added since. *Inferred:* that read's compute seconds are CU-seconds, since its 5,572 active seconds at 0.25 CU come to about 1,393.
- **Active time.** Not shown on the pages read.
- **Allowance reset.** The date and UTC time are not shown. The projects list says "Usage since Nov 1, 2026"; the project overview says "Since Oct 31, 2026".
- **Plan limit.** Free plan, 100 compute hours per project, in Billing's words. Billing also lists 1 GB storage and autoscaling to 2 CU per project. *Inferred:* the 100 hours are CU-hours, the unit the usage figures use.
- **History window.** 6 hours. The Postgres settings offer 0, 1, 2, or 6 hours on this plan, and say an upgrade gives up to 30 days. *Inferred:* a restore of `main` from Neon's history reaches back 6 hours at most, so it no longer reaches the November 4 run. Past that, only a branch such as P2.2's keeps an older state, and the run's branch was deleted on November 5.
- **Branches.** 10 per project (Billing). One is in use: `main`, which never expires.
- **Compute settings.** 0.25 CU, scale to zero after 5 minutes.
- **Rate, an estimate.** All 4.80 CU-hrs are put on the cron period, from November 6 at 01:11 UTC to the read: 47.65 hours, about 190 wakes. That is about 2.42 CU-hrs a day, or 2.47 if the metrics lag by an hour (the projects list says they "may be delayed by an hour"). It includes the November 6 run's own traffic, any use between November 5 at 02:32 and the cron's return, and any of the November 4 run's use that the lagging November 5 API read had not caught. So it is likely an upper bound. With a reset assumed at December 1, 00:00 UTC (not shown), the month comes to about 61 to 62 CU-hrs of 100. Demos, smoke runs, and migrations add to that.
- **The cron.** Kept. *Coordinator-reported:* asked after this read, the owner chose to keep the `*/15` cron. The answer was given in the coordinator's session and is not recorded elsewhere in the repository. The owner's [2026-11-05 decision](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-05-payments-and-the-cron-back-on-owner-decision) already covers it, so no new register entry.
