Update, 2026-11-02: asked "Request-size cap: in the first deploy, or after it?", the owner replied "go ahead" (the same message also asked whether the coordinator should run the deploy in the owner's terminal). The coordinator reads that as confirming the cap for the first deploy, so it merges after `0014` and before the runbook's first live step. The coordinator's default had been: if confirmed, it would merge after `0014` and before both the first live step of the [Nov 1 runbook](runbooks/2026-11-01-preview-deploy.md) and the tooling goal, which rebases onto it; that order is a coordinator default, not an owner decision. D1 to D8 and the 413 contract stay coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation. Proposed hardening. The runbook had not run: no journal, the live API at `b87e269`, Neon at `0002`.
- **Merged `main`** into `build/public-body-cap` with `git merge` twice, no conflicts: at `7a75fad` (`aba58ab`), then at `a44e5fa`. Together they brought pull requests 51 (rollback postcondition), 52 (release schema guard), and 53 (rollback listing re-read). They touched only `tools/release` and the runbook. The `0014` branch was not merged.
- **The contract.** `pnpm contracts:generate` after the merges left `packages/contracts/generated/openapi.json` unchanged.
- **Cancel coverage, new tests.** The cap's two `cancel()` calls in `apps/api-worker/src/body-limit.ts` were not covered. The declared-oversize case now gives its source a `cancel()` callback and asserts it ran once. A new chunked case sends 16 chunks of 10 KiB with no Content-Length, expects 413, fewer than 16 pulls, and one cancel. Uncommitted mutants, each red on its own: deleting `body.cancel()` failed the declared case (cancel count 0, expected 1); deleting `reader.cancel()` failed the chunked case (the same). The other 28 cases passed under each mutant.
- **Failing-first counts not recorded again.** The November 1 entry ties a recount to the tooling goal's Workers pool. That goal is not built, and the pool on `main` is the one the counts were taken on, so they stand.
- **Local runs** after the merges: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, then `pnpm check` passed (21 vitest runs, 3,320 tests, and 29 node tests; 3,314 on the earlier run, before the new case and pull request 53's release-tool tests). The Workers suite of the API Worker passed 180 of 180, and the body-cap file 29 of 29. `pnpm test:local-db integration` on a private PostgreSQL 17 container (port 55442) passed every suite, 835 passed and 2 skipped; the API Worker's 22 files, 270 tests. The browser parts were not run here; their fixed ports are shared with the other lanes, and the coordinator runs them.
- **The preview smoke check** is proposed for the deploy's P5 smoke phase, pending the owner's call on A0: A0 authorizes read-only HTTP, and this is a POST. It would send one 1 MB chunked HTTP/1.1 POST (`curl --http1.1`) to `/v1/public/quotes` with no Content-Length and expect 413 with `Cache-Control: no-store`, only when the approved release SHA contains the cap (the runbook gives the `git merge-base --is-ancestor` test). The command was checked against a local plain HTTP/1.1 listener on 127.0.0.1 (curl 8.7.1): it sent `Transfer-Encoding: chunked`, `Expect: 100-continue`, and no Content-Length. It was not sent to any deployed host, and not over TLS. An HTTP/2 upload is a separate, unverified framing.
- **Still not proven:** Cloudflare's edge framing of Content-Length for HTTP/2 and HTTP/3; what the edge answers an oversize chunked upload (local `wrangler dev` mostly answered a proxy 500); whether the edge ever hands the Worker a body on a GET or HEAD; the memory a body near the platform's 100 MB limit takes in production. No Neon or deployed run, and no CI run, since no pull request exists.
- **Merged `main` at `84fa71b`** (pull request 54, `0014`), after the owner's confirmation above. The log's two sections were kept. `pnpm contracts:generate` left the contract unchanged. `pnpm check` passed. `pnpm test:local-db` passed every part (integration, guest, console) in 282 s; the API Worker's integration run was 271 of 271.

## 2026-11-01

### Public body-size cap verification, November 1, 2026

- **Built** on branch `build/public-body-cap` from `main` at `89dd8df`, against the body-size cap goal packet. The owner authorized building it on 2026-10-31 by selecting "Request-size cap" in the coordinator's question tool ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#owner-decision-2026-11-08-public-body-size-cap-coordinator-defaults-2026-11-01) quotes the question and the option). Proposed hardening; D1 to D9 are coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#owner-decision-2026-11-08-public-body-size-cap-coordinator-defaults-2026-11-01)). No pull request: under D9 as it stood on November 1, none opened before P11 of the Nov 1 runbook recorded its release or its no-go. The owner's November 2 selection names `0014` and does not name this goal; this goal's pull request waited for the owner's explicit confirmation (the entry above; the owner's 2026-11-02 reply, read as confirming: see the body cap's D9 in the register). These counts were taken on the pre-tooling Workers test pool, which is still the pool on `main`.
- **Tests first.** `ce9627d` added the Workers tests. The module did not exist, so the file failed at import. Against a no-op stub of `src/body-limit.ts` (not committed), 21 of 27 cases failed for the stated reasons: 1 MB bodies answered 400 `validation_failed` on the public routes and 404 on the fake provider, staff, and unknown paths; the declared-length and Content-Length echo cases saw the client's value; no operation declared 413. The 6 that passed guard behavior that holds today: the webhook's 64 KiB boundary, the abandoned-read 400, GET and HEAD, the preflight, the webhook's own 413, and the contract scan. `9eeb531` added the integration tests: 2 of 6 failed on a private PostgreSQL 17 container (a 1 MB quote and a 17 KiB staff note each answered 400 `validation_failed`); the lookup counter, a 16,000-byte padded quote, the shared lookup, and a chunked signed event passed.
- **The cap** (`f91d3a9`): 16 KiB, 64 KiB under `/v1/webhooks/payments/`, mounted before CORS. Workers suite 178 of 178 (27 new); the body-cap integration file 6 of 6.
- **Mutants, not committed.** The cap moved after CORS: the oversize case spent a `cors:` limiter call, and a checkout made 2 hostname lookups, not 1. Trusting a declared length under the limit: the declared-length and echo cases failed. No refusal headers: the no-store and `Vary: Origin` case failed.
- **The lookup counter.** `pg_stat_user_functions` for `app.resolve_hostname`, with `track_functions = 'all'` set on the runtime role by the admin for the suite and reset after it. Each request's connection flushes its counts when it closes; a sentinel lookup after each oversize request shows that no straggling count arrived.
- **The contract.** `pnpm contracts:generate`: 23 operations gain a 413, +808/-26 lines, and the document is otherwise identical once sorted (checked by script).
- **Largest legitimate bodies,** worst case from the published contract, by script. The quote at every array maximum is the largest: 4,380 bytes as compact JSON, and 7,984 bytes pretty-printed with an indent of 4. The staff member invite is 2,178 bytes as compact UTF-8 with 3-byte characters in its free text, and 4,038 bytes from an encoder that escapes every non-ASCII character (Python's `ensure_ascii`). Participant 1,300 and checkout 794, compact UTF-8. Every guest and console client sends compact `JSON.stringify` bodies. A client that escaped every character, ASCII and keys included, could exceed 16 KiB with a quote (20,360 bytes); no mainstream encoder does that, so it is not a legitimate-traffic case.
- **Review fix round** (adversarial review of `47f5490`, no must-fix after its skeptics). The "with a Content-Length" cases sent none: inside the Workers runtime neither `app.request` nor `fetch` adds one to a string body, so only one pair reached the early refusal, and dropping its no-store and `Vary: Origin` left the suite green. `75855d4` sends the header, adds declared boundary pairs (16,384 and 16,385; 65,536 and 65,537 on the webhook) and a declared 1 MB stream refused with no pull, sends the abandoned read to an unrouted path and asserts the cap's message, makes the contract scan follow `$ref` and report untyped nodes, and makes the integration string bodies declare their length. Against the no-op stub, 23 of 28 Workers cases failed. Uncommitted mutants, each red: the early refusal without its marks (12 Workers cases, 2 integration), its threshold at half the limit (2), the early branch off (1), the abandoned read treated as the end of the body (1), the scan ignoring `$ref` (1). `21be1d0` says in the 10 guest-route 413 descriptions that a cross-origin browser gets the answer without CORS headers; no CORS echo.
- **Local runs** on the fix round's code (`21be1d0`): `pnpm check` passed, the Workers suite 179 of 179 (28 body-cap cases). `pnpm test:local-db integration` on a private PostgreSQL 17 container (port 55660) passed every suite, 835 passed and 2 skipped; the API Worker's 22 files, 270 tests. The browser parts were not run: the goal changes no browser-facing code, and their fixed ports are shared with the other lanes.
- **Not proven:** Cloudflare's edge framing of Content-Length for HTTP/2 and HTTP/3; what the edge answers an oversize chunked upload (in the review, local `wrangler dev` answered 5 of 6 chunked 1 MB uploads with a proxy 500, "Network connection lost", although the Worker logged its 413 each time; a 20 KB chunked body got 413 6 of 6). A preview smoke check, one 1 MB chunked POST expecting 413 with `no-store`, was planned for after the D9 rebase; it is now proposed for the deploy's P5 smoke phase, pending the owner's call on A0, which authorizes read-only HTTP while this is a POST (the entry above); whether the edge ever hands the Worker a body on a GET or HEAD (the cap skips them either way); the memory a body near the platform's 100 MB limit takes in production. The memory bound is checked by review only. No Neon or deployed run, and no CI run, since no pull request exists.

### G2.16b follow-up: database-held resend limits verification, November 1, 2026

Branch `build/g2.16b-followup-db-limits`, rebased on `main` at `027d876` (pull request 51). Local runs only: no pull request was opened during the Nov 1 deploy window, so CI has not run. Decisions D1 to D11 are coordinator defaults pending owner confirmation ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-01-g216b-follow-up-database-held-resend-limits-coordinator-defaults-owner-decision-2026-11-08)).

- **What it adds.** Migration `0014_booking_email_resends.sql`: the append-only ledger `booking_email_resends` under forced row-level security, `app.booking_resend_facts` (D4's counting rules in one SQL function, D2 A), a check trigger that stamps the actor (D1 B), refuses any isolation level but READ COMMITTED (25000), takes the tenant's resend lock, binds the row to the message its transaction queued, and refuses `_limit`, `_interval`, and `_rate` (D7 A); a deferred check that a `booking.resend:` message commits only with its ledger row (D4 A); and a backfill behind the 0009 guard with a strict key parse and a count check (D8 A). The service reads the facts, writes the ledger row in its savepoint, reads the database clock on the send path (D6 A), and maps a trigger's limit refusal to its typed refusal (D3 A). `isolation.spec.ts` probes the resend POST and the messages read under both tenant ids (D9 A). Design: the [database README](../packages/database/README.md#staff-resend-ledger-0014) and the [booking README](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#staff-resend-of-a-bookings-email-g216b).
- **Failing first** (commit `518ad8a`, the tests alone). On 0013: DB1 committed the bypass message (`expected 'ok' to be '23514:email_messages_staff_resend'`); the backfill test applied nothing and its strict-parse cases did not fail. DB2 to DB10 failed there only for the missing table (42P01), which does not count. Against a staged 0014 whose check trigger only stamped, with no deferred check (logged, not committed): DB1, DB2, DB3, DB4, DB5, DB7, DB8, and DB9 red; DB6 and DB10 green, as they test the guards and the stamping the stage kept.
- **Green.** The new suite `staff-email-resend-database.integration.test.ts`, 10 of 10; the existing `staff-email-resend.integration.test.ts`, 34 of 34, with time passing by moving ledger rows earlier and earlier resends seeded by the admin with the check off; `migrate-0014.integration.test.ts` and `tenancy.integration.test.ts`, 25 of 25.
- **Mutants** (each on a fresh container, logged, not committed; each turned exactly one case red): no advisory lock in the trigger, DB4 (all six writers counted 49 and committed); no deferred check, DB1; `attempts > 0` dropped from the facts function, DB3 (`attempted: 4`); no isolation check, DB9 (the stale REPEATABLE READ writer committed the 51st); no mapping in the service, DB7 (the `_rate` error propagated). For the isolation spec, a stored own-tenant 404 made the same-key probe answer 422, and a skipped membership check made the tenant B probes answer 200 or 201; both red in both projects.
- **O3, shown.** `main`'s service and suite against 0014: 19 of 34 failed, each with `staff resend message ... commits only with its ledger row`. A build from before this goal cannot send a staff resend once 0014 is on its database.
- **Local runs on the final code**: `pnpm test:local-db integration` on a fresh PostgreSQL 17 container passed in 143 s, every integration suite (841 passed, 2 skipped). `pnpm test:local-db console` passed 10 of 10 at 375 and 1280 in 38 s; the probe table printed the five new rows (six tenant B probes in all, each `404 tenant_not_found`; each own-tenant resend or messages probe `404 booking_not_found`), with no leaks. `pnpm check` passed. On the way it found `tools/release/test/live-gate.test.ts`, "a local target runs with no gate", red on this branch (1 failed, 119 passed): its mocked health answer named `0013_sweep_fairness.sql`, and the smoke check compares that with the tree's last migration, now `0014`. The mock now takes the name from `treeMigrations`, as smoke does, so it holds on `main` and here alike (120 of 120); the coordinator authorized that one change outside this goal's paths.
- **Closes**, from the [G2.16b entry of October 30](#g216b-staff-email-resend-verification-october-30-2026):
  - "The limits bind the service, not the database (D4 A); a trigger in `0014` is due before any pilot tenant." Closed on this branch: the trigger binds every writer.
  - "`isolation.spec.ts` has no resend probe. ..." Closed on this branch: the resend POST, with a fresh `Idempotency-Key` and a valid body, and the messages read, under both tenant ids.
  - "The operator's hourly count scans the tenant's messages by key prefix, with no index; fine at demo scale." Closed: the count reads the ledger by `(tenant_id, created_at)`.
  - The mistyped booker address item stays open.
- **Not proven, or open:**
  - CI has not run: no pull request is open yet. It no longer waits for P11. The owner decided on 2026-11-02 to merge this goal before the Nov 1 deploy, which has not run ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-02-g216b-follow-up-merges-before-the-nov-1-deploy-owner-decision); D10 and O1 replaced).
  - The two templates' keys are not pinned: a `booking_confirmation` or `booking_canceled` message under another key is not a resend and is not bound (D4 C, proposed).
  - The eligibility refusals stay in the service (D5 A): a bypass writer can resend a confirmation for a canceled booking within the limits.
  - The waiver ledger's `send_after` hole (D6) is recorded, not fixed.
  - Closed on 2026-11-02, with a limit: the release tool did not check `migrations.latest`. Pull request 52 now refuses an API release when the health reports `database` ok and a `migrations.latest` behind the tree's last migration, or none. When the health cannot say for 15 s, it asks the owner, and a yes releases with no check (`assertSchemaReady` in `tools/release/src/commands/release.ts`). So the runbook's P2 must finish before P3.2. P2 applies `0003` to `0014` together (O2 replaced).
  - A resend's stamp may lag its commit by up to 30 s (the fix round's bound): measured by commit times, a booking's resend may commit 9 minutes 30 seconds after its previous one, and 51 of the operator's within 59 minutes 30 seconds. Those figures hold for the service and for writers that wait for nothing after the commit check's deferred event runs. Any wait after it, before the commit record, is unbounded: a deferred foreign key whose referenced row another session holds (the final check's probe let two resends commit 45 ms apart), `PREPARE TRANSACTION` where `max_prepared_transactions` is above 0, or `SET CONSTRAINTS ... IMMEDIATE`. Each needs a direct writer with `tidegrid_app`'s credentials. Bounding sends at the delivery claim is a possible pre-pilot follow-up.
  - The console gives up on a call after 15 s, so a resend refused at the 30 s bound shows "can't reach TideGrid" rather than `booking_email_busy`; the failure is not final and the dialog keeps the key.
  - No security review (O5).

#### Fix round, November 1, 2026

The coordinator's adversarial review of `3da840c` (run `wf_fcd75283-bba`, five dimensions, three skeptics per finding) left no must-fix after the skeptics; the counting rules matched the service in every probe. Its should-fix items and nits were fixed on this branch, with 0014 edited in place (it is not merged), under coordinator decisions recorded in the [decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-11-01-g216b-follow-up-database-held-resend-limits-coordinator-defaults-owner-decision-2026-11-08).

- **What changed.**
  - The ledger row is stamped, and the limits are decided, at the transaction's time to the millisecond. That is the service's instant and its message's time.
  - A resend whose transaction ran longer than 30 s, or less if the transaction asks through `app.resend_max_transaction`, is refused `booking_email_resends_late` at its insert and at commit. The route answers that as 503 `booking_email_busy`, with nothing written or stored.
  - Every `booking.resend:` message insert takes the tenant's resend lock before its key is claimed, which removes the deadlock the review found (probe P1).
  - The catalog pins the five 0014 functions as invoker, with their volatility and the runtime's EXECUTE. The facts function is called across tenants as the runtime.
  - DB8's boundary is cast on the server, so it is exactly 10:00.
  - The service's own lock and the direct writer's wait at its message insert are tested.
  - `migrate-0014` migrates only through 0014, asserts the parse's own error, and adds a template case.
  - Stale comments in `isolation.spec.ts` are fixed.
  - A clock stamp taken after the lock was tried first and dropped: it moved the ledger time off the message's time, so the API's availability read (`booking-email-resend.integration.test.ts:277-283`, read only here) came out 23 ms later, and a writer could still hold its transaction after the stamp.
- **Failing first** (commits `0a04b0a` and `c7a2710`, tests alone).
  - On the reviewed 0014: DB12 (the service got 40P01), the old DB13 (stamped at the transaction's start), DB7 (no lock trigger), the catalog row, and the backfill facts (newest_at not at milliseconds).
  - On the clock-stamp 0014: the new DB13 (`'ok'`, no late check), DB15 (no bound function), DB16 (`'committed'`), the catalog row, and both workerd route cases (500 `internal_error`).
  - DB11 and DB14 pin behavior that already held. Their mutants are below.
- **Green.**
  - The new suite, 16 of 16.
  - The existing suite, 34 of 34.
  - `migrate-0014`, `tenancy`, and `migrate-upgrade`, 27 of 27.
  - The workerd route test, 52 of 52.
  - With a dummy `0015` present, `migrate-0014` still passed.
- **Mutants** (each on a fresh container, not committed; each turned the named cases red):
  - interval `>=`: DB8 (direct `_interval` and service `too_soon` at 10:00)
  - no service lock: DB11 (25 relation locks held by the waiters) and DB12
  - an upper bound on the newest resend: DB14; on the hourly count: DB14
  - SECURITY DEFINER on the facts function: DB5 (B's facts seen from A) and the catalog row
  - no lock at the message: DB12 (40P01)
  - neither lock: DB4 and DB12
  - no template check in the backfill: `migrate-0014`
  - no late check at the insert: DB13 and DB16 (refused only at commit)
  - no late check at commit: DB13 and DB16
  - the bound without `least`: DB15 (`01:00:00`); a malformed setting read as 30 s: DB15
- **The service under the bound.** Measured locally, a service resend's transaction runs about 20 ms, and the last of 20 at once on one tenant about 450 ms, queued on the tenant's lock.
  - A delivery claim it waits for is a short transaction; the provider call, bounded at 10 s, is outside it.
  - At Neon's round trips, a resend is about 25 statements, so reaching 30 s would take over 100 resends of one tenant at once.
  - The bound stays at 30 s.
- **Local runs on the final code**:
  - `pnpm test:local-db integration` on a fresh PostgreSQL 17 container passed in 127 s, every integration suite (848 passed, 2 skipped), the API resend suite unchanged among them.
  - `pnpm test:local-db console` passed 10 of 10 at 375 and 1280, all 12 probes in each project leak-free.
  - `pnpm check` passed.

#### Final round, November 1, 2026

The coordinator's final check of `7bb40f7` (run `wf_ec178112-382`) found no must-fix: the 503 mapping, the locks, the setting, and the service path held.

- **The residual is a class.** 0014's header, the [database README](../packages/database/README.md#staff-resend-ledger-0014), and the decision register now say the commit check bounds a stamp only up to its deferred event. The 9 min 30 s and 59 min 30 s figures hold for the service and for writers that wait for nothing after it.
  - The check's probes: P1 and P3, a deferred foreign key wait, let two resends commit 45 ms apart; P4 is PREPARE TRANSACTION.
  - The catalog suite now fails unless `max_prepared_transactions` is 0, and the bound relies on that. No migration asserts it.
- **DB14's flake.** Its precondition compared a millisecond stamp with S's microsecond `now()`, so it failed when the direct writer began in S's millisecond; the check saw this in 25 of 150 runs. DB14 now waits 2 ms after S opens and keeps the strict `>`.
  - 20 runs of DB14 alone all passed.
  - The upper-bound mutant still turns it red.
- **The 503 is the exact constraint.** A workerd case answers another 23514 at commit (`email_messages_staff_resend`) as 500. Mapping every 23514 to 503 turned that case red, 1 failed and 52 passed.
- **Documented, no code change.** A 30 s late refusal reaches the console only after its 15 s timeout, so staff see "can't reach TideGrid"; the failure is not final and the key is kept.
  - The trigger's second take of the lock, and its VOLATILE marking, are now described as a second line behind the message lock.
  - The booking README's test list covers DB11 to DB16.
- **Local runs** on the final code: `pnpm test:local-db integration` on a fresh PostgreSQL 17 container passed in 128 s, every integration suite (849 passed, 2 skipped). `pnpm check` passed.

## 2026-10-30

### G2.16b final check verification, October 30, 2026

- **The final check** of `ecf7189` (the coordinator's run `wf_2911e4cc-b13`): clean on locks and the domain. The wait-for analysis in the [booking README](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#staff-resend-of-a-bookings-email-g216b) held; 8 of 8 interleaving probes ran with no deadlock (40P01) and no lock-not-available error (55P03); both lock mutants went red. One should-fix, confirmed by three skeptics, which the previous round introduced, and five nits.
- **Focus after a refusal** (`d534df0` red, 4 failed on `ecf7189`'s card; `9405288` green). The re-read after a final refusal can take Send again away while the dialog is open, and the dialog does not give focus back to an opener that left the page, so Cancel left focus on `<body>`. When the dialog closes and focus has nowhere to go, the card's heading takes it, in a layout effect. The same holds when the re-read lands after Cancel and removes the Send again that focus had returned to. Try again that ends signed out removed its own button and dropped focus too: the heading takes it, and the signed-out notice now offers Sign in again (this page from the start, as the console's other reads do).
- **Nits.** The resend module's header says when its link is minted and that a later link email supersedes it (`b03cfb5`, a comment). The review fixes entry below states the review's tally exactly and names the fix round's CI. The booking README says what a resend holds while it waits for the operator's lock.
- **The race harness** was rebuilt from the memory note after the scratchpad was purged, and kept outside the repository. The whole operator-site suite under it (2 ms spin): 3 of 3 runs, 1,022 tests each.
- **Merge timing.** The owner decided on October 30 to merge G2.16b now, before the Oct 31 freeze ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-10-30-g216b-merge-timing-changed-owner-decision)), so it rides into the Nov 1 deploy unless an earlier SHA is frozen at P0.1. G2.16b adds no console browser spec and changes neither `staff-booking.spec.ts` nor `isolation.spec.ts`, so nothing new runs in deployed mode, and the runbook's acceptance runs select the same tests as before. On the preview, email is off until runbook P7 switches it on, so until then the Email card says so and offers no Send again; from P7 on it offers Send again, which supersedes the booking's earlier link, so P7 does not press it on a booking it verifies. The walk-up journey passed 8 of 8 locally with `EMAIL_PROVIDER` set empty.
- **Local runs:** operator-site 21 files, 1,022 tests; `pnpm check` passed with this entry. The domain and the API are unchanged since `eae3e98` but for that comment. CI on the last code commit, `b03cfb5`: run [41148458089](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41148458089), all four jobs green (checks, integration, the guest browser job, and the console browser job). The commit after it changes docs only.

### G2.16b review fixes verification, October 30, 2026

- **The review.** One combined adversarial review of `6678095` (six dimensions, three skeptics per finding, the coordinator's run `wf_cb9f85e0-2f5`): no must-fix; money, tokens, and tenancy clean; probes found no double send and no deadlock between a resend and a delivery claim, a cancellation, or a link-email claim with a revoke. Three dimensions each reported one item as must-fix, the finance test that could not fail; all its skeptics rated it should-fix. After the skeptics, eight distinct should-fix items (the operator cap, the refund lock, the finance test, the dialog's key, the notice, the link copy, the race test, D4's counting rules) and eight nits, all fixed on the same branch with tests first.
- **The operator's cap is exact** (`67d0dcb` red, `cf71d2b` green). It was a plain count under READ COMMITTED with only the booking locked, so resends of different bookings at once each read the same count. F1 runs six at once against 49 earlier resends while an admin connection holds audit inserts, so each reads the count before any commits: on the old code all six queued and the hour held 55. The send path now takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock per tenant first, before any row lock; F1 then gives one queued and five `rate_limited`, 50 in the hour, and fails again with the lock removed. The lock-order analysis against checkout, confirmation, `cancelBooking`, the link requests, the delivery claim, `settleRefund`, and waivers is in the [booking README](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#staff-resend-of-a-bookings-email-g216b). A coordinator decision within D4 A, no migration.
- **The refund check waits for a refund being refused.** For a cancellation notice the send path locks the cancellation's refund row `FOR SHARE` before deciding. F2 holds a refusal uncommitted on another connection: on the old code the notice queued; now it waits, then refuses `refund_failed`, and fails again with the lock removed. The read stays lock-free.
- **D4's counting rules, pinned.** F3: a resend attempted and then canceled counts toward the 5. F4: a resend canceled with its booking still starts the 10 minutes, so a notice straight after is `too_soon`. F5: canceled resends count toward the 50 an hour. F6: a resend waits for a delivery claim in progress and refuses `in_progress`. Each passed on the code and failed against its one-line mutant (counting only non-canceled rows, three times; reading the earlier messages without their lock, which then trips the `inFlight` guard). An API case pins the read's `allowed: false` with email off; it fails when `allowed` ignores email off.
- **The console** (`ba9ac44` red, 12 failed; `eae3e98` green):
  - The dialog's key is bound to its reason. After no answer, a changed reason takes a new key, so the server's own answer (here `too_soon`) is what staff see, not a 422 loop saying nothing was sent. A 422 `idempotency_key_reused` now says an earlier try may have gone through, and any final refusal reads the card again, so it offers what is current.
  - The notice is "Queued to send again" and leaves delivery to the list; it no longer says the email is on its way beside a resend that failed.
  - The dialog says earlier links stop working once TideGrid starts sending, even if that send fails.
  - `outcome_unknown` names a second working link only for an email that carries one. A signed-out read asks to sign in again, with no Try again.
  - The finance case now names the messages route, so a request would be recorded: it fails when the card is mounted for finance. A new MutationObserver case reads focus when Try again's list commits, before passive effects: it fails when the heading's focus moves into `useEffect`, which the committed suite could not catch before.
  - Race harness (uncommitted, 2 ms spin): the email, detail, canceled, and App suites 3 of 3 (199 each); with 0 to 20 ms of fetch jitter, the email and detail suites 105 of 105.
- **Docs.** The decision register, the build plan, and this log no longer say the cap "may pass by one", nor that only the newest link works (a link email the guest asks for later supersedes the resend's link), and they say D10 was the owner's choice, not a default (replaced on October 30: merge now). The earlier entry's red run is now stated exactly, and part 2's card landing with its tests is recorded as a deviation.
- **Environment.** The podman machine's clock fell 7,139 s, then 1,462 s, behind the Mac after it slept; `chronyc makestep` did not fix it, so the clock was set from the Mac's (`podman machine ssh "sudo date -u -s @<epoch>"`). One domain run stalled 390 s in an unrelated case while the Mac slept (pmset: 18:06 to 18:14); the rerun under `caffeinate` took 5.6 s.
- **Local runs on the fixed code** (`eae3e98`, before this entry): `pnpm test:local-db integration console` passed both parts on fresh PostgreSQL 17 containers in 128 s, every integration suite (823 passed, 2 skipped) and the console browser specs 8 of 8 at 375 and 1280. A first run stalled 470 s in an unrelated API case while the Mac slept (pmset: 19:03 to 19:11) and was rerun. `pnpm check` passed with this entry. CI: run [41138843055](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41138843055) on `eae3e98` and run [41143936708](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41143936708) on `ecf7189`, all four jobs green each time. (The lead's report first named 41138886426, which is the Copilot workflow on `eae3e98`, not CI.)

### G2.16b staff email resend verification, October 30, 2026

- **Built** on branch `build/g216b-email-resend` from `main` at `1d2ebab`, against the G2.16b goal packet, whose defaults the owner accepted on 2026-10-29, with D10 the owner's own choice ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-10-29-g216b-staff-email-resend-owner-decisions)). Pull request 48. The merge was to wait until after the Nov 1 deploy (D10); on October 30 the owner decided to merge it now, before the Oct 31 freeze ([decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#2026-10-30-g216b-merge-timing-changed-owner-decision)). Two parts, so the first can land alone: G2.16b-1 (domain, route, contract) ends at `218980d`; G2.16b-2 (the console card) runs from `fcbda11`. No migration: the next free number stays `0014`.
- **What it does.** Each booking email's delivery shows on the booking's detail page for owners and booking staff, with the failure in plain words and whether email is off. Staff send the confirmation (with a new management link) or the cancellation notice again with a reason code, as a new message: idempotent by key, limited across requests (5 per booking, 10 minutes apart, 50 an hour per operator), audited with ids and codes only, and refused, with nothing written, in a fixed order. Details: the [booking README](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#staff-resend-of-a-bookings-email-g216b) and the [console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#booking-email-g216b).
- **Tests first.** `1a16340` put in the contract, the permission, and a stub service that throws. `30d251c` added the tests, red for the stated reasons: the domain suite 28 failed, 27 on the stub's error ("the staff resend service is not built yet") and 8c on its assertion, because delivery caught the stub's throw inside the provider call; the API suite 5 failed (the route answered 404 and the read had no `emailDelivery` or `resend`), with the two existing staff-read cases green with `staffResend`; the workerd order suite 51 failed on the missing route. `218980d` turned them green: 28 of 28, 7 of 7 with the staff reads, and 84 of 84 with the auth guards.
- **What the domain suite proves** (`packages/domain-booking/src/adversarial/staff-email-resend.integration.test.ts`): over a Cloudflare recorder, a confirmation lost as `outcome_unknown` is sent again once, its new link opens, and the old link answers `superseded` (two binding calls in all); a resend whose own send is unknown, or whose lease lapses mid-send, is not sent again, and the earlier link still renews; one resend is one accepted delivery on the fake; two concurrent resends write one message and the other answers `too_soon`; the interval, the fifth resend across two staff members, and the operator's 50 an hour across bookings, with `at` moved forward since `created_at` cannot be backdated; a resend canceled with its booking or replaced as `resent` keeps its generation and does not count toward the 5; the kind guard (both mismatches, the trip's end, a failed refund); an earlier queued email of the same kind canceled as `resent` with its link superseded, a guest's own link email left alone, and `in_progress` while one is being sent; a refusal leaves every queued email queued; `email_invalid` under a savepoint rolls back the earlier email's cancel; the audit row's JSON holds no address, name, subject, or reference; the status read flags the resend; another tenant reaches nothing; and the refusal order, one pure case per adjacent pair.
- **Packet case 10, changed.** Its trigger, a booker's name over 120 characters, cannot be stored: `checkout_sessions.booker_name` is 1 to 120 characters with no controls and immutable (migration 0007), and the composers cut every text. The case forces a `TypeError` from `enqueueMessage` through a pass-through module mock instead.
- **The API** (`apps/api-worker/test-integration/booking-email-resend.integration.test.ts`): 201, a replay with `Idempotent-Replayed`, 422 for the same key with another body, a refusal stored and replayed as the same 409, finance 403 on the read and the resend with email on and off, the same 404 for another tenant's, unknown, malformed, and uppercase ids, validation before anything (no key, a free-text reason, an address in the body), and with email off: 401 first, then `booking_email_off` with no key stored and no message written, a success replayed, and a key that was never stored getting its own decision once email is on. The workerd suite (`apps/api-worker/test/booking-email-resend.test.ts`) checks the same order for each of the eight off or refused settings, with recorders in place of the database.
- **The console.** `fcbda11` added the card and its tests (38 cases) in one commit, not tests first: a deviation from the packet's order, recorded by the review. `44c0a71` mounted it, red on purpose: 45 detail tests failed on a stray `GET .../messages` in `BookingDetail.test.tsx` and `canceled.test.tsx`; `App.test.tsx` stayed green because it stubs fetch itself. `11cc776` added the default answer in `bookings/fixtures.ts`: 21 files, 1,012 tests green.

That run covers the code before the fix round below. Run [41119521071](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41119521071) on `7ad791b`, the fix round's final code, is green on the same four jobs. The commits after it record the owner's merge-now decision in the decision register, this log, and the demo build plan, and change only the browser spec's deployed-mode skip reason; pull request 47's run on the merged head covers them.
- **Review** (combined, three skeptics per finding): 0 must-fix, 4 should-fix, and nits. Fixed in a fix round, tests first:
  - Finding 1: the other operator's mixed bookings were on another date, so the cross-operator list and cursor checks passed on the date filter alone. Both operators' mixed days now share one date (asserted), and each operator's blackout on it marks only its own trips. With the tenant predicate dropped from the page and cursor queries and row-level security off on their tables, in a scratch run, three G2.12e cases fail; the earlier version of the cases passed under the same change.
  - Finding 2: a Show more answer for the source the person had left was merged into the new list when the new list ended on the same cursor. A new listing now aborts the last one's Show more. The new jsdom case failed first, and fails again when the abort check is removed.
  - Finding 3: Book at a filtered count of 0, and at an unfiltered count of 0, for owner and booking staff. These guard cases passed first; gating Book on the count fails four of them, and gating it on the filter fails two.
  - Coordinator-authorized: a staff booking's opening line on its detail said "Booked online by the guest"; it now says "Staff booking, phone.", as its Source row. A row in a filtered list links to the booking with `?source=`, and the booking's link back to its day keeps that source (only the two API values). An unknown channel reads as plain "Staff booking". Each failed first (new `BookingDetail.source.test.tsx`, model cases, and list cases).
  - Nits: the D7 decision moved to the dated owner decisions in the register; the console README's address rules and staff booking section; this entry's sign-in note.
  - After the fix round: `pnpm check` green; the race harness on the changed files 5 of 5, 5 of 5 with fetch jitter, and the whole operator-site suite once; `pnpm test:local-db integration` and `console` green. CI on the fix round is the coordinator's to name.
- **Deviations from the goal packet:** the branch is `build/g212e-bookings-source`, as the coordinator's brief named it. The first column of the bookings table widened from 7.5rem to 9rem for the badge. The source note sits under the filter controls, beside the zone line.
- **Not proven, or open:**
  - The positive badge and filtered rows in a browser on seeded data: the seed has no staff bookings, so the spec checks the badge's place only when the walk-up journey has made one.
  - Sign-in headroom in the console browser job: the per-address limiter is not bound in the API's `dev` environment, so it does not apply there. The database's own limit of five sign-in links per person in 15 minutes does (`app.auth_issue_login_token`, migration `0002`): Ben Desk now signs in four times per job, two of them in the new spec.
  - `sourceText` reads any source other than staff as "Online, by the guest" (G2.10 must change it).
  - Neon-specific behavior, as for every suite on the container.

## 2026-10-29

### G2.12d uncertain-payment follow-up verification, October 29, 2026

- **Built** as the follow-up filed by the G2.12d final check when pull request 28 merged (two should-fix findings, each reported twice), for the owner's request of 2026-10-29 to start both follow-ups and merge when green. Pull request 46, on branch `build/g212d-uncertain-payment` from `main` at `190caa6`, after pull requests 43 and 45. Console only: `apps/operator-site/src/new-booking/NewBookingPage.tsx`, its tests, and the [console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#staff-assisted-booking-g212d). No server, contract, migration, or integration test change. No Neon, deploy, or install beyond `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`.
- **Server facts the fix rests on**, read in the code, which is unchanged:
  - `withStaffTenant` claims the idempotency key, runs phase 1, and stores its answer in one transaction, and a refusal rolls all of it back (`apps/api-worker/src/staff-tenant.ts`). A key replays only a request whose phase 1 committed; a first try that recorded nothing makes the next one a new request.
  - Phase 2 of the test payment delivers the payment's existing event whatever the checkout's state, and settles only when the checkout is open and inside its hold; otherwise it answers `checkout_not_open` (`apps/api-worker/src/routes/staff-booking.ts`). A new request's phase 1 refuses a closed checkout with `checkout_not_open` before it records anything (`requestStaffTestPayment`). The fake keeps one event per payment (`packages/domain-payments/src/fake.ts`). So no resend on a closed checkout settles anything, and none can settle a second payment.
  - A verified success on a canceled checkout ends unfulfilled, refunded in full with an exception. On an expired one it reacquires the hold and books the guest, or is refunded when the seats are gone (`onSuccess` in `packages/domain-booking/src/finalize.ts`). A failure on a closed checkout marks the payment failed (`onFailure`).
  - The webhook processes a recorded event that is still unprocessed when it arrives again (`handleVerifiedEvent`). The checkout sweep processes one left past its 60 s grace, on the 15-minute cron. Only the staff test payment and the fake's own routes deliver fake events.
- **Finding 1: the canceled outcome's Check again (entries 1 and 4).** Root cause: Check again only read the checkout, and the one resend after a cancel ignored its answer unless it was `checkout_not_open`. A settled success whose delivery was lost twice (a webhook failure before the inbox write, a 503 from the fake's storage, or a request that never ran) was never delivered, refunded, or raised, while the outcome promised all three. A delivered decline still read "may have gone through". Fix: while the payment is unknown on a canceled checkout, Check again sends the payment's own request again on its key, then reads. The cancel's resend takes the same path. A delivered decline, a refusal as not open, or a read whose payment state is failed clears the doubt, so the outcome says nothing was charged. A delivery other than 200 shows the delivery problem, whose Try again on the outcome is the same resend. The resend's other problems show only while the checkout reads canceled with its payment pending. The canceled copy is unchanged and now holds: a webhook failure after the inbox write heals through the sweep within about 16 minutes, and the resend processes it at once.
- **Finding 2: Try again refused as not open (entries 2 and 3).** Root cause: `pay()` showed the checkout on `checkout_not_open` without clearing the doubt, unlike the cancel path, so the hold-ended outcome said the payment may have gone through and offered only Check again. Fix: that refusal, on the payment's own key, clears it, so the outcome says nothing was charged and offers Start a new booking.
- **The hold-ended outcome while the payment is unknown.** After finding 2's fix, the page reaches it only when staff cancel and the cancel is refused because the sweep had already expired the checkout. Its copy promised a refund, but a late success there books the guest if the seats are still free. It now says that, offers Back to calendar beside Check again, and its Check again only reads: delivering a success there would book against the cancel staff asked for. The coordinator's proposal also resent on this outcome; the owner decided against it (below).
- **Carried from pull request 45's final check (test only).** After `quote_expired`, Change details, then Price again on the party step lands on The guest with the booker's name kept, both attestations unchecked on Review, and Hold sends the new quote's id.
- **Tests.** Nine new jsdom cases in `NewBookingPage.test.tsx` and one changed (the canceled outcome's Check again now answers a third payment request). On `main`'s page, the eight new cases for the two findings failed for the stated reasons: the not-open retry kept the "may have gone through" copy and no Start a new booking; Check again sent no request, so the canceled checkout never read as refunded; no delivery problem showed after the cancel's resend, with or without a read after it; a delivered decline and a read showing the payment failed both kept "may have gone through"; and the hold-ended copy still promised the refund. The requote case passed on `main` by design and failed when `requote()` priced with `priceParty(2)`, landing on Review. Nine more mutations of the fix each failed at least one new case: keeping the doubt on not open, Check again only reading, ignoring the delivery status, keeping the doubt after a delivered decline or a failed payment state, resending on the hold-ended outcome, showing the resend's problem whatever the read, a Try again on the outcome that calls `pay()`, and dropping the problem when the read fails. The 55 cases in the file pass.
- **Race harness** (a 2 ms busy-wait after every `setTimeout(fn, 0)`, in an uncommitted temporary config): the nine new cases 5 of 5 runs; the whole file 5 of 5 (55 each); the new cases with the changed one and 0 to 5 ms of fetch jitter 5 of 5. Setup took 11 to 18 ms with the harness and 0 ms without, so it was loaded.
- **Local:** `pnpm check` green before push.
- **CI on the first round**, run [40961906552](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40961906552) on `8c6f041`: checks, integration, the guest browser job, and the console browser job, all green.
- **Owner decision, 2026-10-29.** On the hold-ended outcome after a refused cancel, with the payment unknown, Check again does not redeliver the payment; it only reads. The cost is recorded: a fake success that never reached the inbox stays unrefunded there, with no exception. The decision covers an unanswered Decline too: nothing was charged, and the decline may stay unrecorded on that outcome.
- **Adversarial review of `1cefbaa`**: no must-fix and nothing on money safety. Five should-fix items, fixed in the same pull request, each with a test:
  - copy-1: after an unanswered Decline, only a decline can have settled (Take test payment is unavailable while a payment is unknown, and the fake settles once), and a decline never charges, books, refunds, or raises an exception. The cancel dialog and the canceled and hold-ended outcomes promised a refund, an exception, or a booking. Every uncertain copy now follows the outcome asked for: after a decline it says nothing was charged, and Check again (on the canceled outcome, a resend) still records the decline. The hold-ended outcome after a decline also offers Start a new booking.
  - copy-2: the resend returned the delivery problem before it looked at the event, so a known decline still read "may have gone through" and "refunded in full". The event comes first now: a decline clears the doubt, delivered or not, and an undelivered one shows "The decline didn't reach TideGrid. Nothing was charged." with Try again to record it.
  - S2: Check again dropped a failed read, so on the hold-ended outcome it did nothing visible, even signed out. A failed read now shows its problem: the sign-in notice when signed out, otherwise "The payment couldn't be checked".
  - T1: nothing covered the cancel resend's not-open clear. The UI-3 and UI-4 case now asserts that the outcome says nothing was charged, offers Start a new booking, and has no Check again.
  - U1: the outcome step showed two primary buttons for one action, the problem's Try again and the outcome's Check again. Where the outcome offers Check again, the problem above it now offers no second control. The delivery-problem cases assert one primary button.
  - The nits: the outcome's delivery notice no longer says "nothing is decided yet" under "Booking canceled", and a resend or read failure there is titled "The payment couldn't be checked", not "may not have gone through" above "may have gone through".
  - Evidence: on `1cefbaa`'s page and model, the five new cases and three of the changed ones failed for those reasons. The UI-3 and UI-4 case passed there by design and fails when the cancel resend's not-open clear is removed. Six mutations of the new code (that clear; delivery before the event; the second control; the dropped read failure; the dialog and the outcome ignoring a decline) and seven of the first round's, on the new code, each failed at least one case. 60 cases in the file pass. Race harness, as before: the follow-up cases with the changed ones (16) 5 of 5 runs, the whole file 5 of 5 (60 each), and the 16 with 0 to 5 ms of fetch jitter 5 of 5.
- **Final check of `1416e40`**: no must-fix; the money copy was true everywhere it rendered. Four should-fix items, fixed in the same pull request:
  - F1: after a decline whose delivery failed, a Try again that then got a 503 from the provider or no answer only read the checkout, which cleared the notice and left no way to record the decline. On the canceled outcome, a problem's Try again now always sends the payment's request again; `checkAgain` still resends only on a canceled checkout, where nothing settles.
  - F2: a signed-out problem on the outcome showed Sign in again beside Check again, which could only meet the same 401. Check again waits while the page is signed out.
  - T-1: the provider-timeout copy for a check on the canceled outcome is pinned in `model.test.ts` and on the page ("The payment provider didn't answer", nothing about held seats, one primary button).
  - The nits: a read that fails after the cancel's own resend now says so, with Try again, and the canceled outcome's copy after an unanswered Decline is pinned.
  - Evidence: on `1416e40`'s page and model, the three new cases and two changed ones failed (no fourth payment request on the same key, two primary buttons while signed out, no notice for the cancel's failed read). The T-1 cases passed there by design and fail without that branch; the copy pin fails when the copy changes. Each of those mutations failed at least one case, and the thirteen earlier mutations still do. 64 cases in the file pass. Race harness: the nine new and changed cases 5 of 5 runs, the whole file 5 of 5 (64 each), and the changed cases and the follow-up block with 0 to 5 ms of fetch jitter 5 of 5 each. Two of the new cases first waited only for a request to be sent, which fetch jitter exposed; they now wait for its answer.
  - `pnpm check` ran green locally on this round's code.
- **CI on the final code**, run [40969650052](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40969650052) on `8cc1322`, after the final check's fixes: checks, integration, the guest browser job, and the console browser job, all green.
- **Not proven, or open:**
  - The accepted R5 race: a payment request still settling when the resend is refused as not open later reads as refunded, while the page has already said nothing was charged.
  - Two staff sessions on one checkout: when another session settled the other outcome, this page's request on that outcome is new, and a closed checkout refuses it as not open, which the page reads as nothing settled.
  - After an unanswered Take test payment, the cancel dialog promises a refund. When the cancel is refused because the hold had already ended, a late success books instead; the outcome then says so.
  - When Check again changes the outcome, its button goes and focus is not moved to the heading, as before this change.

### Console search latency verification, October 29, 2026

- **Built** as a follow-up of standing goal item 5, for the owner's target of 2026-10-29 (D-E8: 300 ms p95 for a whole request on a tenant of about 50,000 bookings, common fragments included), in pull request 41 on branch `build/search-latency` from `main` at `b6d0247`, merged forward to `efdbfcb`, `e1991a6` (pull request 42), and `7e5f6af`. No index, extension, or migration. No Neon, deploy, or install. The owner decided on 2026-10-29 to merge it when green, not after the Nov 1 deploy.
- **What changed.** `searchBookings` (`packages/domain-booking/src/search.ts`) is one statement instead of a cursor check, a count, and a page. It collects the matches once (`m`, materialized), counts them, checks the cursor among them, and orders and limits them to the page. Then, for each page row, it reads the booking by its id in one LATERAL subquery, and that booking's trip, product, boat, order, payment, latest refund, and booker from the booking's keys in a second. Both are fenced with OFFSET 0, so each runs once per page row, at most limit + 1 times; how each table is read inside is the planner's choice. The text condition, the cursor position, the order, and the contract are unchanged. Finance's statement never names the checkout. Details: the [booking README](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#deferred-and-known-gaps).
- **Decisions.** The goal packet's coordinator defaults: S1 A with escalation to S1 B, S2 A (no index), S3 A (equality and plan shape gate normal CI; the 300 ms assertion lives only in the off-by-default benchmark), S4 A, S5 A, S9 A. S8 A (merge after Nov 1) was replaced by the owner's decision above. S1 A met 300 ms but not the packet's 225 ms headroom, so S1 B was taken.
- **Runs, in order, on CI PostgreSQL 17, not Neon:**
  - [40893467723](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40893467723) on `0604a3c`: the extended gate and the benchmark against the b6d0247 shape on both sides, the before baseline. The benchmark passed; one gate case was red on a test bug (the synthetic confirmation tie named a booking on another trip), fixed in `b1cc9f7`.
  - [40898413073](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40898413073) on `b1cc9f7`: S1 A, the matching ids ordered and limited in a subquery, then the details joined. Green, every case under 300 ms, but tenant B over 225 ms: "@g" 267.5, every day 259.6, and "qj" 235.2 ms p95. Its LIMIT let the custom plan sort every booking and probe each checkout in order, hoping to stop early (about 100 ms for a fragment that matches nothing), and with 1,265 trips the planner joined the page's rows to every trip before probing bookings (a Materialize of 50,001 rows read 26 times).
  - [40902374643](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40902374643) on `f65c1ab`: S1 B without the fence. Red on the benchmark's detail-row bound only: the planner merged the LATERAL detail subquery into the outer joins and read 50,006 rows of each detail table (1.3 million with a cursor under a generic plan). Every outcome equaled the b6d0247 search's.
  - [40910950938](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40910950938) on `0b14c59`, attempts 1 and 2: S1 B with OFFSET 0. Green on every job both times. These are the two benchmark runs below.
  - [40936200469](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40936200469) on `1354c05`: the booking by id and its details in two fenced subqueries (`44c26cb`, after the final check), with the throwaway flip. Green on every job: the benchmark's equality, plan-mode, detail-row, and 300 ms checks all held. Run 3 below.
- **Benchmark.** `search-benchmark.integration.test.ts`, on only through THROWAWAY commits (`0604a3c`, then `1354c05` for run 3), each reverted. Tenant A: 50,006 bookings, 50,000 of them cloned onto 40 of its 85 trips and five synthetic ones on its last trip; tenant B: 50,001 over its 1,265 trips, loaded after tenant A's series. Per case and plan mode (`plan_cache_mode` forced to custom, then generic): three warm-ups of each shape, then 20 requests of the b6d0247 search ("before") and 60 of today's ("after"), interleaved one before to three after, on one connection. Whole requests in a snapshot, as the runtime, timed around the transaction. p95 is nearest rank; ms. `pg_prepared_statements` showed only custom plans in every custom series and only generic plans in every generic series. No after plan used JIT. Every orders, payments, and refunds scan of the after shape read at most limit + 1 (26) rows. The runner: 4 CPUs, PostgreSQL 17.11, `shared_buffers` 128MB, `work_mem` 4MB, `jit` on.

- **Result.** Today's statement, run 3, worse plan mode, p95. A first page or page two at 20,000 or 50,000 matches: before 489.5 to 1,169.8 ms, after at most 131.3 ms (tenant B, every day, generic). A page ten rows from the end of 20,000 matches, which the cursor already shortened before: 147.9 ms before, 112 ms after. 2,500 matches or fewer: 175.9 to 242.8 ms before, at most 91.4 ms after. Finance: up to 669.4 ms before, at most 33.3 ms after. No after request took longer than 138.5 ms. The one-subquery statement before it, runs 1 and 2: a first page or page two before 438.8 to 913.2 and 416.7 to 1,110.9 ms, after at most 128.9 and 122 ms; the deep page 147.7 and 143.2 ms before, 112.6 and 109.7 ms after; 2,500 or fewer 183.6 to 236.3 and 170 to 225.8 ms before, at most 85.3 and 84.6 ms after; finance up to 618.1 and 578.4 ms before, at most 37 and 32.6 ms after; no after request over 146.2 ms. Every after-p95 in all three runs is under the 225 ms headroom and the 300 ms target, so the benchmark asserts the target for the after shape; it stays off by default and never gates normal CI.
- **Equality.** The benchmark compared every one of its requests, before and after, with the first outcome of its case: equal in every case, all three runs, including data loaded with foreign keys off. The result gate (`search.integration.test.ts`, always on) compares whole outcomes of today's search and the b6d0247 search kept literally in `search-baseline.testing.ts`, in one snapshot: every text arm, a reference with name words, escaping and punctuation, references typed every way, dates alone and with text (a range with no trips and one that ends before it starts among them), limits 1, 2, 25, and 100, paging to the end for both roles at limits 1 and 2, a cursor at the last row, five kinds of refused cursor, the canceled booking with its refund, a staff-assisted booking, a charter tied on departure with a shared trip, and two bookings tied on confirmation (one synthetic update, throwaway database only). Its id-level checks against the literal reference condition are kept. All 10 cases passed on the final code.
- **Plan shape.** As `tidegrid_app` under forced row-level security, custom (bound values) and generic (PREPARE and EXECUTE), for both roles, with and without a cursor: one statement; the CTE that collects the matches reads only bookings, trips, and for staff the checkout, with no detail alias inside it or under the Limit that picks the page, and no matching scan outside it. Finance's statement never names `checkout_sessions`, and every join names its tenant. Asserted on shape only, never timing.
- **Executed plan, always on (reviews T1 and the final check).** The plan test runs the statement with `explain (analyze, timing off)` under both plan modes, for both roles, with and without a cursor. It asserts what the fences guarantee: one scan of the page's bookings (`b1`) and one subtree for their details, each run between once and limit + 1 times, the details' subtree holding neither `b1` nor the matches; and both fences in the statement's text. Its `beforeAll` analyzes the tables, so the result does not depend on autovacuum. History:
  - With one fenced subquery for the booking and its details (`0b14c59`), a first check counted `b1` scans. A reviewer then showed it failing on that correct statement once the tables had statistics: the planner drove the subquery from the trips and scanned `b1` once per trip per page row (87 scans for a page of 2). So 87 scans was not a sign of a missing fence. The booking is now read by id in a fenced subquery of its own, and its details in a second.
  - Proved on a local PostgreSQL 17.11 container, the search file alone on a fresh database: with both fences and the ANALYZE, 10 of 10 pass; with both fences and no ANALYZE, 10 of 10 pass; with both fences removed, the plan test fails on the fence text. The full domain-booking suite passes too (20 files, 160 tests, the benchmark skipped), B0 included: its admin control now explains the name-only query, so it no longer depends on how many rows other files left in `checkout_sessions`.
  - What the executed check cannot do on tables this small: with both fences removed and the text assertions disabled, it passed, with and without the ANALYZE. A reviewer's plans show why. Without its fence the booking's subquery (`pb`) flattens into the outer join. The details' subquery (`d`) cannot be merged even without its fence: its order and payment joins name `pb` in their ON conditions under its own lateral refund join, and PostgreSQL does not pull up such a subquery. So `d` still runs once per page row, and the executed check passes. Its fence guards against a later edit that makes `d` mergeable. The fence text is what catches a removed fence. Run [40902374643](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40902374643) (every booking's details read, 1.3 million rows under a generic cursor plan) shows what an unfenced, mergeable detail subquery did, in the one-subquery shape of `f65c1ab`; it is not a measurement of today's statement without its fences.
  - Other details are logged, not bounded: on the gate's small tables the planner may hash the checkout, order, or payment table once and reuse it for every page row, while at 50,000 bookings the benchmark read at most 26 rows of each.
- **CI:** runs 1 and 2 are on `0b14c59`, run 3 on `1354c05`. The final head, with the throwaway flip reverted, is green in the run linked from pull request 41, which records its head and run id.
- **Not proven:**
  - Neon: no Neon run before Nov 1. The benchmark times the domain transaction (begin, the leaked-context check, the context, the statement, commit) on one reused connection, not the API's principal lookup or membership check. An API search runs 7 statements now (the principal lookup outside the transaction, then begin, the leaked-context check, the context, the membership load, the search, commit), down from 8 or 9. In wire round trips it is more: `getDb` (`apps/api-worker/src/db.ts`) makes a new postgres.js instance per request, so a request pays the type fetch, and a Parse and Describe before Bind and Execute for each of the 4 parameterized statements (the principal lookup, the context, the membership load, the search). That is at least 12 wire round trips now, 14 or 16 before, plus connection startup; the benchmark's reused connection pays neither. At the worst measured p95 of 131.3 ms (run 3, today's statement), the 300 ms target leaves about 169 ms, about 14 ms a wire round trip; the packet's 225 ms aim leaves about 94 ms, about 8 ms a round trip. Inferred, not measured;
  - growth past 50,000 bookings: one matching pass a request, linear in the tenant's bookings (inferred);
  - the 300 ms assertion runs only when the benchmark is on;
  - tenants with more than one checkout per booking. Abandoned checkouts keep their booker's details, and staff's matching pass reads every checkout. The benchmark has one checkout per booking. In a reviewer's local run with three checkouts per booking, custom p95 rose to 57.6 ms ("@g"), 34.0 ms (every day), and 85.1 ms (a six-word name), still far under the target; not a CI run.
- **Follow-ups, flagged, not fixed:**
  - the benchmark on a throwaway Neon branch after Nov 1, with `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`;
  - the day list and day report share the order but were not measured at pilot volume;
  - the lateral refund's ordering is redundant under one refund per payment; a plain left join is simpler.

- **Built** as standing goal item 5, under the owner's standing goal of 2026-10-28 and the owner's request of 2026-10-29, "keep going, hardening parts D and E, merge when green". No Neon, deploy, install, or live provider call was made.
  - Part A, pull request 32, merged as `e93f0aa`: `runFairSweep`, migration `0013_sweep_fairness.sql`, and the hold and checkout sweeps on the scheduler.
  - Part B, pull request 33, merged as `894839e`: pg_trgm deferred under D4 B; search unchanged.

Its merge SHA goes in the next entry, or in the Nov 1 runbook's evidence pull request.
- **Decisions.** Coordinator defaults, each pending owner confirmation: D1 A, D2 A, D3 A, D4 B, D5 A, D6 A, D7 A, D8 C, and D9 B (2026-10-28), and D-E1 to D-E6 and D-E9 to D-E12 (2026-10-29). Owner decisions of 2026-10-29: D-E7 keeps the 20 s email budget, and D-E8 sets a console search target of 300 ms p95 for a tenant with about 50,000 bookings, common fragments included. All are in the [decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#decision-log) and the [demo build plan](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence).
- **Failing-first record.** A red run records only the first failing package, because `pnpm -r` stops there (root `package.json`). Behavioral means the assertion failed on the old behavior itself; nominal means it failed on a missing option or field.
  - Part A, behavioral: run [40755745908](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40755745908), the hold list order (received `[low, high]`, as the old loop wrote no stamps); run [40756715324](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40756715324), the booking expiry, inbox, and refund write order (the hog's items first). Nominal: the hold adversarial pair (no `onTurnStart`), the hold zero-budget test (no `turns`), and the booking poison and at-most-once tests (no `checkoutSweepStore`), with the reasoning in pull request 32.
  - Part C, behavioral: run [40767816390](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40767816390): the victim's mail went out 13th, not 6th; the list order did not change; `complete` was true under a 5 s budget with a 10 s send bound.
  - Part D: run [40868382975](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40868382975) on `eaa0577`, the tests alone on the old loop. Integration went red at domain-hostnames, where `pnpm -r` stopped, so the later packages' suites did not run; `lifecycle.integration.test.ts` stayed green. The `checks` job was red too, on typecheck: the tests pass options and read fields the old sweep lacks. Each case, with its first failing assertion:
    - behavioral, the adversarial pair's read order: the victim's hostname was read 13th (index 12), not 6th;
    - behavioral, the pair's list order on the next run: `[hog, victim]`, not `[victim, hog]`;
    - behavioral, a declined stamp after a throttle, an account fault, and the call limit (three cases): the list stayed `[hog, victim]`;
    - behavioral, the poison read turn: the list stayed `[poison, other]`;
    - behavioral, the account fault in pass 2: the victim read none of its hostnames, not 5;
    - behavioral on stamp rows, the throttle in pass 2: no stamps, not `[victim, hog]`, after its expiry and read assertions passed;
    - behavioral on stamp rows, the call limit at the end of a turn: no stamp for the hog, after `checked` 5 and `complete` false passed;
    - nominal, the pair's turns: no `onTurnStart` call;
    - nominal after pins that passed: the per-tenant cap (`checked` 6, `complete` false, one row still due), the zero budget, `complete` true, and the budget cut inside a turn (with its second run), each then failing on `onTurnStart` or a new report field;

Behavioral: a failed stamp logged `hold_sweep_tenant_failed`, `email_sweep_tenant_failed`, and `hostname_sweep_tenant_failed`. Nominal: the summaries lacked the new counts, and the email summary lacked `account_fault`. The 26 cases of `lifecycle.integration.test.ts`, its 16 sweep cases among them, and the hostname CLI's suite pass unedited. The benchmark, run [40765472082](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40765472082), on one tenant with 50,000 bookings: about 320 ms (max 355 ms) at about 20,000 matches, about 175 ms at 2,000, and 120 to 160 ms with none. The count took 55 to 75 ms; the page statement dominates. That is above the owner's 300 ms p95 target (D-E8), so the search follow-up, ordering and limiting the matching ids before the joins, is now required work, in a goal of its own. **B1 was dropped:** in run [40764463348](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40764463348) the arms rewrite's page statement passed a 90 s statement timeout at 20,000 matches.

- **Closes review F8** of the [G2.11c entry of 2026-10-20](#g211c-guest-self-service-and-the-email-shim-verification-october-20-2026): the email sweep visited tenants by id with a fixed limit and budget. That entry is not edited.
- **Not proven:**

  - one exact-name lookup can pass the call cap (D-E12);

  - the search page statement, now required work under D-E8;
  - the doc comments in `fair-sweep.ts`, `delivery.ts`, and the hold and checkout sweeps say a turn always runs after a failed stamp; at the budget edge it does not (the scheduler re-checks the budget after the stamp). The hostname sweep's comments say so; the others are out of this pull request's paths;
  - an integration case where a handled hostname stays due within the run, which is what the attempted-set exclusion guards;
  - the Neon checks after Nov 1, on a throwaway branch with `TIDEGRID_EPHEMERAL_DB=1`.

## 2026-10-28

### G2.16a Cloudflare Email Service adapter verification, October 28, 2026

- Built on branch `build/email-adapter` from `main` at `a6f07d5`, merged forward to `6d37795` (#29) and `ca943a7` (#28), as G2.16a, the first part of G2.16. Draft pull request 30 runs CI on every push. No Cloudflare API call, no email send, no account or dashboard change, no deploy, no Neon, and no install were made. **The adapter has never called the real service**, as the hostname client had not before G2.14b-live.
- **Coordinator defaults, 2026-10-28, each pending owner confirmation:** D1 B (at most once on a provider that does not deduplicate; `E_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` and `E_DELIVERY_FAILED` are unknown outcomes), D2 B (an account-level fault stops the run and the sweep; since the review, a rate limit is not one), D3 B (the recipient allowlist is the `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` secret; the binding carries `allowed_sender_addresses` only), D4 A (the allowlist decides the queued backlog at switch-on), D5 A (the `X-TideGrid-Message-Id` header), D6 A (the email fake only in local development), D7 A (specific failure codes), D8 (the defensive security reviewer runs). They are recorded in the [email adapter narrowing](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence) and the [decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#decision-log), added after pull request 28 merged, with the contract's `failureCode` description and a regenerated `openapi.json`.
- **What was built:**
  - `packages/domain-messaging`: the Cloudflare adapter over the Workers `send_email` binding with the structured builder; all 21 documented binding codes mapped by name; provider ids normalized to the message table's pattern; a recipient fence required at construction; a 10 s send bound with late answers logged by code or normalized provider id only; control characters and non-address fields refused before the provider; a recorded-shape fixture of the binding; a provider contract shared with the fake. The port gains optional `deduplicates` and `sendTimeoutMs`, and `outcome` and `scope` on `EmailProviderUnavailableError`. Delivery is at most once on a provider that does not say `deduplicates: true`, stops on an account-level fault, and honors a `deadline`. The contract is in the [messaging README](../packages/domain-messaging/README.md#the-cloudflare-adapter-g216a).
  - The API: `EMAIL_PROVIDER=cloudflare` needs the `EMAIL` binding (else off, `binding_missing`) and, outside production, a non-empty parsed allowlist (else off, `allowlist_missing`; an entry that is not one address is refused, `allowlist_invalid`). A sender (`EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS`) that is not one address is refused too, `from_address_invalid`, so a typo turns email off instead of failing every message as `invalid_message` (from the final check of `d3367c5`, with a test that failed first). The fake is refused outside local development and beside a binding. After-commit delivery admits Cloudflare and passes an 18 s deadline. The typecheck proves the generated `SendEmail` type fits the adapter's binding type, and a drifted builder would not. Deployed config gains a comment only: no binding, no `EMAIL_PROVIDER`.
- **Unit tests** (`pnpm check`, green, after the review fixes): messaging 69 (the adapter 60: the exact builder and headers, every one of the 21 codes by name with no personal data in the port error or the logs, unknown codes and non-Error throws, a synchronous throw, a timeout, a late rejection with no unhandled rejection, a late acceptance logged by its normalized id, control characters refused, id normalization and a missing id, the fence with case and whitespace, construction refusals, the fixture's header, sender and size checks, and the contract suite; no `fetch` call and no console line with personal data in any case); the API 89 in workerd, 12 of them the settings and selection matrix. A mutation that copied the provider's error text into the port error, and one that dropped the fence, each failed 16 cases.
- **Integration**, on CI's PostgreSQL 17 container: `packages/domain-messaging/src/cloudflare.integration.test.ts` (14 cases, two tenants and one of its own for each stop case: the provider and its id recorded, a normalized id stored, a suppressed recipient failed at once, a rate limit retried with a backoff, a fenced recipient failed with no binding call, no claim of another tenant's message, an ambiguous error, a timeout, an unexpected throw and a lapsed lease each failed as `outcome_unknown` with one binding call, the fake's path unchanged, an account-level fault stopping the run and the sweep, and the deadline); `src/fake.contract.integration.test.ts` (the contract over the fake, 5 cases); and `packages/domain-booking/src/adversarial/cloudflare-email.integration.test.ts` (5 cases: the confirmation's link filled into the HTML and the text and redeemed; a confirmation, a manage-link email, and a cancellation notice each failed once as `outcome_unknown`; a guest's new link working after a lost confirmation; a lapsed lease leaving one copy and one live link). Every existing suite passed unchanged.
- **Failed first.** On the pre-change delivery code the new delivery cases failed as they should: CI run [40743365967](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40743365967) on `7140620` (8 delivery cases in domain-messaging; two more failures there were test bugs, fixed before the change), and run [40744232133](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40744232133) on `f430fb0`, with that suite skipped so the booking suite could run (4 cases; the lapsed-lease case had a test bug too, fixed before the change). The link-fill case passed there, as it should, since delivery's fill is unchanged.

Fixed, each behavior change with a test that failed first (5 unit cases locally and in CI run [40749539901](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40749539901) on `5daadbc`, and 3 cases in `packages/domain-booking/src/adversarial/cloudflare-email-review.integration.test.ts` in the same run):

The [email lifecycle](https://developers.cloudflare.com/email-service/concepts/email-lifecycle/) page says the limit stage "checks sending limits per account, domain, and recipient" under that one code. D2 B holds for the daily limit, the sender, and a suspended account.

  - F4 and S1: the Workers API page gives `E_DELIVERY_FAILED` the description "Could not deliver the email" and the common causes "SMTP delivery failure, recipient server rejection". A recipient server's rejection points toward Rejected; the mapping stays ambiguous, the side that never sends twice, now quoted in full in the code, the README, and docs/v2/12 and 06.

This entry was then updated in a docs-only commit.
- **Open gaps** (recorded in the [messaging README](../packages/domain-messaging/README.md#known-gaps)):
  - Idempotency: Cloudflare documents none, so D1 B trades duplicates for a missed email. Recovery is a guest's new link, a waiver's audited resend, or staff resend (G2.16b).
  - Post-acceptance bounces and complaints are not ingested, so `sent` means accepted. This is a Core live gate blocker under "silent message failure", not only a follow-up.
  - Provider revalidation for US customer data, deletion, export and audit is open; Cloudflare keeps recipient addresses in its suppression list and Activity log.
  - The `messageId` format (and whether `send()` returns the id events carry), the error wording, and which code a refused sender gets are unverified against live Cloudflare.
  - Under D1 B an `E_DELIVERY_FAILED` that is really a recipient server's rejection ends `outcome_unknown`, not a specific refusal code; the owner confirms D1 knowing the page's common causes.
