### Stripe adapter (Stripe-local) verification, October 28, 2026

- Built on branch `build/stripe-adapter` from `main` at `a6f07d5`, merged forward to `6d37795` (#29) and `ca943a7` (#28), as standing goal item 3. Draft pull request 31 runs CI on every push. **Recorded-shape fixtures; no live Stripe call made.** No Stripe account, CLI, SDK, sandbox, install, deploy, or Neon was used. The adapter is off in every deployed configuration.
- **Coordinator defaults, 2026-10-28, each pending owner confirmation:** D1 (a) to D12 (a), recorded in the [Stripe adapter narrowing](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence) and the [decision register](v2/06-decision-register.md#decision-log).
- **What was built:**
  - `packages/domain-payments/src/stripe.ts`: a Connect direct-charge adapter behind `PaymentProvider` over plain `fetch`. Card-only PaymentIntents with the port's idempotency key; `Stripe-Account` and the pinned `Stripe-Version` on every call; refunds looked up by key (five pages of 100) before they are created; a strict snapshot-event parse after `verifySignature`; intents without TideGrid's metadata recorded as `other`; a canonical projection hash for redeliveries. Live keys refused everywhere, test keys refused in production, and the fixture's credentials refused by every deployment check.
  - `stripe-fixture.ts`, exported only as `@tidegrid/domain-payments/testing`; `payment-provider.contract.ts`, run against Stripe (unit) and the fake (integration); `provenance.test.ts` (brand minting, imports into the package, runtime imports of the fixture); `stripe-fixture-hygiene.test.ts` (the capture scrub guard).
  - `apps/api-worker`: `PaymentSettings` gains the Stripe branch through `checkStripeConfig`; `paymentProvider()` builds Stripe with no fetch and no API base; the payment block of `env.ts` and `.dev.vars.example` (placeholders that fail the key checks); the webhook route's OpenAPI description, with `openapi.json` regenerated. `wrangler.jsonc` is unchanged.
- **Phase A review (independent, security, and fixture fidelity, three skeptics per finding):** 0 must-fix, 2 should-fix, 3 refuted, 10 notes. Fixed, each with a failing-first test (12 cases failed before the fix): Stripe's documented key, platform, and account codes, and six "not now" codes, are unknown outcomes (F1); any refused refund lookup is unknown, never final (N3); the import guard resolves relative paths inside the package (SEC-1); the brand guard catches cast-free forgeries that name the type, and documents its limit (SEC-2); fixture-shaped credentials (SEC-3); shipping and guest-identity fields in the scrub guard (SEC-4); the fixture's cached validation errors, account behavior, and failed charge after a decline (F3, F4, F5); the README's Phase B wording (N5). Correction: the package README's text for these fixes, N5 included, did not apply in `1a74810`, whose message says it did; it landed with the Phase B docs commit.
- **Checks:** `pnpm check` green locally. `pnpm contracts:generate` shows no drift after the description change. `@tidegrid/domain-payments` unit tests 76 passed; `@tidegrid/api-worker` unit tests 84 passed, `test/stripe-settings.test.ts` among them (every settings branch, the `.dev.vars.example` placeholders refused, the fake controls and the staff test payment 404 under Stripe, the webhook verified before the database). Every Stripe unit test replaces the global `fetch` with one that fails if reached. 23 hand mutations of `stripe.ts` (lookup skipped, 401 or 403 Rejected, config codes dropped, livemode, raw-body hash, metadata check, canceled amount, pending as succeeded, page bound, missing headers, production allowed, clamp, `cause` kept, an extra mint, and others) each fail the suite.
- **CI:** run [40750202625](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40750202625) on `03c2449`: checks, integration, browser, and console-browser green. Integration on PostgreSQL 17: `fake.contract.integration.test.ts` 6 passed; `stripe-webhook.integration.test.ts` 7 passed (confirm only from a signed success; a redelivery with changed `pending_webhooks` recorded once with no `payload_mismatch` log; decline then success; a canceled intent released with no `payment_mismatch`; tenant B's account naming A's intent changes nothing for A; a cancellation refund through `settleRefund`; a pending refund left requested, then settled by `sweepCheckouts`; a 401 on the refund leaves it requested with no refund-failed email). The API's integration suites: 20 files, 252 passed. The existing suites stay on the fake. Earlier runs: [40744507614](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40744507614) (Phase A) and [40748684094](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40748684094) (review fixes).
- **Phase B review (independent on the stronger configuration and security, three skeptics):** accept, 0 confirmed, 0 refuted, notes only. Applied after merging `main` at `a5cdb40` (#30, the email adapter; `env.ts` and the demo plan's owner-input rows kept both goals' blocks), each with a failing-first test (4 failed before the fix): a test key and the fake run only when `ENVIRONMENT` is `local`, `preview`, or `staging`, so an unset or misspelled value is refused as `environment_unknown` in `checkStripeConfig`, the Stripe constructor, `paymentSettings`, and the fake's constructor (SECB-2); test keys and webhook secrets that pass the deployment checks are random per run, and the hygiene guard now fails on any test source in this package or the API's tests that spells one out, split into literals or not (SECB-1); `stripe-fixture-hygiene.test.ts` and `stripe-settings.test.ts` now fail if the global `fetch` is reached, so the claim above holds for every Stripe unit test (SECB-3); the D3 risk names a refund lookup refused for good beside a pending refund, and the register names the six transient codes (B-N1, B-N3). The API Worker README waits for pull request 26 (B-N2).
- **Not proven:** anything against real Stripe. The fixture bodies are modeled on the API reference and unverified against live; the capture step after the owner's approval replaces them. The guest cannot pay through Stripe in a browser until the G2.11 Stripe Element. Recorded risks for Stripe-live are in the [package README](../packages/domain-payments/README.md#the-stripe-adapter-proposed-off-by-default): a create replay after key pruning, a cached 500, pending refunds at the front of the sweep, inline webhook processing, signature-only authentication, the restricted key's `Stripe-Account` permission, and metadata cleared in the Dashboard.

### G2.14b Hostnames and PWA local-first verification, October 28, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.14b-hostnames-and-pwa` from `main` at `c0b540a`, merged forward to `eaaaf11` (the Node pin, zod, and vite updates), as the local-first pass of G2.14b. Draft pull request 25 runs CI on every push. The scope and the coordinator defaults behind it, each pending owner confirmation, are the [hostnames and PWA narrowing](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence) in the demo build plan. No deploy, no Neon, no Cloudflare API call, and no DNS change were made.
- **What was built:**
  - Migration `0011_custom_hostname_lifecycle.sql`: `tenant_hostname_provisioning` (forced row-level security, runtime SELECT only, no DELETE or TRUNCATE for any role), transition guards on `tenant_hostnames`, the `platform` kind for an allowlist, `app.resolve_hostname` with a `verified_at` guard, and six hostname functions, two of them runtime-callable (`hostname_sweep_tenants`, `hostname_record_check`). The contract is in the [database README](../packages/database/README.md#custom-hostname-lifecycle-g214b).
  - `packages/domain-hostnames`: the strict origin parser, the provider port, the exhaustive Cloudflare status mapping, the scripted fake, the Cloudflare for SaaS client over recorded fixtures with one contract suite for both, and the lifecycle service ([README](../packages/domain-hostnames/README.md)). `tools/hostnames`: the admin CLI ([README](../tools/hostnames/README.md)).
  - The API: one resolver for every public route, the bootstrap included, on the strict parser; verified-origin CORS with the bootstrap exception, a 30-second in-isolate projection shared by preflights and simple requests behind a per-address limiter (a just-disabled host can keep its CORS grant in one isolate for up to 30 seconds, while its data routes answer 404 at once), and `PUBLIC_CORS=static`, or unset, as the deployed default and the rollback; the `GuestGateway` RPC entrypoint; provider selection that admits the fake only in local development; the hostname sweep as the cron's fourth job ([API README](../apps/api-worker/README.md)). Email link origins go through the same parser with the environment.
  - The guest site: an edge Worker for the per-tenant manifest, composed SVG icons, and `sw.js`, with the kill switch only on a definite miss and 503 with nothing cleared on a gateway failure; the service worker with its cache rules and a neutral offline page; the page's half of the kill switch; `worker-src` and `manifest-src 'self'`; `/support`, `/offline`, `/terms`, and `/privacy`; and the preview routes in config, not deployed ([guest README](../apps/guest-site/README.md#the-installable-site-g214b)).
- **Unit tests** (`pnpm check`, green, after the review fixes): domain-hostnames 145 (the parser under every environment, every Cloudflare status value, the contract suite against the fake and the fixture client, the client's request shapes and token handling); tenant-config 30 (reserved slugs and the name rules, label-boundary suffixes included); the API 76 in workerd (provider refusal under `preview`, `staging`, and `production` and beside a zone, the preflight projection's cap and lifetime, public CORS with no database, floods of distinct origins, preflight and simple, limited per address before any lookup, the static default when `PUBLIC_CORS` is unset, the sweep's refusal logs, and `GuestGateway`'s single method and its own parsing); the guest site 895 in Node and jsdom (service worker routing, caching, and update policy, the kill-switch rollback from build N to N+1, the precache list, support pages, registration, and static pins) and 16 in workerd for its Worker (manifest per brand, 404 and 503 and their headers, spoofed `Host` and `X-Forwarded-Host` ignored, the kill switch only on a miss, icons under their policy, the neutral fallbacks); the CLI 3; messaging 8 (the strict default when no environment is named).
- **Integration**, on CI's PostgreSQL 17 container: `packages/domain-hostnames/src/lifecycle.integration.test.ts` (24 cases, two tenants, a suspended third, and a tenant of its own for each refusal case: the lifecycle with the fake driven in process, demotion on moved, failed, deleted, and mismatched readings, an expiring certificate kept servable, disable, expired claims, held names in any status, activation refusals including from the runtime role, recorded checks refused across tenants, adoption only after a lost answer, a 429 storm, account-level refusals and an unreachable zone recording nothing, a refusal about one record recorded with no change while the run goes on, a renewing or unknown reading keeping an active hostname, guards for every role, the platform allowlist, and injection-shaped names); `apps/api-worker/test-integration/hostnames.integration.test.ts` (every case under `ENVIRONMENT` `local` and `preview`: non-canonical origins on the bootstrap and on one route of every kind, no CORS for pending, disabled, or unknown hosts, preflight and simple requests alike, the catalog's shared 404, cross-tenant offers, `*.localhost` rows only locally, a disable failing closed at once on data routes, with the 90-second browser bound stated, the gateway's public fields, and the static rollback); `packages/domain-messaging/src/guest-origin.integration.test.ts`; `tools/hostnames/src/commands.integration.test.ts`; and the tenancy catalog checks for the new table's grants and the runtime's EXECUTE on each hostname function. Every existing suite passed on `0011` with no fixture edited before the review fixes; the R2 fix then edited four booking fixtures, listed below.
- **Browser checks**, Playwright at 375 and 1280, in CI's browser job and, for part A, also locally on an installed Google Chrome:
  - Part A, the built site under the guest Worker with a stub gateway and no database: each brand serves only its own manifest, theme color, and icons; Chromium's DevTools manifest reading has no errors and `Page.getInstallabilityErrors` reports none on CI's Chromium (a local run on Google Chrome reports only `in-incognito`, which every test profile there reports); a definite miss gets a 404 manifest and a kill switch that clears caches and unregisters; a gateway outage is 503 and clears nothing; the Worker's headers; and the neutral offline page with the network cut, with no navigation, token, or API answer in CacheStorage.
  - Part B, the guest Worker bound to the API's `GuestGateway` across `wrangler dev` processes, the API on `:8961` against the container seeded with `SEED_LOCAL_HOSTNAMES=1`: each brand on `/`, `/support`, `/offline`, `/terms`, `/privacy`, and `/legal/terms` with no other brand on the page and no CSP violation; the same installability readings; manage and waiver link addresses leaving no token or API answer in CacheStorage; the not-published screen on an unknown host; and a host disabled through the admin CLI failing closed after a hard reload, with its service worker unregistered.
- **D3 decided by the first run.** An SVG-only icon set reported no manifest or installability errors in Chromium, for both brands at both widths, in part A and in part B, so the composed SVG icons (D3(a)) stand and no raster icon is needed for the check. Safari and iOS install behavior was not tested.
- **Deviations from the goal packet:** a sixth admin-only function, `app.hostname_attach_provider`, so a name is reserved in the database before the provider's record exists; verified-origin CORS also covers `/v1/fake-provider/*`, which the guest page's pay step calls from the tenant origin; `app.hostname_request` takes the provider; the guest Worker hands the request URL's hostname to the gateway, which parses it, rather than importing the parser, which would pull the database client into its bundle; the page registers its service worker from the bootstrap rather than `main.tsx`; `src/sw/kill-switch.ts` holds the kill-switch script beside `sw.ts`; `apps/api-worker/src/email.ts` passes the environment to email delivery; the offline page is `neutral-offline.html`, because static assets serve an `.html` path with a redirect that cannot answer a navigation; and the manage and waiver journeys use link addresses with synthetic tokens, not a completed booking.
- **CI on the final code**, run [40703012696](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40703012696) on `6d39d11`, after the review and both rechecks, all green: checks (lint, typecheck, unit tests, contracts with no drift, build with the precache check, release dry runs, doc links, prototype); integration, 663 tests in 58 files across the workspace on the PostgreSQL 17 container, the API's 229 in 18 files and the hostname lifecycle's 24 among them; browser, part A 16 passed and part B 13 passed with 3 skipped (the disable journey and the two service worker update checks run once each, at 1280). The browser job ran four times on that commit, the first attempt and three reruns, and passed each time. The two update checks had failed now and then before it, CI-only, with the new worker left waiting after a page load that should have asked; `6d39d11` reads the registration again once a worker is ready before it asks, since the registration `register()` resolves with can lag the browser's worker states. That cause was likely, not proven (the follow-up's experiment below disproved it): the failure did not recur, so the diagnostics the checks print on failure never ran. This entry was then updated in a docs-only commit. Earlier green runs: [40693730301](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40693730301) on `aa07954`, [40692365952](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40692365952) on `6a570da`, [40687717550](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40687717550) on `6a81717` after the review fixes, and [40682125627](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40682125627) on `142cd46` before the review. An unrelated operator-site unit test (`BookingsPage.test.tsx`, the finance view's request list) failed twice in the checks job on `92382cb` and passed on rerun; it is outside this goal.
- **Review of `978a316`** (independent, security, and PWA, by fresh reviewers): accept with fixes; 6 confirmed should-fix, no must-fix, 16 notes. Each fix has a test that fails on the earlier sources where one is feasible:
  - R1: a provider refusal (401, 403, another 4xx, a malformed answer) or a wrong zone (404 by id and for the exact-name lookup) no longer demotes. The sweep records nothing for the hostname it was reading and stops its provider reads as a provider fault (narrowed by the rechecks below: only account-level refusals stop them, a refusal about one record is recorded with no change, and claims that ran out still expire after the stop), and `app.hostname_record_check` demotes only on a definitive negative (moved, blocked, deleted, or a failed certificate), so a renewing certificate or an unknown state keeps an active hostname serving. A record by id that is gone is confirmed by an exact-name lookup before it reads as deleted. Disabled stays terminal; the database README records that `0012` is the recovery path. A fixture case reads a renewal (`pending_validation` on an active hostname) as pending; it is unverified against live Cloudflare and listed for G2.14b-live.
  - R2 and SEC-1: email delivery with no environment now applies the strict deployed rules, not the local ones. Four fixture files outside this goal's paths say `environment: "local"` where they mint `*.localhost` links, one line each: `packages/domain-booking/src/access.integration.test.ts`, `cancel.integration.test.ts`, `adversarial/self-service-harness.ts`, and `adversarial/self-service-recipient.integration.test.ts`. The first three are on the goal packet's list of fixture files not to edit (the files that insert `tenant_hostnames` rows); the coordinator authorized the minimal change, and no hostname row they insert changed.
  - PWA-1: the precache list is built after Vite drops the CSS-only entry's empty script chunk, and `scripts/check-precache.mjs` fails every build whose precache names a file the build did not write (it failed on the earlier build, naming `/assets/offline-*.js`).
  - PWA-2: D11(a). The worker never skips waiting by itself; the next page load posts `SKIP_WAITING` once, and the worker takes over only when that page is the site's one open window (corrected in the recheck below).
  - UI-1: the standalone links on `/support` are 44 px tap targets, as the footer's are.
  - Notes fixed: unset `PUBLIC_CORS` means static (R6, SEC-2); simple requests answer CORS from the same projection and limiter as preflights, so a flood on a made-up path costs no lookup (R5); `/offline` is neutral, with no operator header, footer, or contact (UI-2); the support copy no longer offers platform support "by email" with no address (UI-3); local browser checks default to the installed Chrome and never reuse a server on 5361 (CI-1, CI-2). Documented: the interrupted-request runbook in the [hostname CLI](../tools/hostnames/README.md) (R4), the release order and the custom domains' DNS records (R9), and the gateway's connection per call (R7, SEC-3) in the G2.14b-live list of the demo build plan.
- **Recheck of `e7b0232`** (one checker per fix and a regression sweep): fixes, each with a test that fails on the earlier sources where one is feasible:
  - The update policy: the worker takes over only when the asking page is the site's one open window (`clients.matchAll`), so another tab or the installed app keeps build N's worker and cache; the page asks once per page load. Two browser checks ship a build with a new build id: one proves the new worker installs, waits, and is active with the new cache after the next navigation; the other proves it keeps waiting while a second window is open and takes over once that window closes. The multi-window rule was also run against a local Chrome.
  - The R1 tests read the hostname under test: each runs on a tenant of its own and asserts the sweep read that hostname and what its row holds afterwards.
  - Head of line: only account-level refusals stop the provider reads (401, 403, Cloudflare's authentication codes, a lookup's 404). A refusal about one record is recorded with no change (`app.hostname_record_check` with both states NULL and the code, edited in the unmerged 0011): the states stay, the next check is scheduled as usual, a claim past its end still expires, and the run goes on to the next hostname.
  - The cron logs the refusal code and the opaque provider record id, and the sweep summary its provider fault and unread count. The hostname sweep's attributes were missing from the logger's allowlist, so its summary had logged none of them; `packages/observability` now allows them (an edit outside the goal's paths).
  - Docs: the CORS projection and its 30-second grant after a disable, the definitive-negative rule as a coordinator default in 06 and 12 against the hosting plan's fail-closed wording, the fixture edits above, `guestOriginFor`'s doc comment, and the wrong-zone limit (a token that reaches another real zone) as a G2.14b-live check. Any database that applied an earlier `0011` must be recreated: the runner refuses a changed applied migration.
- **Second recheck of `f583365`:** fixes, each with a test that fails on the earlier sources:
  - The take-over checks who asked: the worker skips waiting only when the one open window is the client that posted `SKIP_WAITING` (`event.source`), and stops looking when the asker leaves. A unit case has the asker leave while another window stays: no take-over. It looks seven times over 60 seconds, because the 15-second schedule had failed in CI; the reason given then, a replaced document staying on headless Chromium's client list for tens of seconds, was a hypothesis and was never observed (see the follow-up below); the multi-window browser check holds past that schedule before it asserts the worker is still waiting, and the single-window check starts and ends with exactly one window.
  - Any HTTP 401 or 403 is an account-level refusal, whatever Cloudflare code it carries; the client keeps the HTTP status on every refusal. Tested with a 403 carrying an unlisted code.
  - A pending hostname whose claim ran out expires without a provider read, first in its tenant, and also after an account-level refusal or a throttle stopped the provider reads. A pending hostname whose one record gives no reading backs off on the pending schedule (60, 120, 240 seconds). Both are tested, except expiry after a throttle, which the follow-up below adds.
  - The database README describes the no-reading mode of `app.hostname_record_check` and its 22023 guard; the integration file count is 58.
  - The page asks from the registration as the browser reports it once a worker is ready, not from the one `register()` resolves with (see the CI bullet above).
- **Follow-up**, pull request 26 on branch `build/g2.14b-followups`, merged into `main` as `b6d0247` without an independent review. This bullet, the review, and its fixes landed after the merge, on a branch of their own:
  - The sweep's budget ends the whole run, claim expiries included. After the call limit, a throttle, or an account-level refusal, the sweep switches to expiry only: it reads no provider and selects only pending hostnames whose claim ran out, until the budget ends. The comments and READMEs that said such a stop ends the run and records nothing now say so (`packages/domain-hostnames`, the database README, the API Worker's README and `scheduled.ts`). Three integration cases were added: expiry after a throttle, the budget ending the run before an ended claim expires (the next run expires it with no read), and expiry with no further provider call after the call limit. The claim-expiry cases pass a tenant limit above the tenant count, so the 100-tenant sweep limit no longer decides them. Budget wording: no hostname read and no expiry starts after the budget, but a read already under way can still make its second provider call (the exact-name lookup), so a run can end later than the budget by up to the client's request timeouts. The lifecycle file has 26 cases, green in CI run [40713681606](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40713681606) and every later run.
  - The D11(a) update checks ran 10 extra times each on desktop through a temporary CI step, reverted before the merge (`03672cf`). Each worker script logged what it saw: each `SKIP_WAITING` with the sender's client id, each `clients.matchAll` answer, each `skipWaiting` call, and, in the last run only, each fetch event the old worker got, all on the browser's clock.
    - Run [40713681606](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40713681606) on `39ddd68` (the ask from `6d39d11`): 22 of 22 passed. The asker was listed at the first look, 0 to 7 ms after the ask. The single-window list was exactly the asker. The multi-window list held both windows for all seven looks; after the other window closed, the new worker called `skipWaiting` 7 ms or less after the next ask. A replaced document lingering on the client list, and an asker not listed yet, never occurred.
    - Run [40716971051](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40716971051) on `f2a9e4d`, an experiment with the ask from before `6d39d11`: 2 of the 10 single-window repeats failed (2 of 11 single-window runs with part B); the 10 multi-window repeats passed. In both, the new worker got the ask and called `skipWaiting` within 1 ms, so the earlier theory of a missed ask was wrong. Chromium's version events show the old worker stopping 5 ms after the ask, starting again at 14 ms, and running at 17 ms in the first failure (3, 16, and 20 ms in the second). After that no event came for 75 seconds; the new worker stayed installed and both caches stayed.
    - Cause, as read from that run (a hypothesis, not proven): the bootstrap calls `followExperience` before the ready page renders. So an early ask goes out just before the page's next requests, and every request from a controlled page, the cross-origin API included, is a fetch event on the old worker. One of them restarted the old worker after `skipWaiting`, and in CI it was still running 75 seconds later. That run logged no fetch events in the old worker. The trips read is the likely request (see the next run). `6d39d11` asked later and so avoided this in the 27 runs that saw it, without addressing it.
    - Change, `23f0617`: the page asks only after `pageSettled`: the load event, then two frames, then `document.fonts.ready`. A unit case checks the order. Run [40766279773](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/40766279773), the first with the old worker's fetch log: 22 of 22 passed. The new worker called `skipWaiting` 0 to 5 ms after a single-window ask, and up to 12 ms after the multi-window ask once the other window closed; each test then saw the new worker active. Activation itself was not timed. In every ask, the last fetch the old worker got before it was the page's second API request, 7 to 37 ms earlier. Nothing reached the old worker after the new one skipped waiting. On a reload the fonts never reached it; they came from the memory cache.
    - What that proves, and what it does not: the passes alone do not tell the change from `6d39d11`, which also passed 22 of 22; 11 single-window passes in a row would happen about 11% of the time at the experiment's rate of 2 in 11. The evidence for asking after the page settles is the mechanism: no request reached the old worker after `skipWaiting`. `register.ts` had credited the font requests; this branch corrects its comment and the guest site README.
    - `1304c37`: the take-over loop stops only once it has seen the asker and the asker has left, so an asker not listed yet is looked for again. A unit case covers it. CI never showed this case.
  - Independent review, October 30, 2026, of the merged diff and this branch: accept with fixes, no must-fix. Fixed here: the experiment's counts and the fix run's numbers above (they had said 2 of 20, one failure's timings for both, 0 to 6 ms, and 8 to 37 ms); the page could ask a newer worker that its own update check installed while the page settled, so it now notes the worker waiting when it first reads its registration (after `ready`) and asks only that worker, with unit cases. Both D11(a) browser checks passed on that code (`3bc9878`; later commits change only its comments) once in CI (run [41146568793](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41146568793), part B 18 of 18) and once locally on a throwaway PostgreSQL 17 container with the installed Google Chrome. Locally the single-window take-over came 1 ms after the ask, the multi-window check held for all seven looks, and nothing reached the old worker after `skipWaiting`. No repeated run covers this code; the budget's wording; the guest site README called a lingering client list a fact; and two rows in 06 and 12 (D2 B, email) said an account-level refusal stops the hostname sweep, where it stops only the provider reads. Not fixed, as notes: no test covers a budget cut inside one tenant, and none checks that `registerServiceWorker` itself waits for `pageSettled` (the parts are tested apart).
  - Coordinator's review, October 31, 2026 (pull request 49, three skeptics per finding): no must-fix. Fixed here: the `askWaitingWorker` comments had justified skipping a newer worker as "this load is the old build", but navigations go network first, so the page is usually the newer build; the reason is D11(a): the next load asks the worker waiting when it first reads its registration. The timings above were `skipWaiting` calls, now named so. The budget wording now says a read under way finishes, by up to two request timeouts and one write, in the hostnames README, the sweep's comments, and the API README. Browser job with `askWaitingWorker`, on `cca158e` (main merged in), run [41207280294](https://github.com/jkowall/TideGrid/actions/runs/41207280294): both D11(a) checks passed, once each.
  - Not verified: whether Chromium without DevTools attached stalls the same way. A delay until the old worker idles is expected, but not measured. Firefox and Safari did not run. A request the page makes after the ask, such as a tap, can still reach the old worker.
- **Not proven, and no gate claimed:** nothing here passes the custom-hostname feasibility spike, the Core live gate's shared web build, custom hostname, or tenant isolation proofs, or its production domain checks. The Cloudflare client never called the real API. The part B journey checked CacheStorage after manage and waiver link addresses with synthetic tokens, not after a completed booking, and its two-tenant cases are the brand pages; cross-tenant reads of quotes, checkouts, manage, and waiver ids rest on the existing adversarial suites, which run under `local` only. No preview deployment ran: the production-like preview deploy, deployed CORS, demo acceptance step 1 on a preview deployment, and the `demo.tidegrid.us` cutover are G2.14b-live, which needs the owner (Cloudflare for SaaS, Neon quota, and deploy and cutover authorization). G2.14b stays open until then.

## 2026-10-20

### G2.15 Waiver evidence verification, October 20, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.15-waivers` from `c6dbfcd`, as lane 2 of wave 2, on lane 1's participants, messaging, access links, and management page. Merged through lane 1's final `91e14ca`, which carries main at `ad56def` (lane 3, PR #14). Paused from October 6 to October 19 for usage limits; the [demo build plan](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence) records the status. One migration, `0010_waivers.sql`, after lane 3's `0008` and lane 1's `0009`; it lands last of the three lanes.
- The contract is in [packages/domain-waivers](../packages/domain-waivers/README.md), with the API in [packages/contracts/src/waivers.ts](../packages/contracts/src/waivers.ts):
  - immutable, versioned templates for an operator or a product (the product's wins), with the content hash computed by the database and every version append-only;
  - a request per participant of a confirmed booking, assigned through lane 1's booking hooks in the confirming or party-changing transaction, at most one live per participant, revoked on cancellation or when who signs changes, and re-requested when a signed participant or the guardian who signed is renamed;
  - the request email through lane 1's `enqueueMessage`, its link issued at send time with only the token's hash stored, and resends audited with a reason. Sending limits are the database's, across every request a participant has had (`app.waiver_send_refusal`): 10 minutes between sends to one address, 5 generations per request, 8 a day per participant, 10 an hour per address, 40 a day per booking, and 1000 an hour per operator. A send no one waits on that only the interval holds is queued for when it is allowed; a cap leaves the status `email_held`. Delivery is reported apart from signature;
  - a single-use link exchanged only by POST for an hour's signing session bound to the tenant, booking, participant, request, and signer; acknowledgments, consent, confirmation of identity, a guardian's relationship, and the typed name the booking gives the signer;
  - append-only evidence with the rendered PDF and its SHA-256, in PostgreSQL for the demo (the reasons are in the README; R2 with object lock is a pre-pilot follow-up), a database chain that refuses a signed request without evidence, or evidence without its consumed link and open session, for every role, and the hash checked again on every read;
  - status per participant as an icon and a label on lane 1's `/manage` page (`GET /v1/public/manage/waivers`, names and statuses only), in the console's booking detail, and on the trip's roster.
- Owner decision, 2026-10-19: an adult participant without their own email gets no waiver email until one is added, with no fallback to the booker. Recorded in the README and the plan; journey 2 below shows the state.
- Test specialist: adversarial suites in `packages/domain-waivers/src/adversarial/` (assignment, links and sessions, email delivery, signing and evidence, and sending limits) and `apps/api-worker/test-integration/waivers-adversarial.integration.test.ts`. They were written before the pause and first ran on October 20. Confirmed defects, each fixed with its test failing first on the pre-fix sources:
  - a correction after the fifth send, or too near the trip's end, left the old address's link and session working (the limit check returned first);
  - renaming the guardian who signed for a minor kept the signature;
  - a signature that waited on the request's lock while an address correction ended its session failed in the evidence trigger (500); it now answers as an ended session (401);
  - a participant added while a version was published with unsigned work moved could keep the older version. Moving unsigned work is now `reassignUnsignedRequests`, after the publish commits, behind a barrier on the tenant's assignment key that every assignment holds shared; with the barrier removed the race test fails, with it the test passes;
  - in lane 1's delivery job, a retry of an email in flight could give a corrected-away address a live link or link a signed request. Lane 1 fixed it in `b7e6f9d`, and both tests pass on it;
  - after lane 1's review found that the delivery job's claim-time recheck does not stop a queued email after a change that only revokes links, tests now hold that every recipient change cancels the old message with `cancelMessage` in the correcting transaction. Removing an adult's address was a gap (the queued email still went to the old address); it now cancels it and ends the old links and sessions.
  - Three harness faults in the API suite were fixed (a HEAD answer parsed as JSON, a link read from an email meant to bounce, a test timeout), and one expectation changed with lane 1's `cancelMessage`: a signature now cancels an email in flight rather than letting it fail.
- Review of `b5145f1` in four parts (evidence integrity, security, lifecycle, and the screens), by fresh reviewers on a throwaway branch. Confirmed findings, each fixed with a test that fails first on the earlier sources:
  - security F1, the one must-fix: resend limits counted per request and only against the latest send, so alternating addresses or flipping a participant between adult and minor sent without limit. Fixed by the database's sending limits above; seven tests reproduce the review's cases, including direct inserts as `tidegrid_app`;
  - evidence F1: an assignment that began before a publish could keep the older version, because the in-effect check used the transaction's start time. The check and the request trigger now use the wall clock (`clock_timestamp()`); a test holds a transaction open across the publish;
  - security F2 and lifecycle F1: names at their 120-character limits overflowed the email's subject, and the throw rolled back the guest's party change or a whole version move. Names are now elided in the subject, preview, and heading, and email content the messaging module refuses answers `email_invalid` under a savepoint instead of throwing;
  - lifecycle F2: an address removed and added back within 10 minutes left no working link while the status read waiting. It now counts as a change and is sent when the interval allows, and the status says when;
  - ui F1 to F3: the resend dialog no longer says the reason shows in the booking's history (it is kept in the waiver's audit record); a rate-limited or failed open or signature keeps the token or the filled form for another tap; the signature's error reads "Enter your full name to sign";
  - evidence F3: `pnpm check` failed until this entry existed.
- Review notes fixed: a staff resend's refusal no longer rolls back the old address's revocation (lifecycle F5); one version move per template at a time (lifecycle F3, test fails without the lock); one consumed link opens a waiver session or a booking session, never both, in either order (security N3); a waiver link never outlives its trip, and once the trip has ended a link opens nothing and an open session signs nothing (`waiver_trip_over`), with the evidence trigger refusing it too (N5); signing closes the request's other sessions (N4); a spent link or ended session of a signed, replaced, or withdrawn waiver says so (ui F4 and F5); the PDF's hash is checked again on read (evidence F6); the minor tag is separated from the name for screen readers (ui F6); "Sent again" shows under the person and the card reads again (ui F7); the PDF's signing record stays on one page (ui F8); the API test's timeout (lifecycle F4); and the field-encryption deferral is recorded in the README (security N2). Left as notes: version window semantics (evidence F5), the source address taken from the route (evidence F7), and two matters of taste (ui F9 and F10).
- Integration runs, each on a throwaway Neon branch, one at a time:
  - while fixing: the domain-waivers suites and the API waiver suites on lane 1's `b7e6f9d`, then on the review fixes;
  - the full suite once, at `f5051f6` (later commits touch documents and that one test only), on a fresh branch migrated through `0010` and seeded twice with `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1` (2 minutes 23 seconds, then 16 seconds adding nothing): 619 of 620 tests in 54 files passed in 99 minutes (database 43, catalog 25, messaging 21, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 126, waivers 46, API 215). The one failure was a stale expectation in the API adversarial suite: after a cancellation or a version move, a session's routes now answer 409 with the reason (ui F4) instead of 401. The expectation is updated in `4f7e20b`, and that file then passed 8 of 8 on the same branch.
- Browser journeys in the built-in browser against `wrangler dev` (port 8911) with the fake provider and email, and the guest (5311) and console (5312) dev servers, before the review. The runtime connected as `tidegrid_app` with the inherited password; nothing was rotated.

Both PDFs render, and the guest's and the staff's downloads are the stored bytes (SHA-256 matched).

### G2.11c Guest self-service and the email shim verification, October 20, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.11c-self-service-and-email` from `c6dbfcd` (main with G2.1 through G2.12b), as lane 1 of wave 2, beside lane 2 (waivers) and lane 3 (console completion) on disjoint paths; main's `e87ace9` (PR #13) is merged in. Paused from October 6 to 19, resumed on the 19th. One migration, `0009_self_service_and_email.sql`, after lane 3's `0008_console_completion.sql` (lane 3 landed first, as PR #14, and is merged into this branch). Demo acceptance items 2, 3, and 4: the guest names the party, the confirmation email's link opens the booking without an account, and the guest cancels within policy while the console shows the change. Email runs on the shim the owner allowed on 2026-10-05, recorded as the [email narrowing](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence).
- Participants ([booking contract](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#participants), [packages/contracts/src/participants.ts](../packages/contracts/src/participants.ts)): one per place up to the party size, with a name, whether a minor, a guardian's name and email for a minor, and an optional email. Edited in place, never removed, only while the booking stands and before departure; audit and events name which fields changed, never the values. Staff read them (`bookings.read`).
- Email ([messaging contract](../packages/domain-messaging/README.md)): `enqueueMessage(trx, ctx, …)` writes a message in the caller's transaction, rendered with the operator's brand from escaped blocks, once per dedupe key. The delivery job sends right after the commit and from the cron, with the message id as the provider's idempotency key; it retries temporary failures after 1, 5, 15, and 60 minutes and 4 hours, fails after six attempts or a refusal, and records each message's state. Templates: the confirmation with its receipt and management link, a new link on request, and the cancellation. The fake provider keeps what it accepted in its own table, and the local mailbox at `/v1/fake-email/mailbox` shows it.
- Access ([booking contract](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#guest-self-service-lane-1-g211c)): a link is issued only when its email is sent, travels only in the URL's fragment, and is stored as a hash. Only an explicit POST redeems it, once, for a session of an hour bound to the operator, the booking, and the purpose; a GET, HEAD, preview, or query string opens nothing. A newer email's link supersedes older ones. A guest may ask for a new link, three an hour, to the booker's address on file. Right after paying, the checkout's secret opens the same session for an hour.
- Cancellation within policy: the remedy comes from the booking's own policy version on the database clock (full, a percentage rounded half up to the cent, or none after the cutoff; a credit is the operator's). The guest sees the amount first, and the command carries it, refused if the terms moved. One transaction writes the cancellation, the canceled booking, the refund row, the released hold, the audit, the events, the email, and the waiver hook; the refund then goes through the payment shim with its key. The database refuses any other terms, a second cancellation, or a booking change other than confirmed to canceled, for every role.
- Hooks for lane 2: `BookingHooks` (`bookingConfirmed`, `participantAdded`, `participantUpdated`, `bookingCanceled`) run inside the changing transaction, wired once in `apps/api-worker/src/booking-hooks.ts`; lane 2 owns that function's body.
- Guest site: `/manage` shows the booking, its receipt, and its party; names and corrects participants; cancels within policy; asks for a new link; and ends the session. The confirmation's "Name your party now" opens it at once ([guest README](../apps/guest-site/README.md#the-management-page-g211c)). Console: canceled bookings in the day list, detail, and history, with the cancellation's terms and refund ([console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#canceled-bookings-lane-1-g211c)).
- Decisions:
  - In-transaction hooks rather than an outbox consumer or a trigger, so a waiver is assigned atomically with the confirmation or the participant.
  - Links are minted at send time, per attempt, never stored raw; a retry's unsent token simply expires.
  - The mailbox is local only: it shows every booking's link, so a deployed preview gets it only behind Cloudflare Access (proposed).
  - The day list keeps canceled bookings, badged; its counts are of active bookings and say "No active bookings" when only canceled ones show.
  - Booking and participant rows are locked `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so the delivery job's foreign key checks never wait behind a change that waits for the job.
- Defects found and fixed after the pause, each with a test that failed first:
  - A retried older email superseded the link in a newer email the guest held, so no delivered link worked (the test specialist's red test). Issuing a link now supersedes only older messages' links.
  - A retry of an email that was being sent when the guest corrected the address minted a live link for the old address, and a waiver signed mid-send could get a live signing link again (raised by lane 2). `cancelMessage` now cancels a sending message, waits for a claim in progress, and revokes its links; the claim cancels, unsent, a message whose own link was revoked or whose subject's access closed or moved after it was written. `self-service-recipient.integration.test.ts`, 6 tests: all 5 behavior tests failed on the old code.
  - A sent message still reported an earlier attempt's error as its `failureCode`. The trigger now clears it when the message is sent.
  - A typecheck error and two Biome findings in the UI tests, and guest copy that read "There's no refund now" under "If you cancel now".
  - Before the pause, the browser journey found: the console could not read a canceled booking (its shape checks expected confirmed only); delivery hung on the Worker's one-connection pool (the link origin was read inside the claim; now read once before it, with a one-connection test); a saved participant appeared only after a reload; focus fell to the page after a cancellation.
- Second-model review of `61c8734` (four dimensions, each finding checked by three skeptics): nothing blocking. Every confirmed finding and every note the coordinator kept is fixed, each with a test that failed first:
  - Email F1: the claim's check missed a recipient change made before any link existed, and a revocation racing a claim. Fixed by making the safety net real rather than narrowing the contract: `revokeAccessLinks`, for `recipient_changed`, `completed`, `withdrawn`, and `booking_canceled`, locks and cancels the subject's pending link-carrying email before it revokes the links, waiting for a claim in progress (`c4f896d`).
  - Email F2: a product name with a tab or a newline made the confirmation email throw inside the confirming transaction, rolling back a paid booking. Subjects, previews, and recipients' names are single-line, and the confirmation email is written under a savepoint whose failure is audited instead (`302b2f9`).
  - UI F1: a new link's outcome showed off screen; it now shows under the button and takes focus (`00d3ad6`).
  - Money M2 and email F7: a confirmation still in its retry backoff went out after the cancellation; cancellation now cancels it, keeping its links (`7a9d112`).
  - Money M1: the cancellation email says the refund is on its way, and a refused refund sends one follow-up (`61356b5`).
  - Access AA-1 and AA-2: no renewal from links revoked on purpose; at most three live sessions per checkout's secret (`2e92b89`).
  - Email F4: a canceled message reports only its own reason (`58ad429`). UI F2: a late redemption answer no longer replaces a newer link's landing (`8071697`). UI F3: the renewal notice says the hourly limit (`fe5e980`).
  - Left as the coordinator decided: AA-3 to AA-7, M3, F5, F6, F8, F9, and two taste notes. F8, the email sweep visiting tenants by id with a fixed limit and budget, is a follow-up, as the checkout sweep's is.
- After lane 3 landed (PR #14 as `ad56def`), main is merged in and the migration is `0009`. Two follow-ups lane 3 left are done: a canceled booking stays on the day's report and CSV with its money and counts 0 bookings and 0 guests, as the day list's counts do; and the roster lists each party's names by place (`38c9d5f`).
- Tests by the lane's helpers: the test specialist's adversarial suites (domain-booking cancel 12 and access 13, messaging 12, API access 11, cancel 6, staff 2) for token replay, revocation, scanner GETs, cross-tenant use, expiry, cancel against late events, double cancel, and refund idempotency; the routine implementer's UI suites (session 66, model 110, management page 112, console canceled view 39), with 25 mutations checked.
- Integration runs, each on its own throwaway Neon branch, one at a time:
  - after the pause's fixes: messaging 20 of 20; domain-booking lane 1 files 50 of 50; recipient 6 of 6; the API's self-service suites 19 of 19 (all four never-run files now pass);
  - after the review's fixes, each changed suite on its own branch: recipient 9 of 9, access 15 of 15 and 6 of 6, email safety 2 of 2, cancellation email 4 of 4, messaging 21 of 21, console reads 2 of 2, the API's access suite 11 of 11;
  - the full suite once more at `38c9d5f`, after the review's fixes and the merge of main, on a fresh branch migrated from empty through `0009` and seeded twice with `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1` (the second run added nothing: 6 bookings and 6 confirmation emails): 559 tests in 46 files passed in 74 minutes (database 43, catalog 25, messaging 21, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 126, API 200). Later commits touch documents only;
  - the full suite at `9a0144f`, before the review, on a fresh branch migrated from empty and seeded twice with `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1` (the second run added nothing: 6 bookings and their 6 confirmation emails, none new): 506 tests in 42 files passed in 67 minutes (database 43, catalog 25, messaging 20, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 113, API 161). Later commits touch the guest site and documents only.
- Email shim evidence, `apps/api-worker/scripts/email-shim-evidence.ts` against `wrangler dev --test-scheduled` on that fresh branch:
  - sent once after the commit, with the booking; the fake's redelivery of the payment event, twice, and two sweeps changed nothing (one provider call, one accepted);
  - a temporary failure stayed queued until due, then sent on attempt 2 with the same key (two calls, one accepted, the error cleared);
  - a bounced address failed at once and two sweeps left it alone;
  - a lost answer (the provider had accepted; the Worker never heard) was retried with the same key, and the message recorded the delivery the provider already had;
  - control total: 4 messages, 3 sent, 1 failed, 6 provider calls, 3 accepted deliveries, none accepted twice.
- Browser journeys in the built-in browser:
  - October 6, on a seeded branch with the API on 8901, the guest site on 5301, and the console on 5302, at 375 and 1280: booking `1MKVTY6E` ($130.55) on a Demo Harbor cruise; its confirmation in the mailbox; the link's landing (the fragment gone from the address, nothing redeemed until the click); guests 1 and 3 named, the second a minor with a guardian; canceled before the cutoff with the full refund shown first and settled; the cancellation email; the console's Canceled badge, Cancellation and Refund blocks, history, and "No active bookings" on the day. Booking `YAHFENZY`, after the cutoff: no refund, canceled, the email, focus on the result. No horizontal scroll at 375.
  - October 20, after the fixes, at 375 on the fresh branch: booking `9J530972` from the mailbox's link, a guest named, canceled with a full refund of $50.65, both emails sent.
- Automated checks: `pnpm check` is green at the final commit: typecheck, Biome over 414 files, 2347 unit tests across 15 packages (guest site 835, console 856, design system 210, catalog 118, pricing 92, API 57, booking 52, contracts 47, and the rest), three dry-run deploys, doc links across 90 Markdown files, and 29 prototype tests. `pnpm contracts:generate` shows no drift.
- Not done: real email (the Cloudflare Email Service adapter), the mailbox on a deployed preview, staff resend and failure surfaces in the console (G2.16); waiver status on the roster (lane 2); the email sweep's fairness across many tenants (review F8); removing a participant, rescheduling, credits, deposits, SMS, and staff manual booking; screen reader and physical device tests.

## 2026-10-06

### G2.12c Console completion verification, October 6, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.12c-console-completion` from `c6dbfcd` (main with G2.1 through G2.12b), as lane 3 of wave 2, beside lane 1 (email and guest self-service) and lane 2 (waivers) on disjoint paths; main's `e87ace9` (PR #13, Neon branch autoscaling) is merged in. One migration, `0008_console_completion.sql`, which the coordinator renumbers at merge. Demo acceptance items 5 and 7: the operator finds a booking, exports the day, and the totals match; one operator never reaches another's results or export.
- API, in [packages/contracts/src/console.ts](../packages/contracts/src/console.ts) and the [checkout contract](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#console-completion-g212c):
  - `POST /v1/staff/tenants/{tenantId}/booking-searches`: bookings across every day by the start of a reference (read as a guest quotes it), by every word of the booker's name, by part of the email, and by departure dates, 25 at a time with a keyset cursor and a count. A POST, so names and emails never enter a URL. For finance the text matches references only and the query never joins the table holding the booker;
  - `GET .../booking-day-totals?date=`: the day's control totals from the ledger (bookings, guests, gross, discounts, fees, added taxes, total charged, refunds, net, included tax, and pending refunds);
  - `GET .../booking-day-export?date=`: the same day as a CSV attachment with stable columns (each departure's UTC instant and pending refunds among them, since the review), the booker's name and email for owners and booking staff only, and a total row equal to the control totals; `no-store`, named by the operator's slug and the date only, and audited (`booking_day.exported`, with the row count);
  - `GET` and `POST .../bookings/{bookingId}/notes`: owners and booking staff read and add a booking's notes (`notes.read`, `notes.write`; finance holds neither, by the owner's decision of October 19), idempotent by key and audited by the note's id, never its text; the answer to an added note leaves the text out, so the idempotency store keeps no copy;
  - the calendar's trips query refuses year 0000 in the contract, as the day list's does.
- Migration 0008 adds `booking_notes` under tenancy rules 1 to 10 ([database README](../packages/database/README.md#booking-notes)): append-only for every role, the author, request, and time stamped from the transaction by a trigger that refuses any actor but staff, and composite keys to the booking and to a membership of the same tenant.
- Console ([console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#console-completion-g212c)):
  - Search at `/bookings/search`, with designed idle, loading, no-match, error, and field-error states, Show more bookings, and the last search kept in the tab's memory so Back returns to it; finance's field reads "Booking reference" and says why;
  - the day's totals above its trips, and Export CSV, which fetches the file same-origin and hands it to the browser under the server's file name;
  - a Notes card on each booking for owners and booking staff (none for finance), newest first, with author and the marina's time, and a 500-character form whose idempotency key survives a retry of the same note;
  - the G2.12b follow-ups: the rail shows the whole email; signing in from a deep link returns to it; the roster's scroll region is a tab stop only while it scrolls; a day beyond two years says which day it opened; and every "Sign in again" reloads its own page;
  - the roster's party is one cell, ready for participants' names; design system: a `download` icon.
- Decisions:
  - Search terms travel in a POST body, never a URL, and the console keeps them in memory only, never in the address, history, or storage.
  - A day is the marina's departure date, as on the Bookings page. Gross is trip prices and extras before discounts; total is what guests were charged; refunds count once they succeed; a refund still requested is reported as pending and counted nowhere else.
  - The export's money is a plain decimal, never negative, with discounts and refunds in their own columns. Text a person typed is kept from running as a formula with a leading apostrophe. A day over 5,000 bookings is refused rather than cut short.
  - Each export is audited, since it carries guests' details out of the console for two roles. Notes are for owners and booking staff only (the owner's decision of October 19, after the review): a note is free text that can name a guest, so it follows the booker's details. This replaces the first build's rule that every role reads notes.
  - Not merged: lane 1's branch. By the coordinator's direction this pull request stays unstacked; participants' names on the roster are a small follow-up once lane 1 is on main.
  - Proposed, for when guest cancellation lands (lane 1): a canceled booking keeps its order and refund in the money columns and leaves the bookings and guests counts; the day list's counts should follow the same rule. Recorded in the [checkout contract](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#deferred-and-known-gaps).
- Tests:
  - API integration, by the lead, in `apps/api-worker/test-integration/console-completion.integration.test.ts` (37 tests), with every booking made through the real checkout, the fake provider, and its signed webhook. Search for each role by a quoted reference, a reference's start, every word of a name in any order, part of an email, LIKE's own characters as text, and dates; finance's text never matching a name or an email and its raw JSON free of every booker; roles judged per operator for a person with finance at one and owner at the other; keyset pages without overlap or gaps, and cursors from another search or tenant refused. The day's totals for each role equal to sums read straight from `orders`, again from `order_lines` by kind, and from `payment_refunds`, on a rich day (every kind of line), a refund day (a succeeded and a requested refund, written past G2.7's refund rule inside one admin transaction because no request can refund a booking yet), and an empty day. The CSV's headers, bytes, columns per role, rows against each order, total row against the ledger, formula guard, and audit rows. Notes: roles, authorship from the transaction, idempotent replay and reuse, validation, audit by id, and the table's append-only rules, author stamping, and composite keys checked as `tidegrid_app` and as the owner. Another tenant's ids answer exactly as unknown ones on every route, and the calendar's year-0000 guard answers 400.
  - API unit, in workerd (`apps/api-worker/test/auth-guards.test.ts`, 10 more): the public entry answers all five new operations with the ordinary 404, each new read asks for a session first and is `no-store`, and a search or a note without the console's Origin is refused before authentication. `pnpm check` caught that this suite's list of documented paths lacked the four new ones; it is fixed.
  - Domain unit (`packages/domain-booking/src/console-completion.test.ts`, 41): CSV quoting, the formula guard, money and instant formats, the byte order mark and CRLF, stable columns per role, total rows equal to the summed rows, and how a search reads its text.
  - Console unit: a routine implementer wrote 324 of them, for search, its rules and memory, the day's totals and export plumbing, notes, and the return to a deep link after signing in, with about 115 deliberate mutations of the production code, all caught but one that changes nothing observable. They found four gaps, each marked skipped until fixed and then run: focus fell to the page when Try again worked and its button went (search with no match, the day's totals, and notes), and a search answer in another currency crashed the page instead of reading as unshowable. Its notes also led to search-specific words for a refused search, tabs and line breaks in the search field read as spaces, and "1 character left". The lead updated the expectations G2.12c moved (the new reads, the h3 count, "Sign in again" to the same page, the roster's tab stop) and added the search page to the shell's route tests.
- Integration runs, each on its own throwaway Neon branch:
  - while building: the new suite 30, then 35, then 36 of 36 as the fixtures were corrected, and 37 of 37 with the calendar guard and the search's latest-refund join;
  - the full suite once, with the code through `9faa95e`, on a fresh branch migrated and seeded twice with `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1`, where the second run added nothing: 453 tests in 32 files passed in about 43 minutes (database 43 with `booking_notes` in the catalog guards, catalog 25, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 62, API 179).
  - after the review's fixes, which changed migration 0008 and the API, on one fresh branch migrated and seeded twice (the second run added nothing): the console-completion suite 39 of 39 (two more than before: the database refusing C1 and invisible bodies past the API, and `day_too_large`), then the full suite, 455 tests in 32 files in about 46 minutes (database 43, catalog 25, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 62, API 181).
- Automated checks: `pnpm check` is green at the final commit. It ran 2,001 unit tests across 14 packages (812 console, 57 API, 49 booking domain, 47 contracts, 23 identity with the notes permissions, and the rest unchanged); Biome checked 358 files; typecheck, three dry-run deploys, doc links across 89 Markdown files, and 29 prototype tests passed. `pnpm contracts:generate` shows no drift. Before the review the counts were 1,927 tests and 357 files.
- Browser journeys in the built-in browser on a throwaway Neon branch (migrated, seeded with demo bookings), against `wrangler dev` with the fake provider on port 8921, with the console both on its development server and as the production build served with its `_headers` policy plus a `report-uri`, on port 5322:
  - Owner at 1280: signed in from `/bookings` and landed back there; the day's totals ($241.00 gross, -$9.00 discounts, $12.50 fees, $16.24 taxes, $260.74 total and net) equal to the CSV's total row; a search by surname, a result opened, a two-line note added, and Back to the same results.
  - Finance at 1280: the same totals and an export without the booker's columns or any name; a surname read as a reference prefix and matching nothing; a reference prefix typed with a hyphen found its booking without a booker; the owner's note read, with no form. After an explicit sign-out the next sign-in landed on the overview.
  - Booking staff at 375: a search by part of an email, a note added, and an export with the booker's columns; the roster's region a tab stop at 375, where its table scrolls, and not at 1280.
  - Demo Reef's owner, on the production build: its own totals with Honolulu times and included taxes, a date-range search, the note form's empty and over-limit states, and a Demo Harbor surname finding nothing.
  - A day of 9999-12-31 in the address opened October 5, 2028 and said so. Nothing overflowed at 375. The export was checked by capturing the file the page handed to the browser rather than saving it; it carried the byte order mark and the server's file name. No content security policy report came from the app; the one report in the run was the test harness reading a blob URL with `fetch`, which `connect-src` refuses, and which the app never does.
- Browser after the review's fixes, on a second fresh branch (the integration suites rotate the runtime password, so the first could not serve a dev API), against `wrangler dev` on port 8921 and the console's development server on 5322:
  - Owner at 1280: dates in the wrong order put both date inputs at the same top and height (44 px each, where the review measured 59 and 44) and said the problem in the status; a date-range search showed "Departing Oct 1, 2026 to May 31, 2027." under the count. With search answers held for 2.5 seconds, new text and Enter during a search sent a second request, and only its answer showed, with the terms it was found for.
  - Owner at 375: the totals grid's ten figures in two columns, Added taxes and Included tax among them, and no page overflow; the day's export header with Departure at (UTC) and Pending refunds, a row's 18:00 EDT departure as 22:00 UTC, and a total row equal to the grid. An address day of 2026-02-30 opened today with its notice. A note with an emoji counted 48 characters, as the API counts, and showed once added from the API's bodiless answer.
  - Finance at 375: the same booking with no Notes card and no notes request; the guest card names the notes among what owners and booking staff see; a direct request for the notes answered 403 `forbidden` with `no-store`.
- Spreadsheet: the owner, booking staff, and finance exports, and a synthetic export with accented names, quoted commas, a formula-shaped name, and an email starting with a plus sign, opened in LibreOffice Calc (headless CSV import, UTF-8 by the byte order mark): money as numbers whose rows sum exactly to the total row, dates and times as dates, text intact, and no cell a formula.
- Paused from October 6 to October 19 at the owner's request; finished on October 19 and 20 with the route rows, the guard tests, `pnpm check`, and this entry.
- Review, October 20: the coordinator's combined UI, code, and data review of `199ab1f` confirmed seven should-fix findings, refuted two, and left fifteen notes. Each fix has a test that failed before it: the new console tests, run against the console's source from before the fixes, failed 80 times; the new contract, domain, and API tests cover the rest.
  - Search dropped new terms submitted while a search loaded, then showed the old answer under the new text, so a desk agent could tell a guest their booking did not exist. New terms now replace the search on its way, whose answer never shows. Only the same terms wait, and the button is busy only for those, so Enter reaches the form in a browser too. Under the count the results say what they were found for.
  - Field problems were not announced when focus was already in the field. They are said in the status, clear on the next change, and take away results for other terms (note UI-7).
  - A day over 5,000 bookings read as an outage with a Try again that could never work. `day_too_large` has its own reason, with no Try again, on the totals and on Export CSV; an API test reaches it with a lowered limit.
  - The CSV had no pending refunds. `Pending refunds` follows `Refunds`, in each row and in the total row, equal to the control totals. With it, `Taxes` became `Added taxes`, and `Departure at (UTC)` keeps two departures at 01:30 on the night the clocks go back apart (note data F5).
  - The date fields shifted when one showed an error; they align to the top now.
  - A note body accepted C1 controls and text that showed nothing. The contract and migration 0008's CHECK refuse both; 0008 is edited in place, as it is not merged. A database test writes such bodies past the API.
  - Notes, fixed: no-store on the guards' refusals, by one middleware in `app.ts` for every staff, sign-in, and `/v1/me` answer; the added note's answer, which the idempotency store keeps, without its text; included tax as its own figure on the totals grid; Show more bookings says how many are shown; the note counter counts code points after trimming; one shared reference-prefix reader for the API and the console; a later signed-out visit to the overview drops an older kept page; an impossible day in the address says so; and Search is secondary while the person is signed out.
  - Declined or deferred: UI-12, the totals panel's height on a phone, is deferred as taste. Code F7 widens the documented spreadsheet risk to references read as scientific notation: about one in 6,900 references in all (one in 11,000 for digits alone, which the first entry gave wrongly as one in 7,000). Data F4 is superseded by the owner's decision. UI-4 and data F1 were refuted by the review.
  - Found while fixing: the source held literal invisible characters (the CSV's byte order mark, the CHECK's character set, and test bodies), which no reader can see or review. They are written as escapes now, and no file on the branch holds one.
- Not done: participants and waiver status on the roster (lanes 1 and 2), staff manual booking (a later goal), screen reader and physical device tests.

### G2.12b Console booking views verification, October 6, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.12b-console-bookings` from `632d80b` on `prep/g2.11b` (main with G2.5, G2.6, G2.7, the trips slice, and G2.11b). The owner pulled this thin piece of G2.12 forward on 2026-10-06 so the bookings they made as a guest show on the operator side; the step is recorded in the [demo build plan](v2/12-demo-build-plan.md#goal-sequence). No migration: every read uses tables G2.7 created.
- API, read-only, in [packages/contracts/src/bookings.ts](../packages/contracts/src/bookings.ts) and the [checkout contract](../packages/domain-booking/README.md#console-reads-g212b):
  - `GET /v1/staff/tenants/{tenantId}/bookings?date=&tripId=&limit=&after=`: a local date's trips with their booking and guest counts, and a page of bookings by departure and confirmation, with a keyset cursor that must name a booking in the same listing;
  - `GET .../bookings/{bookingId}`: the order's lines and totals with each tax's rate, the payment with its provider reference masked, any refund, and a timeline;
  - `GET .../booking-references/{reference}`: quick find, read as typed (case, spaces, and hyphens ignored; O, I, and L read as 0, 1, and 1);
  - `GET .../trips/{tripId}/roster`: every booking on a trip with the totals, for `bookings.read` only;
  - the finalization exceptions read gains the trip, the checkout's state and expiry, the masked provider reference, what the provider's event reported, who paid (for `bookings.read`), and a `before` cursor.
  - Every role with `payments.read` reads bookings as money. The booker's name and email are left out of the queries for any role without `bookings.read`, so finance's responses never carry them. Each read runs in one repeatable-read, read-only snapshot (`snapshotRead` in `@tidegrid/database`), so a page's counts and rows describe the same moment. Unknown, malformed, and other tenants' ids share one 404, and every answer and refusal is `no-store`. `pnpm contracts:generate` regenerated the OpenAPI document and shows no drift.
- Console, for the selected operator ([console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#booking-views-g212b)):
  - Bookings: a day's trips on the marina's clock, each with its bookings (reference, booker for owners and booking staff, party by ticket type or a charter's guests, extras, total, payment, and state); Previous, Today, and Next within two years; a trip filter; both kept in the address; Show more bookings past 100; designed loading, empty, error, and unreadable states; and quick find by reference;
  - one booking: trip, when, boat, where to meet, the guest for allowed roles, party and extras, the order as a ledger with tax rates, the payment with the masked reference, any refund, and a timeline with the zone named;
  - Exceptions: what happened, the trip, the party, the amount, when the checkout ran out and the payment arrived, the masked reference, the refund's state, and who paid for owners and booking staff;
  - a printable roster per trip with guest totals by ticket type, extras to prepare, a notice that participants and waivers are not collected yet, and print rules that leave the console out; on a phone its table scrolls inside its own region;
  - the calendar's booked count links to the trip's bookings; the navigation gains Exceptions and lays out two by two on a phone;
  - design system: `search` and `printer` icons.
- Seed: `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1` gives each demo operator two shared-seat bookings on its earliest bookable departure (one with extras, one with its promotion code), a private charter, and a late payment refunded in full with its `no_capacity` exception. Each goes through the pricing service, the checkout service, the fake provider, and a signed event verified and handed to `handleVerifiedEvent`, as the webhook route does; the late checkout holds for the service's one-minute minimum and runs out before another guest takes the boat. Its output counts guests and names none.
- Decisions:
  - Finance sees payments, totals, refunds, and exceptions, not the booker's name or email, and no roster. The roster is a booking-management sheet for running a departure; without names it would be the bookings list again, and keeping it to `bookings.read` keeps every printed sheet with the roles that manage bookings. The [console README](../apps/operator-site/README.md#who-sees-what) records it.
  - Exceptions name who paid for owners and booking staff, since following up means reaching the guest. G2.7's exceptions read carried no personal data; it now carries the booker for `bookings.read` only.
  - The printed roster names the booker but leaves out the email, which is on the booking.
  - The console remembers the chosen operator's id, for the tab in `sessionStorage` and as the last choice in `localStorage`, so a reload or a new tab opens a booking or roster address with its own operator; an id that is not one of the person's operators is ignored. Choosing another operator drops the list's trip and day.
  - No migration and no writes; resolving exceptions, cancellation, and refunds stay with G2.9, G2.11, and G2.12.
- API integration tests by the test specialist, in `apps/api-worker/test-integration/bookings-console.integration.test.ts` (22 tests): each role's view of each route, with finance's raw JSON checked for every personal key and value; two-tenant escape on every new route; one 404 for unknown, malformed, and other tenants' ids; masked references and no secrets in any answer; `no-store` on answers and refusals; paging, cursors, and limits; and counts that match the rows. They found three defects, each fixed with a test that failed first: a page's counts and rows could describe two moments under concurrent confirmations (now one snapshot, and `packages/database/src/snapshot.integration.test.ts` holds the snapshot's isolation, its refusal to write, and its tenant binding); a date in year 0000 answered 500 (now a 400 from the contract); and refusals carried no `Cache-Control` (now `no-store`).
- Integration runs, each on its own throwaway Neon branch:
  - while building: domain-booking 62 of 62, the existing API suites 42 of 42, the new suite 22 of 22, and the snapshot suite 3 of 3;
  - the full suite once, at `2e73d2b`, on a fresh branch migrated from empty and seeded twice with `SEED_DEMO_BOOKINGS=1`: 416 tests in 31 files passed in 43 minutes (database 43, catalog 25, payments 5, pricing 50, inventory 89, booking 62, API 142). The code review ran the same 416 on its own branch and got the same result. Later commits touch the console, the seed, and the new suite only;
  - after the code review's fixes, a fresh branch migrated and seeded twice (the first run about two minutes, the second skipping every guest in 14 s), then the new suite, with its two added cursor cases, 22 of 22;
  - the seed's recovery: on another fresh branch, a run stopped on purpose after the late payment's refund (a temporary local change, never committed), then a run with the final code booked only the missing charter and finished the other operator, and a third run changed nothing. Each operator ended with three bookings, one exception, and one refund, succeeded.
- Automated checks: `pnpm check` is green at the final commit. It ran 1541 unit tests across 14 packages: 433 console, 47 API (the new routes' guards and their OpenAPI paths), 18 contracts (the reference normalizer), 8 booking domain (masking and the views' helpers), and the rest unchanged. Biome checked 336 files, and typecheck, three dry-run deploys, doc links across 89 Markdown files, and 29 prototype tests passed. `pnpm contracts:generate` shows no drift.
  - The console's tests run in jsdom against a fake API, with the viewer in Tokyo and the marinas in New York and Honolulu, and fail on any request no route answers. A routine implementer wrote 343 of them. They found defects it marked as expected failures until each was fixed: quick find never finishing under React's development double run of effects, the Bookings link not returning to today after a day change, an operator switch keeping the trip filter, focus falling to the page in five places, an invalid day read on the viewer's clock, paging during a trip refresh, and answer fields formatted without a check.
  - One run under heavy local load, with the full integration suite and a typecheck running alongside, failed two timing-sensitive console tests. One was a real race, a heading taking focus from a button the test had just focused, which the focus rule below now prevents; neither failed again in 11 runs, 6 of them three at a time.
- Browser journeys in the built-in browser on a throwaway Neon branch (migrated, seeded twice with demo bookings), against `wrangler dev` with the fake provider on port 8887 and the production guest and console builds served with their `_headers` policies (two local changes: a `report-uri`, and the guest's `connect-src` gained the local API). Screenshots are in the session's scratch folder.
  - As a guest at 1280, a Demo Harbor cruise on Oct 7 booked as `5X0JG8DP`; at 375, a Demo Reef booking, `CF7JWTEY`. Each showed in its own operator's list and detail, and quick find opened `5X0JG8DP`.
  - Owner at 1280: the seeded Oct 10 charter and the guest's booking in the list; the charter's roster, printed to PDF alone, black on white, with its notice and footer.
  - Booking staff at 375: the Oct 10 list as cards, and the roster's table scrolling inside its region, the page never.
  - Finance at 1280 and 375: no booker column, no names or emails anywhere in the page or the API's answers, the guest card's explanation, the roster's designed refusal, and exceptions without who paid.
  - Demo Reef's owner: a Demo Harbor booking's address showed "This booking isn't here", and quick find refused a Harbor reference. A person who belongs to both operators switched between them; each list, booking, and exceptions page started afresh in its own operator's zone and role.
