### G2.5 Pricing, add-ons, fees, and policies verification, October 5, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.5-pricing-and-policies` from `a541eab`:
  - migration 0005: per-product price lists (ticket types or a charter price, mandatory fees, and paid add-ons with optional date ranges), per-product policy versions, tenant tax rates (inclusive or exclusive), promotion codes with versioned terms, and immutable quotes with their lines and per-line taxes;
  - `packages/domain-pricing`: the pure quote computation, commands that append versions, loaders, and the quote and offer services;
  - `GET /v1/public/trips/{tripId}/offer`, an idempotent `POST /v1/public/quotes`, and `GET /v1/public/quotes/{quoteId}`, each resolved by verified origin;
  - the publish check: a product needs a price list and a policy, in the service and in a trigger;
  - seeded terms for both demo operators. Demo Harbor adds a state and a county tax and offers `HARBOR10`. Demo Reef's prices include its tax, and it offers `REEF25`.
- Money rules, set out in the [pricing contract](../packages/domain-pricing/README.md):
  - integer cents, rounded half up, per line;
  - the discount comes off the trip price only and is split by the largest remainder;
  - tax is charged on discounted amounts, inclusive tax is extracted from the price, and no tax is charged on tax.
- The database holds the rules for every writer:
  - every 0005 table is append-only for every role, and child rows can be written only in their parent's transaction;
  - actors are stamped from the transaction, so a guest cannot write terms;
  - a price list must be complete at commit;
  - a quote must add up, and must be what its trip and named versions produce, or it does not commit.
- Checks on a fresh throwaway Neon branch:
  - migrate, then seed: 892 trips, 5 price lists with 24 items, 5 policies, 3 tax rates, 2 promotion codes, and 5 published products;
  - a second seed: nothing added;
  - integration: 40 database, 25 catalog, 45 pricing, and 90 API tests;
  - unit: 457 tests across 11 packages, 28 of them in the Workers runtime, plus 29 prototype tests.
- Workers runtime: on the same branch, `wrangler dev` served the seeded data through the runtime role.
  - Harbor's sunset cruise, for two adults, a child, and a photo with `HARBOR10`, came to $131.09: $127.00, less $11.50, plus a $7.50 harbor fee and $8.09 in state and county tax.
  - Reef's dive for two divers with `REEF25` came to $325.00, with $13.72 of excise tax included.
  - Each retry replayed the same quote with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, and each read matched.
  - A trip asked for from the other operator's origin answered 404.
- Seed backfill: a branch was set up like a database seeded before this goal, with published products and no terms. The seed added 5 price lists and 5 policies, and a rerun added nothing.
- Mutation checks, each reverted:
  - half-down rounding turned 5 of the lead's unit tests red;
  - taxing the amount before the discount turned 5 of them red;
  - with the two new quote checks neutered, all 9 inconsistent quote copies committed.
- An independent test specialist wrote 79 adversarial tests: 27 unit, 32 database, and 20 API. They found five defects, each red before its fix and green after:
  - a per-booking add-on limit was skipped when the party could not be counted;
  - names with C1 control characters passed validation but failed the insert;
  - a guest transaction could write terms by naming another actor;
  - pricing error responses could be cached;
  - a trip starting at a time with seconds could not be quoted.
- The specialist also probed what the database self-check cannot see. Current versions, active rates, and the quote instant come from the service and its clock, and the contract lists them. A bound of one hour from `quotedAt` to `expiresAt` was added, but `quotedAt` is the writer's, so the bound holds for honest quotes only. The review fixes below add what checkout can rely on: `created_at` stamped from the database clock.
- Independent review, October 5, 2026: accept with listed fixes, none blocking. Each fix has a test that failed before it and passes after; on a branch migrated with the unfixed 0005, each new test failed at its first forged case.
  - `POST /v1/public/quotes` is rate limited. An Origin header is not authentication, and each quote is about 20 round trips plus rows. A second Workers rate limit binding, `PUBLIC_RATE_LIMITER`, allows 30 a minute per client address and tenant, checked before the transaction opens. A limited request answers 429 `rate_limited` and writes nothing; reads are not limited. The API test's third quote was written before the fix and answers 429 after it.
  - Quote validity no longer rests on the writer's clock alone. The reviewer's copy of a real quote dated 2030, years after its trip, had committed; a quote dated at or after its departure is now refused. A trigger stamps `created_at` from the database clock whatever the writer sends, so checkout (G2.7) can require `now() < least(expires_at, created_at + interval '30 minutes')`.
  - The discount split is checked line by line. Forged copies that put the whole discount on either trip-price line had committed. The database now requires the exact largest-remainder split the service computes, so moving even one odd cent is refused.
  - A quote's party must be within its product's limits and its trip's seats. In review, a charter quote re-stored with 500 guests on a 6-seat boat had committed, as had one re-stored with fewer guests and a lower fee. The first is now refused. The second stays possible within the bounds, because the database cannot count a charter's guests; the contract says so, and G2.7 must re-check the party.
  - A discount line must carry its promotion's label exactly. A name with a control character had committed. `app.discount_label` gives the same label as the service for every percentage and for 217 fixed amounts.
  - The demo plan lists quotes under `domain-pricing` only. The contract now states the validity guarantee, what checkout must do, what the self-check cannot see, and the review's follow-ups.
- Checks after the fixes, on a fresh throwaway Neon branch:
  - migrate, then seed: 892 trips, 5 price lists with 24 items, 5 policies, 3 tax rates, 2 promotion codes, and 5 published products;
  - a second seed: nothing added;
  - integration: 40 database, 25 catalog, 50 pricing, and 91 API tests;
  - `pnpm check`: lint, typecheck, 457 unit tests across 11 packages (28 in the Workers runtime), 29 prototype tests, dry-run deploys of the three Workers (the API's lists `PUBLIC_RATE_LIMITER` at 30 requests per 60 seconds), and doc links;
  - `pnpm contracts:generate` added the 429 to the OpenAPI document, and a second run changed nothing.
- Not rerun after the fixes: the Workers-runtime smoke test under `wrangler dev`. The new limiter follows the sign-in limiter's pattern, which the G2.2 review confirmed under `wrangler dev`, and the API test drives it through a stand-in binding.
- Not done:
  - 0005 is not applied to the Neon main branch, and nothing is deployed;
  - no staff endpoints for terms or quotes;
  - tax rates are tenant-wide, not per location;
  - the review's follow-ups, listed in the [pricing contract](../packages/domain-pricing/README.md#follow-ups).

## 2026-09-30

### G2.14a Design system and brand bootstrap verification, September 30, 2026

- Built on branch `build/g2.14a-design-system-and-brand` from `a0807c6`, in parallel with G2.4. The brand contract in [packages/contracts/src/brand.ts](../packages/contracts/src/brand.ts) is the single source of truth for tenant brand input. Colors are six-digit hex under three contrast rules: primary 4.5:1 on the Foam page, 4.5:1 with its ink, and accent 3:1 on the primary. Fonts come from three OFL fonts per role. The logo is an SVG, PNG, or WebP of at most 32 KB, stored as a base64 data URI; SVG passes an element and attribute allowlist. Copy is plain text without angle brackets or control characters. Legal links are https or same-site paths, and locale and guest capabilities are enums. Unknown fields are refused everywhere.
- `packages/design-system` re-exports that contract and adds semantic tokens with a dark console theme, six self-hosted variable fonts (Sora, Fraunces, Bricolage Grotesque; Inter, Source Sans 3, Atkinson Hyperlegible Next), components with their states (button, text field, status badge, notice, empty state, skeleton, icons), the brand-to-CSS mapping, and a development gallery. `packages/tenant-config` validates the two synthetic manifests in [config/tenants](../config/tenants/README.md), Demo Harbor Charters and Demo Reef Divers, each with a hand-drawn logo mark.
- Migration 0004 (numbered 0003 until G2.4 landed first) adds append-only `brand_config_versions` and `brand_activations` under forced row-level security, with SELECT and INSERT only for the runtime, and `app.resolve_public_brand`, a pinned definer function that reuses `app.resolve_hostname`. `GET /v1/public/tenant` keeps its path, headers, and 404 body, adds the active brand after validating it again, and answers 503 when a stored brand fails the contract. The seed publishes each manifest's brand once and is idempotent; `SEED_LOCAL_HOSTNAMES=1` adds `<slug>.book.localhost` hosts for local rendering.
- Automated checks: `pnpm check` is green. It ran 165 unit tests across seven packages (27 in the Workers runtime), Biome on 137 files, typecheck, three dry-run deploys, doc links, and 29 prototype tests. On a fresh throwaway Neon branch, migrate and seed ran as CI runs them, then 40 database and 49 API integration tests passed. The 10 new API tests cover: each origin gets its own brand; interleaved requests never cross tenants; unknown, disabled, unverified, and suspended hostnames share one 404; a tenant without a brand gets identity only; a tampered stored brand is withheld with 503; a new version and a re-activation switch the brand while old versions stay unchanged; UPDATE, DELETE, and TRUNCATE fail for every role; row-level security holds per tenant; and the privilege matrix matches. The OpenAPI document regenerates without drift and a frozen install is clean. The API bundle contains no React, CSS, design-system, or tenant-config code.
- Mutation checks, each reverted: skipping the API's outbound validation turned the tampered-brand test red; dropping the tenant filter from the definer function turned four isolation tests red; granting the runtime UPDATE on brand versions turned two brand tests and the shared privilege-matrix test red; admitting `<script>` into the logo allowlist turned the manifest test red, and exposed that the design-system suite caught it only through a second rule, so a test now names the refused element or attribute for empty elements.
- Browser walkthrough in the built-in browser at 375 and 1280. The production builds were served with their `_headers` policies; the guest policy gained one `connect-src` entry for the local API. Both brands render distinctly from configuration. Loading, not found, not yet open, and failure states render neutral, and the last three move focus to their heading. On wide screens the brand band shows a large decorative mark. The console's signed-out, validation error, link sent, confirm, signed-in (one and two operators), empty calendar and bookings, and not-provisioned screens all render. A synthetic owner signed in through a local magic link and signed out. An in-page audit found no horizontal overflow, no target under 44 by 44, and no text under its contrast minimum. No text is below 14 px except 12 px uppercase eyebrows. Every tab stop shows a 3 px focus ring: info blue on light surfaces, Tide Lime on the console. This pass reported no policy violations, which was wrong for the guest page; the follow-up below corrects it. The only console errors are the browser's network lines for the API's 401 while signed out and for the deliberately blocked origin in the failure test.
- Defects found and fixed during the goal: zod ran the contrast refinement on malformed colors and threw instead of reporting; eyebrows rendered in the display font; the console inherited a tenant's fonts; guest pages downloaded Inter, a font no tenant chose; loading placeholders were invisible on the loading band; legal copy read "This terms is"; gate screens kept a signed-in page title; two pages sat off the header's left edge.
- Incident: the session scratch directory is shared with the root session. A helper read a `branch.json` that the G2.4 work had overwritten and set the `tidegrid_app` password twice on the G2.4 throwaway branch `br-lucky-dust-b451xpml` (about 08:38 and 08:46 UTC). Five G2.4 catalog API tests that failed with 500s from 08:38:53 to 08:38:57 UTC coincide with the first change; the next G2.4 run passed 48 of 48. No data was written there. G2.14a state now lives in its own subdirectory, and its own branches were used for every result above.
- Not done: the independent UI review on the shells; Cloudflare Access sign-in was not exercised locally (its code path is unchanged); deployed CORS does not yet list tenant preview hostnames, which G2.14b owns with hostname routing and the PWA; no brand publish endpoint or editing UI; no screen-reader or physical-device test.
- Follow-up the same day, after the independent code and UI reviews (both: accept with fixes). Corrected CSP result: the guest page violated its policy once on every load. zod 4 probes `new Function("")` when it builds an object schema, and the guest `script-src` blocks eval. The guest now turns zod's JIT off in a module imported before anything else, and a unit test fails if building or parsing the public contract compiles any code. To observe violations, the local verification server added a `report-uri` to the policies it served; neither `_headers` file has one. A control build without that import sent one report (`script-src`, blocked URI `eval`); the fixed guest and console builds sent none across every state below.
- Code review fixes: the SVG allowlist no longer strips comments before reading tags. It refuses comments, DOCTYPE, CDATA, processing instructions, a `<`, `>`, or backslash in an attribute value, any parenthesis other than a local `url(#id)` in any letter case, `<use>` and `<symbol>`, `xlink:` without its declared namespace, and an XML declaration that is not leading or names an encoding other than UTF-8. Brand copy refuses control, format, and separator characters and lone surrogates. The public tenant identity is a strict object with a slug pattern and a checked name. The API withholds with 503 a stored brand that carries its own `version` key and an identity that fails the contract. The guest shows "no booking site" only for the API's `tenant_not_found`. Its bootstrap no longer needs `AbortSignal.any` or `AbortSignal.timeout` (Safari 16 to 17.3), and any loader failure becomes the failure screen. The isolation test now checks tenant B after tenant A switches versions, so dropping the definer function's tenant filter turns it red. The seed imports `@tidegrid/tenant-config` as a declared dependency, the unused `PublicTenantResponse` is gone, and the database README says any brand publish must take the seed's per-tenant advisory lock. A new check caught one more defect: the OpenAPI generator wrote the new copy pattern as `...$/u`, which matches nothing. The contract now publishes the bare pattern, and a test compiles every pattern in the document.
- UI review fixes: focus moves to the new screen's heading after "Continue signing in", sign-out, the console's "Try again", a successful guest "Try again", and every page change, including Calendar to Bookings, because pages are keyed on the path. Sign out sits in the rail's user block at every width, shows "Signing out…", and lands on sign-in with a "You're signed out" notice. The console failure screen has a heading and plain copy with no status codes. Below 64rem the rail keeps its own height on short pages. The reading measure is in em, calibrated per body font from measured character widths (Inter 30em, Source Sans 3 26.25em, Atkinson Hyperlegible Next 28em, about 65 characters), and the gallery counts the characters on each rendered line. The reduced-motion rule uses `!important` instead of a zero-weight `:where()`. The no-access lock is info blue. "Selected" is text beside the Active badge. "Not open yet" no longer tells guests to contact the operator. Both sites serve favicons, and the guest shows the operator's mark once its brand applies.
- Proof: `pnpm check` is green with 236 unit tests across nine packages (28 in the Workers runtime), Biome on 149 files, three dry-run deploys, doc links, and 29 prototype tests. New tests were run against the previous code and failed there: 28 of 81 brand validation tests, all 7 guest bootstrap tests, 5 of 7 guest app tests, 13 of the first 14 console tests (the 14th checks that a page load leaves focus alone) and both later navigation tests, 4 design system token and theme tests, the zod no-eval test, and the pattern test. On a fresh throwaway Neon branch, migrate and seed ran, then 40 database and 51 API integration tests passed. A first attempt on another branch stalled for about 10 minutes on one adversarial staff API request and then failed three tests; nothing in those suites changed, and the rerun passed. In the browser at 375 and 1280, the longest guest line is 61 characters (the review measured up to 93), the phone rail stays 220 px on a short page, focus lands on the expected heading after every transition above, and `/favicon.ico` answers 200 on both sites.
- Follow-ups recorded, not fixed here. G2.11 (guest): the loading skeleton does not match the loaded layout; a failed retry gives no new feedback; the empty-state card is now much wider than its text; the guest alignment and taste notes from the UI review. G2.12 (console): a failed retry gives no new feedback; after "Check your email" the send button stays primary; the navigation's current-page mark disappears in forced-colors mode; the single-operator box reads as an input; the console alignment and taste notes. Content security policy: the guest's `payment=()` will block wallet payments in G2.11; the Cloudflare Insights origins in both policies are unused; the console's `style-src` still allows `'unsafe-inline'`. From the recheck of `fc3a364`: G2.12 should make Sign out reachable earlier in the tab order below 1024 px, where it is drawn top-right but is the last tab stop, and should clear the "Sign-out didn't finish" alert on navigation. The goal that adds tenant creation must apply the brand copy rule to `tenants.display_name`: a name the table accepts but the public contract refuses makes the guest bootstrap answer 503. The published copy pattern uses `\p{...}` classes, so its consumers need a Unicode-aware regex engine, as JSON Schema 2020-12 specifies; the contract notes this.
- Second follow-up, after both reviewers rechecked `fc3a364` (UI passed; code accepted with one fix). The SVG transform check backtracked catastrophically: between transforms, `\s*,?\s*` let two quantifiers split one run of spaces, so a failing value under the 200-character cap took 0.67 s with single spaces, 60 s with double spaces, and 58 s with triple spaces in Node. The boundary is now `\s*(?:,\s*)?`, and the same values fail in 0.2 ms. A test fails if such a value takes 50 ms or more, and a second test times hostile input at each other contract pattern's size limit; none had the same shape. The invalid-link error on the sign-in screen had become silent when its heading took focus; the heading now names the notice as its description, which a jsdom test checks for both the error and "You're signed out".

### G2.4 Catalog and schedule verification, September 30, 2026

- Built:
  - migration 0003: locations, boats, products and the boats each may use, seasonal schedules, scheduled trips, and blackouts;
  - `packages/domain-catalog`: a local-time resolver, recurrence expansion, trip generation, sales states, product publishing, and the availability query;
  - public availability by verified origin;
  - staff catalog, calendar, generation, sales-state, and publish routes;
  - a seeded 2026-27 season for both demo operators, built through the services.
- Time follows ADR 0016.
  - Trips store their zone, local date and time, UTC instants, and offsets, and database triggers refuse any row where they disagree.
  - A departure in a spring-forward gap is skipped. One in a fall-back overlap needs an explicit earlier or later choice.
  - A trip's end is its start plus elapsed minutes. The booking cutoff is an exact instant on either side of a clock change.
- Checks on a fresh throwaway Neon branch:
  - migrate, then seed: 892 trips, with 5 departures skipped on blackout days;
  - a second seed: 0 new trips;
  - integration: 40 database, 23 catalog, and 49 API tests (final commit);
  - unit: 181 tests across six packages, 24 of them in the Workers runtime, plus 29 prototype tests.
- A routine implementer wrote recurrence expansion and its 96 tests against the accepted contract. Its mutation run caught 72 of 73 deliberate bugs; the one survivor cannot change output.
- Independent review (fresh agent) accepted with fixes and found no blocking defect. Applied:
  - **Zone data disagreed.** The runtime's zone data and PostgreSQL's disagreed about Vancouver, Edmonton, Casablanca, and El Aaiun, which returned an opaque 500. Generation now checks every departure with PostgreSQL and skips a disagreeing one as `zone_data_mismatch`. Blackout creation raises a named error. A drift test compares both sources for every accepted zone.
  - **Buffers.** The turnaround buffer was stored but never applied. An exclusion constraint now keeps one departure per boat, counting the buffer. Schedule creation refuses departures too close together, generation skips an overlap as `boat_conflict`, and a lost race returns a retryable 409.
  - **Tests.** Added a cutoff that spans a clock change, the earlier and later overlap choices stored through the trigger, product and boat blackouts, the product filter, and a retired boat or archived location.
  - **Smaller fixes.**
    - The contract's generation range now says 92 days.
    - Publish and sales-state races report the real current state.
    - The sales-state trigger covers inserts, and a location or product blackout must use its location's zone.
    - `Etc/GMT` zones are refused, and a no-op generation leaves no audit row.
    - Date edges return 400, not 500, and the README states what the database enforces and what only the service checks.
- The reviewer rechecked `32620ae` and confirmed every finding fixed or documented, on its own branch: 40 database, 22 catalog, and 48 API integration tests. It accepted with small follow-ups, all applied:
  - Zone names are now case-proof, and locations and blackouts must use the exact spelling PostgreSQL lists.
  - The one-departure-per-boat rule is recorded as a demo narrowing in the demo build plan; the owner confirmed it on 2026-10-01.
  - An API test holds a conflicting trip uncommitted until the request blocks on it, and proves the retryable 409.
  - The README now says disagreeing zone data can also surface as gap or overlap skips.
- Flaky runs:
  - The reviewer's five 500s came from the parallel G2.14a agent. Through a shared scratch file it rotated the runtime password on the reviewer's branch. Each agent now gets its own scratch folder.
  - Connect stalls, and one silent hang in integration setup, hit the coordinator's own branches. The cause is not known. Setup now bounds every step and waits until both roles answer, and unhandled errors log their SQLSTATE.
- Not done: migration 0003 is not applied to the Neon main branch, and nothing is deployed. Known gaps are listed in the [catalog README](../packages/domain-catalog/README.md): no re-check of stored trips after a zone data update, statuses with no runtime write path until the console goal, and routing the cancellation of a trip with bookings through remedies in G2.6 and G2.7.
